Radharani is the Queen of Vrnda’s forest. She is the Supreme Goddess of the entire creation. Radha enjoys playing fun games with Krsna, Srimati Radharani is the final transcendental goal. She is naturally situated far, far beyond anyone’s meditation. Radha is very meditative by nature. She is always playing mischievous pranks on Krsna.

# The Glories and Pastimes of Srimati Radharani. Part II

## Dedication

I humbly offer ‘The Glories and Pastimes of Srimati Radharani' Part II unto the lotus hands of my Guru Maharaja, His Holiness Jayapataka Swami Maharaja, that he might hand it over to his spiritual master, His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Srila Prabhupada, our most beloved Founder-Acarya of the International Society for Krsna Consciousness.

His father, Gour Mohan De, would ask any visiting Vaisnava to bless his son Abhaya to be a good devotee of Srimati Radharani. Srila Prabhupada was thus blessed by Srimati Radharani to preach Her glories all over the world.

## Preface

Finally after long waiting Srimati Radharani glories and pastimes Part two is going to manifest. In my book Srimati Radharani part one I had mentioned that I will be publishing Srimati Radharani Part two. Many years have passed since then. Time to time devotees have been asking me when this book will be published. I tell them only when Srimati Radharani will bless then this book will manifest.

The articles in this part two will be little different than the articles in part one. There will be some articles describing small pastimes of Srimati Radharani and some long articles describing pastimes from Lalita Madhava, Prema samputa, Ananda vrindavan champu and Gopal champu etc. In these books our great Acharyas have described different pastimes of the Radha and Krishna in very interesting way. In this book I have described those in very simpler form in the way I have understood. I hope the articles in this book will help readers to have an overall idea of the subject matter. Then they may read the original books written by respective writers. It is not so easy to understand the original writings. But if you will read those carefully by the mercy of Radharani you will enjoy the subject matter very much.

The article “Unalloyed Loving relation between Radharani and Krishna'' describes how Their relationship is beyond material conception of love. Many people are misled by hearing the loving relationship between Radha and Krishna due to their ignorance of truth behind these two personalities and nature of Their loving relationship. I thought it is necessary to remove the doubts from the mind of people in general in regards to loving affairs between Radha and Krishna on the basis of scriptures and verdict of great Acharayas. I hope this will help readers of this book understanding how the love between Radha and Krishan is pure and bonafied.

The article named as “Nava Vrindavan pastime” has been prepared on the basis of the book Lalita Madhava written By Srila Rupa Goswami. This article deals with some portion of subject matter of the book but not as systematically as it has been described in the book. This Lalita madhava book is written by Srila Rupa Goswami in drama format. I have transformed it to a story form. The sequence of description in the article is not same as the sequence it has been described in the original book. I have changed the sequence to make the subject matter understandable more easily. Through this article Srila Rupa Goswami has presented how Sri krishan and Srimati Radha are enjoying in Dwaraka in a different mode than Vrindavan pastime. It has been described that after Sri krishan moves to Dwaraka, due course of time 16108 including Srimati Radharani, Chandravali, Lalita, Visahka and four principal sakhis of Chandravali have been transferred to Dwaraka. In Dwaraka they have been known in different other names. Radharani appeared as Satyabhama and Chandravali appeared as Rukmini. In Vrindavan Radharani had married to Abhimanue and she was going to meet Krishna secretly. In Dwaraka Krishna is married and going to meet Radharani, known as Satyabhama, secretly. In this way Krishna was relishing the opposite rasa to what he was enjoying in Vrindavan.

In this book you will find many descriptions of small pastimes of Srimati Radharani with Sri Krishna.

I am not so elevated to write on the subject matter of Srimati Radharani. But still I have prepared this second part of “Glories and pastimes of Srimati Rdaharani” book. When I was reading all these books I enjoyed it so much. I thought devotees of Radharani will also enjoy to read all these pastimes. All these descriptions in this book is as per my understanding of the original texts written by goswamis. One may go through the original texts to understand the subject matter more better.

I pray at the lotus feet of Srimati Radharani to forgive for any mistakes I have committed in describing her pastimes in this book.

Your servant  
Bhakti Purusottama Swami

## Section 1 - The Transcendental Loving Relationship between Radha and Krsna

The pure and transcendental loving relationship between Krsna has been misunderstood by many. It is crucial for people to know the truth behind the loving affairs between Srimati Radharani and Sri Krsna so that they will be protected from committing offences and thus be saved from degrading themselves.

Let us try to understand the loving affairs between Krsna and Radha from a proper perspective. Due to their ignorance about this subject matter, people in general comment incorrectly about its nature. You should not try to understand Their loving affairs from a material point of view.

As Radha and Krsna are spiritual and transcendental, we should try to understand Their pure loving relationship from the transcendental point of view. Following are some points described based upon scriptural references to understand the subject matter in a more perfect way.

Lord Sri Krsna has said in the Bhagavad-gita:

*avajananti mam mudha*  
*manusim tanum asritam*  
*param bhavam ajananto*  
*mama bhuta-mahesvaram*

**“Fools deride Me when I descend in the human form. They do not know My transcendental nature as the Supreme Lord of all that be.”**

The foolish people do not know or understand the Lord's transcendental nature. When the Supreme Personality of Godhead descends from His spiritual abode to exhibit His transcendental pastimes for the benefit of the populace in general, due to not understanding properly, some unfortunate people do not appreciate this. The reason why the Lord descends to this material world is to attract the conditioned living entities back to spiritual world. When people will hear the sweet pastimes of the Lord their heart will be cleansed and they will be free from material desires and gel attracted to God and go back to Godhead.

In Srimad-Bhagavaram ([10.33.36](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/33.36)) it states:

*anugrahaya bhaktanam*  
*manusam deham asthitah*  
*bhajate tadrsih krida*  
*yah srutva tat-paro bhavet*

**"Lord Krsna descends apparently as a human being, and He exhibits His transcendental pastimes in Vrndavana so that the conditioned soul may be attracted to hearing His transcendental activities.”**

The Lord exhibits many uncommon activities and at the same time He also performs some common activities that ordinary people do. Just like Lord Ramacandra was acting like a bewildered person when Sita Devi was kidnapped by Ravana, Krsna stole butter, became frightened to see a stick in the hand of His mother and so on. The Vedic scriptures inform us that the Lord has unlimited good qualities and no bad qualities—especially there are eighteen bad qualities not supposed to be found in God:

“The scriptures have identified eighteen human shortcomings that go against its tenets. They are as follows: illusion, sleepiness, uncivility, lust, greed, madness, envy, cruelty, lamentations, over-endeavour, deceit, anger, desire, fear, mistakes, intolerance, and dependence. The scriptures proclaim that the Lord’s qualities are absolutely free from these discrepancies. Occasionally, krpa-sakti arranges the display of some of these discrepancies in the dealings of Krsna, Rama, and so on, and the devotees then perceive them."(Mudhurya-Kadambini)

But sometimes these qualities are found in the activities of God. How to reconcile this? Our acaryas have explained that these qualities are exhibited sometimes within God during His human pastimes to please His devotees. In the Bhagavad-gita, Krsna Himself has said:

*ye yatha mam prapadyante*  
*tams tathaiva bhajamy aham*

**“As all surrender unto Me, I reward them accordingly."**

As the living entity surrenders accordingly. Krsna has to reciprocate with them. If a devotee accepts and serves the Lord as their son. for instance, then the Lord reciprocates according to a parental loving service mood.

If devotee’s love is so strong that they serve the Lord as their son, then the Lord also has to reciprocate by exhibiting the mood of an innocent and helpless child. This gives pleasure to the devotees who are serving the Lord in a parental loving relationship. Thus Lord has to sometimes exhibit so-called common bad qualities such as anger, desire, fear, mistakes, intolerance, and dependence etc. When these so-called ‘bad qualities' are found in God in order to give pleasure to His devotees it is not regarded as a fault—rather it shows His greatness. This happens clue to His bhakta-vatsalyata nature. But people in general do not understand this logic and they develop a wrong impression about God's activities.

Thus we should understand that the apparent contradictory activities of the Lord do not reduce His greatness—rather they increase His greatness. These are not dusana or mistakes on God’s part, but rather these are bhusana or ornaments. This way our great acaryas have reconciled the so-called contradictory in the activities of the Lord and His devotees.

When people in general hear of the loving relationship between Krsna, Srimati Radharani and the other gopis, it really bewilders them. They may hear of the pure and unalloyed loving pastimes of Krsna with Radharani and the gopis but they may not be able to relate to these properly. Rather, they may develop doubts and think these pastimes to be ordinary material activities or even as degrading activities. Thus they commit offenses at the feet of the God.

What to speak people of other religions like Islam and Christianity, even some unfortunate Hindus may also carry doubts within their heart about such activities of Sri Krsna, especially His pastimes with Vraja gopikas, many unmarried girls and many married ladies in Vrndavana.

God has many uncommon superhuman activities that surprise and attract people, yet He also has some ‘human-like’ activities that could develop doubts in ordinary people, making them wonder whether to accept Him as God or not.

At the same time Lord performs some activities that are not regarded as suitable activities for even a materially cultured person. These activities are regarded as most abominable among high-class people. These apparently ‘degrading’ activities of the Lord can also make people reject Him as God because they cannot reconcile how the Supreme Personality of Godhead can do things that are regarded as unciviliced and degraded from social point of views.

Sometimes people of other faiths take advantage of these kinds of activities of God and preach that Krsna is not God or they dare to blaspheme Him by find ing serious mistake within the Lord. Thus people in general are creating offenses either knowingly or unknowingly.

Srila Prabhupada in his purport in Caitanya-caritamrta (Adi-lila [4.56](https://vedarama.com/en/books/cc-adi/4.56)) mentioned that Radha-Krsna are very puzzling: “The two transcendentalists Radha and Krsna are a puzzle to materialists.”

And in Srimad-Bhagavatam ([1.8.18](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-1/8.18)) it is stated: “The Lord is, so to speak, a puzzle for the common man.”

We can never truly understand Radha-Krsna in our conditioned stale of mind. But still by the description of the scripture and explanation given by the great acaryas we can understand them and their loving relationship to some extent, which will help us to develop faith in them.

## It is not possible to understand the activities of God through our material senses

*aprakrta vastu nahe prakrta-gocara*  
*veda-puranete ei kahe nirantara*

**“Spiritual substance is never within the jurisdiction of the material conception. This is always the verdict of the Vedas and the Puranas.” Caitanya-caritamrta, (Madhya-lila** [**9.194**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/cc-madhya/9.194)**)**

Transcendental subject matter cannot be understood through the material senses. But what does transcendental or aprakrta mean? Transcendental means being above the modes of material nature.

All the activities of the living entities in this material world are performed by the influence of the three modes of material nature—goodness, passion, and ignorance. But there is no influence of three modes of material nature upon the activities of God.

God is known as ‘nirguna,’ free from all material qualities. But when we living entities, who are under the three modes of material nature try to understand God. who is beyond the modes of material nature, through our limited knowledge we become confused. Our senses and intelligence are not sufficient or qualified to understand the transcendental activities of the Lord. That is why Krsna has said in the Bagavad-gita ([7.25](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/7.25)):

*naham prakasah sarvasya*  
*yoga-maya-samavrtah*  
*mudho yam nabhijanati*  
*loko mam ajam avyayam*

**"I am never manifest to the foolish and unintelligent. For them I am covered by My internal potency, and therefore they do not know that I am unborn and infallible."**

We should not try to understand God or measure God according to our perception or measuring scale.

I will give one example to understand this.

There was an old lady. Throughout her whole life this lady always used one particular jug to measure things like rice and water to use for her cooking. One day she went to buy cloth and she carried that jug with her. When the cloth merchant was measuring the material by a tape measure, the lady objected. She suggested that he should measure the cloth using the jug she had been measuring things with her whole life. But how can you measure cloth by a jug? The way to measure cloth is obviously different to how you measure products used in cooking. In the same way we cannot measure God with conventional ways as we measure things in this material world.

## Who can understand God?

*isvarera krpa-lesa haya ta’ yahare*  
*sei ta’ isvara-tattva janibare pare*

**“If one receives bui a tiny bit of the Lord's favor by dint of devotional service, he can understand the nature of the Supreme Personality of Godhead." Caitanya-caritamrta, (Madhya-lila** [**6.83**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/cc-madhya/6.83)**)**

As Srila Prabhupada has said, “We can see the sun only through its sun rays when the sun rises. Otherwise we cannot sec the sun by our endeavor of using a powerful torch in the middle of the night.”

As only by the help of sunlight can you see the sun. in the same way only by the mercy of God can you understand Him. People become confused because they try to understand God through their materially developed fertile brain. A fertile brain helps you to understand material subject matters, but to understand spiritual subject matters it is insufficient because transcendental subject matter does not manifest in the mundane mind.

*divya jnana hrde prokasito*

**“Transcendental knowledge manifest in the heart.”**

Divya jnana does not manifest in the brain. In Srimad-Bhagavatam ([10.14.29](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/14.29)), Brahma has said:

*athapi te deva padambuja-dvaya-*  
*prascida-lesdnugrhlia eva hi*  
*janati tattvam bhagavan-mahimno*  
*na canya eko ’pi ciram vicinvan*

**“My Lord, if one is favored by even a slight trace of the mercy of Your lotus feet, he can understand the greatness of Your personalily. But those who speculate to understand the Supreme Personality of Godhead are unable to know You, even though they continue to study the Vedas for many years’’**

By the mercy of God we can understand the truths behind God—not by achieving a doctorate. Thus we should first know that a person has to be qualified to understand this most difficult subject matter. You have to be pure enough and receive the proper mercy to understand transcendental topics.

## The Transcendental Nature of Krsna’s Activities

There are two kinds of consideration: one is called apata vicara.' or general superficial consideration, and the other is called tattva vicara.’ to know things in truth.

If you see or hear something and consider it from an external point of view, it will have one kind of meaning. But when you consider something not just from an external perception, but by understanding the meaning in truth by delving deeper into the subject matter, then there will be other deeper meanings. Krsna’s activities has to be understood in truth.

Krsna says in Bhagavad-gita ([4.9](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/4.9)):

*janma karma ca me divyam*  
*evam yo vetti tattvatah*  
*tyaktva deham punar janma*  
*naiti mam eti so 'rjuna*

**“One who knows the transcendental nature of My appearance and activities docs not, upon leaving the body, take his birth again in this material world, but attains My eternal abode, O Arjuna."**

Krsna's activities are transcendental to the three modes of material nature.

Until now we discussed the basis on which a person should understand God's activities.

The following points will eradicate doubts about God's activities. These points are based on the scriptures and the verdicts of the great acaryas. Some people may develop doubts when they read the scriptures about some of God’s activities. But when the same scripture describes facts that reconcile the apparent contradictions, yet they are not ready to accept them. This is called ‘ardha kukuti nyaya—half-hen justice.' This means that they will take the portion of the scripture that helps them justify their opinions, but they do not take the whole teachings into consideration.

## The Love between Radha and Krsna

This subject was discussed by Srila Sukadeva Gosvami. The loving relationship between Radharani and Sri Krsna can never be placed in the same category as a mundane loving relationship. We can be convinced of this by knowing the person who has spoken about this topic.

Srila Sukadeva Gosvami has described the sweet loving pastimes between Lord Krsna and Vraja Gopika in Srimad-Bhagavatam, the crest jewel of all the Puranas, the ripened, sweet, juicy fruit of the Vedas and the essence of Vedanta. Sukadeva was a liberated soul and a life-long brahmacari, and therefore would not talk about some mundane loving relationship. Moreover, this topic of Srimad-Bhagavatam was discussed among great saints from all over the universe at a place called Sukatala.

If Their loving relationship was indeed a mundane so-called love, then this subject could not be discussed at such an esteemed gathering of exulted personalities. Therefore, first we should accept the exulted nature of these loving affairs between the gopis and Krsna without thinking that they are like mundane affairs between ordinary men and women.

In this way, at first we need to be respectful to the subject matter, understanding that it was discussed by very high-class saintly personalities. When we have this kind of conviction, then we can discuss the topic.

## Prema and Kama

In order to understand the elevated position of the loving relationship between Krsna and the gopis, first we should know what is love.

Prema (love) and Kama (lust) are two different topics. We may use the word love' in this material world so we can express our intimate likings for each other. But in reality this word love’ is not meant to be used to express the relationship among materialistically inclined people. Love is specially meant to be used only in the case of the unalloyed loving relationship between the living entities and God.

The definition of love is state as:

*krsnendriya-priti-iccha dhare ‘prema’ nama*

**"..the desire to please the senses of Lord Krsna is prema \[love\].'' Caitanya-cantamrta (Adi-lila** [**4.165**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/cc-adi/4.165)**)**

The desire of giving satisfaction to Krsna’s senses is called love. This is the natural, pure and unalloyed relationship between Krsna and the spirit soul. In this material world there is no love, but rather the love has manifested in its perverted form as lust. The apparent loving relationship among the living entities in this material world is not love but lust (because it is based on satisfying a persons own senses\]. You may say “I love you so much." But in reality you should say “I am lusting after you so much." This is because we ‘love’ each otheronly to ultimately gel something for ourselves. We love each other for our sense gratification. Pure love does not mean I love Krsna for my sense gratification but rather for the pleasure of Krsna's senses.

Because we are only familiar with lustful affairs, when we hear about the pure loving affairs between Krsna and His eternal consorts we also take them as lustful affairs. But in reality they are not of that nature. The lust arises among the living entities in this material world from the mode of passion.

As Krsna says in the Bhagavad-gita ([3.37](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/3.37)):

*kama esa krodha esa*  
*rajo-guna-samudbhavah*  
*mahasano maha-papma*  
*viddhy enam iha vairinam*

**“The Supreme Personality of Godhead said: It is lust only, Arjuna, which is born of contact with the material mode of passion and later transformed into wrath, and which is the all-devouring sinful enemy of this world."**

Loving affairs between the gopis and Krsna did not take place due to the influence of material nature's mode of passion. Krsna is beyond the influence of the modes of material nature. He docs not perform His activities by the influence of material nature’s mode of passion.

In Caitanya-caritamrta (Adi-lila [4.162-172](https://vedarama.com/en/books/cc-adi/4.162)) Srila Krsnadasa Kaviraja Gosvami, eloquently describes love and lust:

*gopi-ganera premera 'rudha-bhava’ nama*  
*visuddha nirmala prema, kabhu nahe kama*

**“The love of the gopis is called rudha-bhava. It is pure and spotless. It is not at any time lust.”**

Srila Prabhupada describes: "As already explained, the position of the gopis in their loving dealings with Krsna is transcendental. Their emotion is called rudha-bhava. Although it is apparently like mundane sex, one should not confuse it with mundane sexual love, for it is pure and unadulterated love of Godhead.”

*premaiva gopa-ramanam*  
*kama ity agamat pratham*  
*ity uddhavadayo py etam*  
*vanchanti bhagavat-priyah*  
*(Bhakti-rasamrta-sindhu* [*1.2.285*](https://vedarama.com/en/books/rupa-brs1/1.2.285)*)*

**“The pure love of the gopis has become celebrated by the name lust. The dear devotees of the Lord, headed by Sri Uddhava, desire to taste that love.”**

*kama, prema,—donhakara vibhinna laksana*  
*lauha arahema yaiche svarupe vilaksana*

**“Lust and love have different characteristics, just as iron and gold have different natures.”**

One should try to discriminate between sexual love and pure love, for they belong to different categories, with a gulf of difference between them. They are as different from one another as iron is from gold.

*atmendriya-priti-vancha—tare bali ‘kama'*  
*krsnendriya-prlti-iccha dhare ‘prema’ nama*

**“The desire to gratify one's own senses is kama \[lust\], but the desire to please the senses of Lord Krsna is prema \[love\].”**

Srila Prabhupada describes in the Purport to this verse:

“The revealed scriptures describe pure love as follows

*sarvatha dhvamsa-rahitam saty api dhvamsa-karane*  
*yad bhava-bandhanam yunoh sa prema parikirtitah*

**“If there is ample reason for the dissolution of a conjugal relationship and yet such a dissolution does not take place, such a relationship of intimate love is called pure.”**

The predominated gopis were bound to Krsna in such pure love. For them there was no question of sexual love based on sense gratification. Their only engagement in life was to see Krsna happy in all respects, regardless of their own personal interests. They dedicated their souls only for the satisfaction of the Personality of Godhead. Sri Krsna. There was not the slightest tinge of sexual love between the gopis and Krsna.

The author of Sri Caitanya-caritamrta asserts with authority that sexual love is a matter of personal sense enjoyment. All the regulative principles in the Vedas pertaining to desires for popularity, fatherhood, wealth and so on are different phases of sense gratification. Acts of sense gratification may be performed under the cover of public welfare, nationalism, religion, altruism, ethical codes. Biblical codes, health directives, fruitive action, bashfulness, tolerance, personal comfort, liberation from material bondage, progress, family affection or fear of social ostracism or legal punishment, but all these categories are different subdivisions of one substance—sense gratification. All such good acts are performed basically for one’s own sense gratification, for no one can sacrifice his personal interest while discharging these much-advertised moral and religious principles. But above all this is a transcendental stage in which one feels himself to be only an eternal servitor of Krsna, the absolute Personality of Godhead. All acts performed in this sense of servitude are called pure love of God because they are performed for the absolute sense gratification of Sri Krsna. However, any act performed for the purpose of enjoying its fruits or results is an act of sense gratification. Such actions are visible sometimes in gross and sometimes in subtle forms."

*kamera tatparya—nija-sambhoga kevala*  
*krsna-sukha-tatparya-matra prema ta prabala*

**“The object of lust is only the enjoyment of one's own senses. But love caters to the enjoyment of Lord Krsna, and thus it is very powerful."**

*loka-dharma, veda-dharma, deha-dharma, karma*  
*lajja, dhairya, deha-sukha, atma-sukha-marma*  
*dustyaja arya-patha, nija parijana*  
*sva-jane karaye yata tadana-bhartsana*  
*satya-tyaga kari’ kare krsnera bhajana*  
*krsna-sukha-hetu kare prema-sevana*

**“Social customs, scriptural injunctions, bodily demands, fruitive action, shyness, patience, bodily pleasures, self-gratification and the path of varnasrama-dharma, which is difficult to give up—the gopis have forsaken all these, along with their families, and suffered their relatives’ punishment and scolding, all for the sake of serving Lord Krsna. They render loving service to Him for the sake of His enjoyment."**

*ihake kahiye krsne drdha anuraga*  
*svaccha dhauta-vastre yaiche nahi kona daga*

**That is called firm attachment to Lord Krsna. It is spotlessly pure, like a clean cloth that has no stain.**

Srila Prabhupada describes in the Purport to this verse:

“The author of Sri Caitanya-caritamrta advises everyone to give up all engagements of sense gratification and. like the gopis, dovetail oneself entirely with the will of the Supreme Lord. That is the ultimate instruction of Krsna in the Bhagavad-gita. We should be prepared to do anything and everything to please the Lord, even at the risk of violating the Vedic principles or ethical laws. That is the standard of love of Godhead. Such activities in pure love of Godhead are as spotless as white linen that has been completely washed. Srila Bhaktivinoda Thakura warns us in this connection that we should not mistakenly think that the idea of giving up everything implies the renunciation of duties necessary in relation to the body and mind. Even such duties are not sense gratification if they are undertaken in a spirit of service to Krsna."

*ataeva kama-preme bahula antara*  
*kama—andha-tamah, prema—nirmala bhaskara*

**“Therefore lust and love are quite different. Lust is like dense darkness, hut love is like the bright sun."**

*ataeva gopi-ganera nahi kama-gandha*  
*krsna-sukha lagi matra, krsna sc sambandha*

**“Thus there is not the slightest taint of lust in the gopis' love. Their relationship with Krsna is only for the sake of His enjoyment."**

## The Material world: A perverted reflection of the spiritual world

Bhagavad-gita ([15.1](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/15.1)) Krsna stales:

*sri-bhagavan uvaca*  
*urdhva-mulam adhah-sakham*  
*asvattham prahur avyayam*  
*chandamsi yasya parnani*  
*yas tam veda sa veda-vit*

**"The Supreme Personality of Godhead said: It is said that there is an imperishable banyan tree that has its roots upward and its branches down and whose leave are the Vedic hymns. One who knows this tree is the knower of the Vedas.”**

Srila Prabhupada explains in his purport:

"... Now, there is no ready experience in this world of a tree situated with its branches down and its roots upward, but there is such a thing. That tree can be found beside a reservoir of water. We can see that the trees on the bank reflect upon the water with their branches down and roots up. In other words, the tree of this material world is only a reflection of the real tree of the spiritual world. This reflection of the spiritual world is situated on desire, just as a tree’s reflection is situated on water ....

This tree, being the reflection of the real tree, is an exact replica. Everything is there in the spiritual world. The impersonalists take Brahman to be the root of this material tree, and from the root, according to Sankhya philosophy, come prakrti, purusa, then the three gunas, then the five gross elements (pan-ca-maha-bhuta). then the ten senses (dasendriya), mind, etc. In this way they divide up the whole material world into twenty-four elements. If Brahman is the center of all manifestations, then this material world is a manifestation of the center by 180 degrees, and the other 180 degrees constitute the spiritual world. The material world is the perverted reflection, so the spiritual world must have the same variegatedness, but in reality. The prakrti is the external energy of the Supreme Lord, and the purusa is the Supreme Lord Himself, and that is explained in Bhagavad-gita. Since this manifestation is material, it is temporary. A reflection is temporary, for it is sometimes seen and sometimes not seen. But the origin from whence the reflection is reflected is eternal. The material reflection of the real tree has to be cut off...”

Thus we know this material world is the perverted reflection of the spiritual world. The Lord has given an example of a banyan tree on the bank of a lake. There we sec the branches of the tree are downward. In the spiritual world the real tree is there. This material world is an exact reflection of the spiritual world—that means that whatever is happening in this material world must be there in the spiritual world. Otherwise if something is not there in the spiritual world, then how this can be here in the material world?

This means that all the activities that are occurring in this material world are also present there in the spiritual world. But the difference is that all activities in the spiritual world are related to God—that’s why it is bona fide and real, not maya or illusory.

But all activities in the material world are not bona fide as these activities are not related to Krsna and thus not real, but maya. But in any case activities look the same. Krsna steals and dances with other girls. There are also people in this material world who have a tendency for stealing and consorting with many ladies. When it happens in Goloka with Krsna, that is real and glorified. But when the same thing happens here in the material world, it is regarded as crim inal. This is because the material world is a perverted reflection of the spiritual world—whatever is highest and most elevated in the spiritual world, when it appears in its perverted reflection in the material world, it is to be considered the lowest and most degraded. Krsna s activities are pure on the spiritual platform. Thus there is no defect in the pastimes of Krsna with the gopis.

Srila Prabhupada describes in Caitanya-caritamrta (Madhya-lila [13.24](https://vedarama.com/en/books/cc-madhya/13.24)) purport what Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati Thakura said about this matter:

“In this connection, Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati Thakura comments that as an ideal husband, Lord Jagannatha remained for fifteen days in a secluded place with His wife, the supreme goddess of fortune. Nonetheless, the Lord wanted to come out of seclusion to give happiness to His devotees. The Lord enjoys Himself in two ways, known as svakiya and parakiya. The Lord's conjugal love in the svakiya-rasa relates to the regulative principles observed in Dvaraka, where the Lord has many married queens. But in Vrndavana the conjugal love of the Lord is not with His married wives but with His girlfriends, the gopis. Conjugal love with the gopis is called parakiya-rasa. Lord Jagannatha leaves the secluded place where He enjoys the company of the supreme goddess of fortune in svakiya-rasa, and He goes to Vrndavana, where He enjoys the parakiya-rasa. Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati Thakura therefore reminds us that the Lord's pleasure in parakiya-rasa is superior to His pleasure in svakiya-rasa."

In the material world, parakiya-rasa, or loving affairs with unmarried girl-friends. is a most degraded relationship, but in the spiritual world this type of loving affair is considered the supreme enjoyment. In the material world everything is but a reflection of the spiritual world, and that reflection is perverted. We cannot understand the affairs of the spiritual world on the basis of our experience in the material world.

“The Lord's pastimes with the gopis are therefore misunderstood by mundane scholars and word-wranglers. The parakiya-rasa of the spiritual world should not be discussed except by one who is very advanced in pure devotional service. I he parakiya-rasa in the spiritual world and that in the material world are not comparable. The former is like gold, and the latter is like iron. Because the difference between the two is so great, they cannot actually be compared. However, just as a knowledgeable person can easily distinguish gold from iron, one who has the proper realization can easily distinguish the transcendental activities of the spiritual world from material activities.”

In Bhaktirasamrta-sindhu ([1.2.28](https://vedarama.com/en/books/rupa-brs1/1.2.28)5), Srila Rupa Gosvami states:

*premaiva gopa - ramanam*  
*kama ity agamat pratham*  
*ity uddhavadayo ’py etam*  
*vanchanti bhagavat-priyah*

**"Although the dealings of the gopis with Krsna are on the platform of pure love of Godhead, such dealings are sometimes considered to be lusty. But because they are completely spiritual. Uddhava and all the other dearmost devotees of the Lord desire to participate in them."**

## Krsna’s loving affairs with all living entities

Krsna can have loving affairs with each and every living entity because He is the Supreme Enjoyer. That is bona fide and has no fault, because all living entities are part and parcel of Krsna. When Krsna develops loving affairs with any living entity there is nothing wrong, but if any boy develops loving affairs with a girl that is not authorised as this girl is not part and parcel of the boy. As the living entity is part and parcel of Krsna, when the living entity develops love for Krsna they do not try to enjoy Krsna. Rather when Krsna enjoys, the living entity feels happiness.

In the material world, people seek what they think is happiness. In the spiritual world, love is to give happiness, whereas in the material world, love is to find happiness for yourself.

Devotees want to give pleasure to Krsna. When they see Krsna is happy, they feel happy. In this way when Krsna develops loving affairs with any living entity, this is bona fide and there is no fault with this. This brings us to the point of Krsna's loving relationship with Radharani (and the other gopis), who is His Svarupa sakti—the pleasure-giving potency. Gopis relationship with Krsna was not for sense gratification but just to give pleasure to Krsna.

The following description in Caitanya-caritamrta [8.214-218](https://vedarama.com/en/books/cc-madhya/8.214) clarifies this point:

*anyonye visuddha preme bare rasa pusta*  
*tan-sabara prema dekhi’ krsna haya tusta*

**“The transcendental mellow is nourished by that mutual behavior in transcendental love of Godhead. When Lord Krsna sees how the gopis have developed pure love for Him, He becomes very satisfied.”**

By seeing the satisfaction of Krsna, the gopis feel happy.

Srila Prabhupada writes in the purport to the above verse :

“Srimati Radharani and the gopis are not interested in their personal happiness derived from association with Krsna. Rather, they become happy by seeing one another associate with Krsna. In this way their dealings are further nourished by love of Godhead, and seeing this, Krsna is very pleased.

*sahaja gopira prema,—nahe prakrta kama*  
*kama-krida-samye tara kahi ‘kama’-nama*

**“It is to be noted that the natural characteristic of the gopis is to love the Supreme Lord. Their lusty desire is not to be compared to material lust. Nonetheless, because their desire sometimes appears to resemble material lust, their transcendental love for Krsna is sometimes described as lust.”**

Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati Thakura says that material lust should never be attributed to Krsna, who is full of transcendental knowledge. Material lust cannot be engaged in the service of the Lord, for it is applicable to materialists, not to Krsna. Only prema, or love of Godhead, is applicable for the satisfaction of Krsna. Prema is full service rendered unto the Lord. The lusty affairs of the gopis actually constitute the topmost love of Godhead because the gopis never act for their own personal satisfaction. They are simply pleased by engaging other gopis in the sendee of the Lord. The gopis derive more transcendental pleasure from indirectly engaging other gopis in the sendee of Krsna than from engaging in His service themselves. That is the difference between material lust and love of Godhead. Lust applies to the material world, and love of Godhead applies only to Krsna."

*nijendriya-sukha-hetu kamera tatparya*  
*krsna-sukha-tatparya gopi-bhava-varya*

**“Lusty desires are experienced when one is concerned with his own personal sense gratification. The mood of the gopis is not like that. Their only desire is to satisfy the senses of Krsna.”**

*nijendriya-sukha-vancha nahi gopikara*  
*krsne sukha dite hare sangama-vihara*

**“Among the gopis, there is not a pinch of desire for sense gratification. Their only desire is to give pleasure to Krsna, and this is why they mingle with Him and enjoy with Him."**

The loving relationship between Radha and Krsna is not mundane love as we sec between boys and girls of the material world. Relationship in this material world is lust, but the spiritual relationship with Krsna is love. In Caitanya-Caritamrrta, Krsna Dasa Kaviraja Gosvami has described the great difference between mundane lust and spiritual love. Love is like gold and lust is like iron.

## Vraja-gopikas are Non-Different from Krsna

Vraja gopikas and Rad haram are not ordinary ladies; they are eternal consorts of Lord Sri Krsna in the spiritual world. When He comes to this material world, He descends along with His abode and associates.

*sri krsna caitanya prabhu jive dayakari*  
*svaparsada sviya dhama saha avatari*

**“When Lord descends to this material world He comes here along with His abode and associates"**

All associates of Krsna are not ordinary living entities who are the marginal potency of the Lord. Rather, all of them are svarup sakti or the internal potency of the Lord. The internal potency means that potency of Krsna that is within Himself. It is within His svarup-sakti and is non-different from Him. Radharani is the personification of the hladini-sakti or the pleasure-giving potency of the Lord.

This pleasure-giving potency is there within Krsna's body in an unmanifested form. But when Krsna desires to enjoy, Radha manifests from Lord’s transcendental body and takes a form visible outside. The Hladini potency is an energy of Krsna that is there within the body of Krsna. When that hladini potency takes form, it transforms into Radharani.

*hladinim sara ‘’prema’, prema-sara ‘bhava*  
*bhavera parama-kastha, nama—‘maha-bhava’*

**The essence of the hladini potency is love of God, the essence of love of God is emotion \[bhava\], and the ultimate development of emotion is mahabhava.**

*mahabhava-svarupa sri-radha-thakurani*  
*sarva-guna-khani krsna-kanta-siromani*

**Sri Radha Thakurani is the embodiment of mahabhava. She is the repository of all good qualities and the crest jewel among all the lovely consorts of Lord Krsna.**

In purport to this verse Srila Prabhupada describes:

“The unadulterated action of the hladini-sakti is displayed in the dealings of the damsels of Vraja and Srimati Rad haram, who is the topmost participant in that transcendental group. The essence of the hladini-sakti is love of Godhead, the essence of love of Godhead is bhava, or transcendental sentiment, and the highest pitch of that bhava is called mahabhava. Srimati Radharani is the personified embodiment of these three aspects of transcendental consciousness. She is therefore the highest principle in love of Godhead and is the supreme lovable object of Sri Krsna."

## Radharani Appeared from Krsna's Body

According to the scriptures, Radha and Krsna are non-different. Radha is the pleasure-giving internal potency of Krsna. Originally She appeared from Krsna in the spiritual world to give pleasure to the Lord. In Caitanya-caritamrta (Adi-lila [4.55-56](https://vedarama.com/en/books/cc-adi/4.55)) this mystery has been revealed.

*radha krsna-pranaya-vikrtir hladini saktir asmad*  
*ekatmanay api bhuvi pura deha-bhedam gatau tau*

**“The loving affairs of Sri Radha and Krsna are transcendental manifestations of the Lord’s internal pleasure-giving potency. Although Radha and Krsna are one in Their identity, They separated Themselves eternally ...”**

*radha-krsna eka atma, dui deha dhari’*  
*anyonye vilase rasa asvadana kari’*

**Radha and Krsna are one and the same, hut They have assumed two bodies. Thus They enjoy each other, tasting the mellows of love.**

Srila Prabhupada describes:

“The two transcendentalists Radha and Krsna are a puzzle to materialists. The above description of Radha and Krsna from the diary of Srila Svarupa Damodara Gosvami is a condensed explanation, but one needs great spiritual insight to understand the mystery of these two personalities. One is enjoying in two. Sri Krsna is the potent factor, and Srimati Radharani is the internal potency. According to Vedanta philosophy, there is no difference between the potent and the potency; they are identical. We cannot differentiate between one and the other, any more than we can separate fire from heat.

Everything in the Absolute is inconceivable in relative existence. Therefore in relative cognizance it is very difficult to assimilate this truth of the oneness between the potent and the potency. The philosophy of inconceivable oneness and difference propounded by Lord Caitanya is the only source of understanding for such intricacies of transcendence.

In fact. Radharani is the internal potency of Sri Krsna, and She eternally intensifies the pleasure of Sri Krsna. Impersonalists cannot understand this without the help of a maha-bhagavata devotee. The very name ‘Radha' suggests that Srimati Radharani is eternally the topmost mistress of the comforts of Sri Krsna. As such, She is the medium transmitting the living entities' service to Sri Krsna ..."

Caitanya-caritamrta (Adi-lila [4.59-60](https://vedarama.com/en/books/cc-adi/4.59)):

*radhika hayena krsnera pranaya-vikara*  
*svarupa-sakti—‘hladini’ nama yanhara*

**Srimati Radhika is the transformation of Krsna’s love. She is His internal energy called hladini.**

*hladini karaya krsne anandasvadana*  
*hladinira dvara kare bhaktera posana*

**That hladini energy gives Krsna pleasure and nourishes His devotees.**

In the purport, Srila Prabhupada describes:

"The potency of the Supreme Personality of Godhead that always enriches Him with transcendental bliss is not material, but the Sahkarites have accepted it as such because they are ignorant of the identity of the Supreme Lord and His pleasure potency. Those ignorant persons cannot understand the distinction between impersonal spiritual bliss and the variegated ness of the spiritual pleasure potency. The hladini potency gives the Lord all transcendental pleasure, and the Lord bestows such a potency upon His pure devotee."

Regarding the appearance of Radharani in the spiritual world, the Purana describes as follows:

"In Goloka Vrndavana there is a place called the rasa mandala. On one side of the rasa-mandala there is a mountain called Satasrnga. This mountain appeared in Bhuloka as Govardhana. In Vrndavana there are nice gardens of malati and mallika flowers. The Lord of the universe, by whose mere desire everything happens, was silting in that garden on a beautiful ratna-simhasana. He had the desire to enjoy, and as soon as this desire came to His mind, immediately from His left side appeared a very beautiful devi. She was decorated with valuable jewels and wore cloth purified by fire. She was dazzling like millions of moons, Her body the color of molten gold. She was illuminating everything with Her radiance. When She smiled, her teeth were very attractive. Her face defeated the beauty of the lotus flower in autumn. She was wearing a flower garland of malati and a diamond necklace. Her bodily effulgence was brilliant like the sun in summer season. She was sitting next to Krsna on the ratna-simhasana.

Thus the Lord appeared in two forms. From the left side of His body appeared Srimati Radharani, and right side of His body appeared Sri Krsna. When Lord Sri Krsna saw Radhika, He wanted to enjoy with Her. Knowing this, Her form became even more beautiful. Immediately She ran to pick flowers to offer to the lotus feet of Sri Hari.

Because She appeared in the rasa-mandala and immediately ran to serve the Lord by picking flowers. She is known as "Radha;" "ra" represents the rasa-mandala, and “dha” represents dhavamana. running.

Because She appeared in the rasa-mandala and was running (dhavamana) to the Lord when She saw Him very enthusiastic to enjoy (ramana), Her name is Radha."

Regarding the appearance of two forms from one original form of the Lord, it is stated in Narada-pancaratra (2.3.21),

*dvibhujah so ’pi goloke*  
*babhrama rasa-mandale*  
*gopa-vesas ca taruno*  
*jaladah syamasundarah*  
*eka isah prathamato*  
*dvidha-rupo babhuva sah*  
*eka stri visnu-mayaya*  
*puman ekah svayam vibhuh*  
*sa ca svecchamayah syamah*  
*saguno nirgunah svayam*  
*tam drstva sundarim*  
*lolam ratim kartum samudyata*

**The two-handed form of Parabrahman in the form of a young cowherd boy whose bodily color was like new clouds, was wandering in rasa-mandala in Goloka-dhama. Only one form of Isvara divided into two forms: one part appeared as a female form known as Yisnu-maya, or the internal potency of Krsna; the other part remained as Himself, vibhu-purusa, or the male form. He is independent and acts according to His own will. Everything happens as soon as He desires. He has a blue bodily effulgence. He is saguna (having transcendental qualities) and nirguna (without material qualities).**

There is no difference between Radha and Krsna. Krsna serves Radha and Radha serves Sri Krsna. Krsna is energetic and Radha is energy.

*radha—purna-sakti, krsna—purna-saktiman*  
*dui vastu bheda nat, sastra-paramana*

By this it has been explained that Radha and Krsna are one in truth. Radha is constitutionally the energy of Krsna. She is His svarupa-sakti or natural energy, just as heat is the natural energy of fire. One cannot separate heat from fire. In the same way one cannot separate Radha from Krsna. Energy and the energetic together is one object. There is no difference between Them. In the same way Sri Sri Radha-Krsna is one and non-different truth.

Parabrahman Krsna is rasa-svarupa, made of rasa, and He desires to enjoy rasa also. Sruti says sa kamayate. He desires.

But ekaki sa na ramate: He cannot enjoy alone. To enjoy through the exchange of mellows there has to be more than one. That is why, even if He is one, He has manifested in many forms from since lime immemorial.

Radha-Krsna is originally one. Even though one and non-different, still that one Para brahman has manifested in two forms which have themselves manifested in many forms. Krsna has manifested in an unlimited number of bhagavat-svarupas and Radha has manifested in an unlimited number of kanta-sakti-rupas.

In Narada-pancaratra it has been mentioned that.

*yatha brahma-svarupas ca*  
*sri-krsnah prakrteh parah*  
*tatha brahma-svarupa sa*  
*nirlipta prakrteh para*

**As Sri Krsna is brahma-svarupa and beyond prakrti, in the same way Radha is also brahma-svarupini and beyond prakrti.**

In regard to the non-difference between Radha and Krsna, the Padma Purana explains:

*devi krsnamayi prokta*  
*radhika para-devata*  
*sarva-laksmi-svarupa*  
*sa krsnahlada-svarupini*  
*tatha sa procyate vipra*  
*ahladiiniti manisibhih*  
*tat-kala-kotya-kotyamsa*  
*durgadyas trigunatmikah*  
*sa tu saksat mahalaksmi*  
*krsno narayano prabhuh*  
*naitayor vidyate bhedah*  
*svalpo 'pi muni-sattamah*  
*krsnake ahlade, ta'te nama—’hladini’*  
*sei sakti-dvare sukha asvade apani*

**“The potency called hladini gives Krsna transcendental pleasure. Through this pleasure potency, Krsna personally tastes all spiritual pleasure. (Caitanya-caritamrta, Madhya-lila** [**8.157**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/cc-madhya/8.157)**)**

In Padma Purana it has also been mentioned that Radha has told to Narada Muni:

*aham ca lalita devi*  
*radhika ya ca giyate*  
*aham ca vasudevakhyo*  
*nityam kama-kalatmakah*  
*satyam yosit-svarupaham*  
*yosic caham sanatani*  
*aham ca lalitd devi*  
*pum-rupah krsna-vigrahah*  
*avayor antaram nasti*  
*satyam satyam hi narada*

**One who is called as Radhika. I am same also known as Lalita. I am also the eternal kama-kalatmaka Vasudeva. I am really ramani (lady) Svarupa. I am sanatani ramani. eternally lady. I am Lalita and I am also Krsna in the male form. Oh, Narada! I am telling you truth that there is no difference between me and Krsna.**

In Brhat Gautamiya Tantra it has been explained (Quoted in Sri Krsna-sandarbha, Anuccheda 189):

*Krsna told Baladeva,*  
*sattvam tattvam paratvam ca*  
*tattva-trayam aham kila*  
*tri-tattva-rupini sapi*  
*radhika mama vallabha*  
*prakrteh para evaham*  
*sapi mac-chakti-rupini*  
*sattvikam rupam asthaya*  
*purno ’ham brahma cit parah*  
*brahmana prarthitah samyak*  
*sambhavami yuge yuge*

**“I am the Svarupa of three tattvas, namely sattva, tattva and paratva (cause, effect and beyond the cause and effect). My dear Radha is also tri-tattva-rupini. I am beyond prakrti. My energy Radha is also beyond prakrti. I am purna brahma situated in visuddha-sattva, transcendental to three modes of material nature. Being prayed to by Brahma in order to kill the demons who are enemies of Indra, I descend to this world millennium after millennium along with You and Radha.”**

Our love, which is known as lust, arises from the mode of passion. In the Bhagavad-gita ([3.37](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/3.37)) it states:

*kama esa krodha esa*  
*rajo-guna-samudbhavah*

**“The Supreme Personality of Godhead said: It is lust only, Arjuna, which is born of contact with the material mode of passion and later transformed into wrath ...”**

But Krsna’s love does not arises from the mode of passion. Relishing mellows, which arise from unalloyed love, is the constitutional position of Krsna.

Sruti says: *Raso vai sah* (Taittiriya Upanisad [2.7.1](https://vedarama.com/en/books/taittiriya/2.7.1)).

He is made of mellow or rasa. He relishes rasa and He creates living entities to relish rasas through loving relationships.

Thus we should not equate the love affairs of the material world with loving affairs between the gopis and Krsna.

There are five kinds of rasas or relationships that living entities can develop with Krsna and establish a relationship with Krsna. These five kinds of relationships are also seen here in this material world, but are a perverted reflection of adi rasa or the original relationship with Krsna.

The five kinds of relationships are santa rasa or neutral relationship; dasya rasa or servitorship, sakhya rasa or friendship or fraternal relationship; vatsalya rasa or parental relationship; madhurya rasa or conjugal relationship.

Originally each and every living entity is related to Krsna within any one of these five rasas. But as we have rejected our relationships with Krsna, we are trying to enjoy by developing temporary relationships with other living entities in this material world through one of these five rasas.

We develop our relationship of santa or dasya. sakhya or vatsalya or conjugal relationship. Similar relationships in this material world are a perverted relationship of the spiritual relationship with Krsna.

A conjugal relationship with Krsna is real and pure, whereas as conjugal relationship seen between a male and female of this material world is unreal and a perverted reflection of real relationships with Krsna. That's why a conjugal relationship between ordinary individuals is contaminated in the material world, whereas it is very pure in relationship to Krsna. Thus we should not consider the relationship between the gopis with Krsna as impure, material and contaminated like a so-called loving relationship between a male and female of this material world.

## Parakiya Relationships

In Goloka Vrndavana, Rad haram and the gopis are eternally serving Krsna as His eternal consorts. There Krsna knows that the gopis are His consorts and the gopis also know that they are consorts of Krsna. In this awareness they serve Krsna and enjoy with Him by giving Him pleasure.

When there is an obstacle and hindrance in your loving relationship with a loved one and you have to overcome all these hindrances, at that lime love be comes more powerful and intense. That gives more pleasure.

When a wife is serving her husband, knowing that he is her husband, that time everything is favorable for them to meet and there is no hindrance to the meeting. But when someone meets their lover when that lover is not their legal wife or husband, then there are so many hindrances.

Such a person has to overcome all these hindrances by disobeying normal codes of conduct that intensify the love. This love is known as ‘parakiya’ The loving relationship between wife and husband is known as ‘svakiva.’ In Goloka Vrindavan. Rad haram and all the gopis are eternally serving Krsna in svakiya rasa, being fully aware that they are consorts of Krsna. But in Bhauma lila (pastimes in the material world), yogamaya arranges that Krsna and Rad haram along with the gopikas do not understand Their eternal relationship. Yogamaya makes them forget and arranges false marriages with other gopas. Thus Radharani and the gopis think that they are married to someone other than Krsna, but their love for Krsna is so great that they will overcome all obstacles and run to Krsna. They will give up all kinds of dharmas like loka-dharma, veda-dharma. deha-dharma etc.... This kind of love is regarded as the highest love in all loving relationships.

The sentiment of the gopis regarding their loving relationship with Krsna as their paramour takes place due to the influence of Yogamaya, the internal potency of Krsna. In this regard Caitanya-caritamrta (Adi-lila [4.29](https://vedarama.com/en/books/cc-adi/4.29)) stales Sri Krsna as saying:

*mo-visaye gopi-ganera upapati-bhave*  
*yoga-maya karibeka apana-prabhave*

**“The influence of Yogamaya will inspire the gopis with the sentiment that I am their paramour.”**

Srila Prabhupada describes:

“Yogamaya is the name of the internal potency that makes the Lord forget Himself and become an object of love for His pure devotee in different transcendental mellows. This yogamaya potency creates a spiritual sentiment in the minds of the damsels of Vraja by which they think of Lord Krsna as their paramour. This sentiment is never to be compared to mundane illicit sexual love. It has nothing to do with sexual psychology, although the pure love of such devotees seems to be sexual. One should know for certain that nothing can exist in this cosmic manifestation that has no real counterpart in the spiritual field. All material manifestations are emanations of the Transcendence. The erotic principles of amorous love reflected in mixed material values are perverted reflections of the reality of spirit, but one cannot understand the reality unless one is sufficiently educated in the spiritual science."

*amiha na jani taha, na jane gopi-gana*  
*dunhara rupa-gune dunhara nitya hare mana*  
*(Caitanya-Caritamrta Adi-lila* [*4.30*](https://vedarama.com/en/books/cc-adi/4.30)*)*

**“Neither the gopis nor I shall notice this, for our minds will always be entranced by one another's beauty and qualities.”**

Srila Prabhupada elaborates:

“In the spiritual sky the Vaikuntha planets are predominated by Narayana. His devotees have the same features He does, and the exchange of devotion there is on the platform of reverence. But above all these Vaikuntha planets is Goloka, or Krsnaloka, where the original Personality of Godhead, Krsna, fully manifests His pleasure potency in free loving affairs. Since the devotees in the material world know almost nothing about these affairs, the Lord desires to show these affairs to them.

In Goloka Vrndavana there is an exchange of love known as parakiya-rasa. It is something like the attraction of a married woman for a man other than her husband. In the material world this sort of relationship is most abominable because it is a perverted reflection of the parakiya-rasa in the spiritual world, where it is the highest kind of loving affair. Such feelings between the devotee and the Lord are presented by the influence of yogamaya. The Bhagavad-gita states that devotees of the highest grade are under the care of daiva-maya, or yogamaya: mahatmanas tu mam partha daivim prakrtim asritah (Bg. [9.13](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/9.13)).

Those who are actually great souls (mahatmas) are fully absorbed in Krsna consciousness, always engaged in the service of the Lord. They are under the care of daivi-prakrti, or yogamaya. Yogamaya creates a situation in which the devotee is prepared to transgress all regulative principles simply to love Krsna. A devotee naturally does not like to transgress the laws of reverence for the Supreme Personality of Godhead, but by the influence of yogamaya he is prepared to do anything to love the Supreme Lord better.

Those under the spell of the material energy cannot at all appreciate the activities of yogamaya, for a conditioned soul can hardly understand the pure reciprocation between the Lord and His devotee. But by executing devotional service under the regulative principles, one can become very highly elevated and then begin to appreciate the dealings of pure love under the management of yogamaya.

In the spiritual loving sentiment induced by the yogamaya potency, both Lord Sri Krsna and the damsels of Vraja forget themselves in spiritual rapture.

By the influence of such forgetfulness, the attractive beauty of the gopis plays a prominent part in the transcendental satisfaction of the Lord, who has nothing to do with mundane sex. Because spiritual love of Godhead is above everything mundane, the gopis superficially seem to transgress the codes of mundane morality. This perpetually puzzles mundane moralists. Therefore yogamaya acts to cover the Lord and His pastimes from the eyes of mundaners. as confirmed in the Bhagavad-gita ([7.25](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/7.25)), where the Lord says that He reserves the right of not being exposed to everyone.

The acts of yogamaya make it possible for the Lord and the gopis, in loving ecstasy, to sometimes meet and sometimes separate. These transcendental loving affairs of the Lord are unimaginable to empiricists involved in the impersonal feature of the Absolute Truth. Therefore the Lord Himself appears before the mundaners to bestow upon them the highest form of spiritual realization and also personally relish its essence. The Lord is so merciful that He Himself descends to take the fallen souls back home to the kingdom of Godhead, where the erotic principles of Godhead are eternally relished in their real form, distinct from the perverted sexual love so much adored and indulged in by the fallen souls in their diseased condition. The reason the Lord displays the is essentially to induce all the fallen souls to give up their diseased morality and religiosity, and to attract them to the kingdom of God to enjoy the reality. A person who actually understands what the rasa-lila is will certainly hale to indulge in mundane sex life. For the realized soul, hearing the Lord's rasa-lila through the proper channel will result in complete abstinence from material sexual pleasure."

As described earlier, there are four kinds of reciprocation of loving service between Krsna and His devotees—dasya, sakhya. valsalva and madhurya—madhurya is considered the fullest. This madhurya rasa is the conjugal relationship, which is divided into two varieties: namely svakiya and parakiya. Svakiya is the relationship with Krsna as a formally married husband, and parakiya is the relationship with Krsna as a paramour.

Caitanya-caritamrta (Adi-lila [4.46-49](https://vedarama.com/en/books/cc-adi/4.46)) states:

*ataeva madhura rasa kahi tara nama*  
*svakiya-parakiya-bhave dvi-vidha samsthana*

**“Therefore I call it madhura-rasa. It has two further divisions, namely wedded and unwedded love."**

*parakiya-bhave ati rasera ullasa*  
*vraja vina ihara anyatra nahi vasa*

**“There is a great increase of mellow in the unwedded conjugal mood. Such love is found nowhere but in Vraja."**

*vraja-vadhu-ganera ei bhava niravadhi*  
*tara madhye sri-radhaya bhavera avadhi*

**“This mood is unbounded in the damsels of Vraja, but among them it finds its perfection in Sri Radha."**

*praudha nirmala-bhava prema sarvottama*  
*krsnera madhurya-rasa-asvada-karana*

**“Her pure, mature love surpasses that of all others. Her love is the cause of Lord Krsna’s tasting the sweetness of the conjugal relationship."**

Srila Prabhupada in his purport (Caitanya-caritamrta, Adi-lila [4.50](https://vedarama.com/en/books/cc-adi/4.50)):

“Of the four kinds of reciprocation of loving service—dasya, sakhya, valsalva and madhurya—madhurya is considered the fullest. But the conjugal relationship is further divided into two varieties, namely svakiya and parakiya. Svakiya is the relationship with Krsna as a formally married husband, and parakiya is the relationship with Krsna as a paramour. Expert analysts have decided that the transcendental ecstasy of the parakiya mellow is better because it is more enthusiastic. This phase of conjugal love is found in those who have surrendered to the Lord in intense love, knowing well that such illicit love with a paramour is not morally approved in society. The risks involved in such love of Godhead make this emotion superior to the relationship in which such risk is not involved. The validity of such risk, however, is possible only in the transcendental realm. Svakiya and parakiya conjugal love of Godhead have no existence in the material world, and parakiya is not exhibited anywhere in Vaikuntha, but only in the portion of Goloka Vrndavana known as Vraja ...”

## Srimati Radharani in separation from Krsna

The following narration describes how Srimati Radharani felt when Sri Krsna left Vrndavana for Mathura, including the many symptoms of love in separation She endured in Krsna’s absence. Srila Rupa Gosvami has described Radha s predicament very nicely in his play Lalita-madhava.

With Krsna and Balarama on the chariot about to depart with Akrura, Visakha and Radha s other close friends were hesitant - Would they tell Radha about Krsna's departure for Mathura or not? How could Radha tolerate being separated from Krsna? Even the fraction of a moment Krsna was absent from Radha s vision due to the blinking of Her eyes was unbearable for Her; indeed, She was known to glorify the good fortune of the fish, who have eyes that never blink.

When Radha heard the tumultuous sound of the gopis' crying, She took off running like a frightened doe. Agitated by transcendental madness, She spoke incoherently. Lalita and Visakha tried to console their friend, but what could they say to pacify Her? One moment She cried bitterly, the next moment She rolled on the ground in front of Akrura's chariot, and the next moment She gazed at Krsna's face with tear-filled eyes. Under normal circumstances She would never look directly at Krsna, even in the confidential association of Her gopi friends. But now She was openly staring at Her beloved, and in the presence of Her superiors! With a blade of grass between Her teeth, Radha begged Krsnas brother, Balarama, not to leave Vrndavana. But still, Balarama and Krsna shortly left on Akrura's chariot, though Krsna assured the gopis that He would come back soon.

Due to Krsna s absence from Vrndavana, the bumblebees stopped drinking honey, the peacocks no longer decorated Vrndavana's forests with their lively dances, and the male cakravaka birds refused the company of their wives. In separation from Sri Krsna, Srimati Radharani went mad. Her mind was so disturbed by the pain of separation that She ran wildly about or stood still like a painted picture. She laughed uncontrollably or wept piteously. She was at times very talkative, while at other limes She was completely silent. She would search for Krsna, asking anyone - and anything - that crossed Her path, “My dear friend, where is My Krsna, the rising full moon of Maharaja Nanda’s oceanic dynasty? Where is Krsna, whose head is decorated with a peacock feather? Where is He? Where is that Krsna, the consummate flute player? Where is that blue jewel? Where is the most expert of all dancers? Oh! Please tell Me where to find Krsna. Who can save My life by telling Me where to find Krsna? O Vidhata, controller of the universe, I condemn you for separating Me from Krsna!”

Visakha tried to pacify Radha by reminding Her about Krsna’s promise to return. In response, Radha said, “You gopis may be convinced that Krsna is compassionate and that He will come back. But the pain of being separated from Krsna, which is My greatest enemy, is cutting My heart to pieces. This enemy of Mine will never allow Me to live happily.’’

In great agony. Radharani said. “The blazing fire of separation from Krsna has burnt My heart to ashes. That fire of separation is more deadly than a strong dose of poison, more intolerable than the thunderbolt of Indra, more sharp than a spear driven directly into the heart, and more horrifying than the final stage of cholera.”

Seeing a cakravaka bird. Radharani said. "O My friend! You have come from the east. Surely you have been in Mathura. You must have some news about Krsna. Please tell Me how He is doing. He must be tired from His journey on the chariot. Has anyone taken care of Him? Has anyone tried to relieve His fatigue? Has anyone decorated Him with leaves, fresh sprouts, and other forest ornaments?”

Next, looking at a crow perched atop a kadamba tree, Radha said, “O brother! Please go to Krsna and offer respectful obeisances on our behalf. Give Him this message: ‘O moon of all amorous heroes, the flames of separation from You are now beginning to burn the animal of My life breath in the house of My body. Please unbolt the firm lock of hope that keeps that animal trapped inside the burning building."

Radharani then saw a female parrot to Her left and said, ”O parrot friend! You are a messenger from Krsna. Please tell Me: What is the news about Him? Has He broken the sharp thorns of His enemies? Is He now surrounded by friends? Are they now preparing a chariot for His return to Vrndavana?”

All of a sudden, feeling very frightened, Radharani started to cry and said, “Now Krsna has left. Where can I hear the nectarean sound of His flute? I cannot feel peace, even for a moment. There are no words that can free Me from My grief. Alas! Where has the lord of My life gone? I cannot see Krsna anymore! Therefore, what is the purpose of My life?"

In an attempt to pacify Sri Radha. Lalita said. "O lotus eyed Radha! Krsna can never leave us. He must be playing a joke on us. Just search for Him in the forests of Vrndavana. You will definitely find Him there.”

“Yes, you are correct,” Radharani said. “It is not possible for Krsna to leave us. Let us search for Him in the forest."

So Radharani searched for Sri Krsna. She saw a small group of does and with tears in Her eyes said to them, “Hello! It looks like you must have seen Krsna. I can see half-eaten grass is falling from your mouths. It must be because charming Krsna has stolen your hearts. Or maybe you have heard the bewildering sound of His flute.”

After a short walk Radha came across some peahens. She laughed loudly and said, ’’O peahens! Please tell me: Where is My Krsna, who is decorated with a peacock feather? Where is He hiding in this forest?”

With Lalita and Visakha by Her side, Radharani's wanderings eventually brought Her to Sakhi-sthali. That is the place of Candravali and her girlfriends, where Radha s rival also was at that moment suffering from intense grief, so much so that she was standing completely still, unable to move. Sri Radha, with tears in Her eyes and a choked voice, said, “O My friend, Candravali! Have you seen your friend Krsna, the independent-minded prince of Vraja?” In reply, Radha could hear only the echoes of what She had said, vibrating in the caves of nearby Govardhana Hill. In Her confused state, Radha wondered why Govardhana was now asking Her the same question She had just asked Candravali. Completely bewildered, She approached the nearest cave and said, “I can see you look very auspicious. Krsna must have embraced you very lightly many times. Please embrace Me with your arms, which have been marked by the fragrance of Krsna's flower carrings.” Then, seeing Her own reflection in one of the stones in the cave, Radharani tried to embrace Herself.

Unsuccessful, Radha turned away from the cave, looked off into the distance, and said. ’’O Lalita! Look! Look! I have seen Krsna. There in the far distance He stands decorated with a peacock feather and embraced by beautiful gopis.” But after looking more carefully She found that it was not Krsna after all. It was a mirage, created by a raincloud, lightning, and a rainbow on top of Govardhana Hill.

Then Radharani fainted.

When She regained consciousness, with great respect She approached Giri Govardhana and prayed. "O king of mountains! You are a very affectionate servant of Krsna. You give shelter to Krsna with your lap. Please open one of your caves and show Krsna to Me." She did not hear any reply from Govardhana. With folded hands She again prayed to the great devotee of Sri Krsna, “O Govardhana. You are so tall. From your great height you can easily see distant places. Please look around in all directions and tell Me where My Krsna is.”

Radharani walked a short distance away from Govardhana Hill and, seeing a kadamba tree, She said, “O Friend! Are you not the same kadamba tree under which I sat while Krsna placed a peacock feather crown on My head?”

Looking back toward Govardhana, Radha saw the entrance to a cave and began crying. While Her tears fell She said, "Whenever I see this place I remember how here Krsna made a bed for Me out of pure white jasmine flowers.”

Try as She might. Radha could not find Krsna anywhere. In great distress She scoured Vrndavana forest and the area all around Govardhana Hill, but She could not find a trace of Krsna. She could not even pick up a him of His aroma.

At one point She saw a hill, covered in golden flowers, with a tamala tree on top. In Her excited state She mistook the hill for Krsna and Akrura riding away on a chariot and exclaimed, "I will grab Krsna and drag Him down from the chariot.” Then she fainted. Visakha told Lalita to fan Radharani with the edge of her sari. Meanwhile, Visakha went off to find some lotus petals. Radharani’s suffering was so intense that Manasi-ganga dried up and Govardhana Hill noticeably shrunk. When Radha finally regained consciousness. She was speaking to Herself in a very incoherent way.

Continuing to wander, the gopis soon reached the bank of the Yamuna. There they worshiped the sun god. hoping that he might make it possible for them to fulfill the desires of Srimati Radharani. But Radha was rapt in remembering the different pastimes She had enjoyed with Krsna there on the Yamuna's shores, and so She fainted once again. Lalita took a garland Krsna had once worn and held it under Radharani's nose. After a long lime, Sri Radha regained consciousness.

Some moments later Radha saw a herd of cows. She thought that Krsna must be there lending His cows, and so She excitedly ran towards the herd. But when She reached there, She did not find Krsna. Instead, She found that the cows were in a very dejected state, making sounds of lamentation due to their own feelings of separation from Krsna. In an angry mood, Radha said, "The king, Kamsa, is responsible for this. He has carried away Krsna, who is the very life of Vraja. Let a black snake bite Kamsa’s chest with great anger.” Having said this, Radha once again fainted and fell to the ground. Ever so gently, Lalita moved Radha's body, holding on to Her lovingly. Gradually Radha opened Her eyes, and the party walked back towards the Yamuna.

When they reached the riverbank Radharani saw, there in the soft sand, Krsna’s footprint, with all kinds of identifying marks like a flag, thunderbolt, and so on. Of course, this loo reminded Her of Krsna, and so Her feelings of separation grew more intense as She remembered Krsna more and more. Seeing Radharani in this condition, Lalita told Her, “Let us again go to worship Surya- narayana and beg him to fulfill Your desire.” So, with palms together Radha prayed to the sun god, begging that he fulfill Her desire. Somehow Visakha could see indications that the sun god had blessed Radha, though She did not know what form that benediction might take.

Looking out over the majesty of the Yamuna, Radharani said. “O friend, Yamuna-devi! All the groves on your white sandy banks are parts of Krsna's playground.” Saying this. Radha again fainted, falling onto the soft sand on the bank of the Yamuna. And, again, Lalita was able to bring Radha back to external consciousness by placing under Her nose a flower garland that had been worn by Vanamali Krsna and that Visakha had brought for that purpose.

## Stealing Krsna’s Flute

Some time after the autumn season, during which Krsna enjoys the rasa-lila, the spring season promises Krsna the enjoyments of Holi. During one such festive season, the gopis stole Krsna's flute.

Each year, during springtime, the residents of Vraja-mandala are blessed by a playful pastime known as Holi, the festival of colors, which takes place in either the morning or evening. One special evening during this season Sri Krsna and His brother, Balarama, went into Vrndavana forest to participate in the Holi festival. This festival is part of the local customs of Vraja, and on this occasion it was so pleasing that even the demigods desired to see it, Sri Krsna was holding His flute and wearing a fresh garland, silk garments, and glittering ornaments. Balarama was holding His plough. And the two of Them, along with all Their respective friends, were happily enjoying the Holi pastime.

Both Krsna and Balarama, each one out of respect for the other’s unique loving mood, engaged in Holi play separately with Their very own gopi companions. And They each sang a special alapana in a raga suffused with affectionate and loving emotions. Because of the special features of the Holi festival, and due to Krsna's and Balarama’s participation in it, the land of Vrndavana looked even more beautiful and auspicious than it usually does.

The cowherd boys and their associates sang sweet songs in different rhythms accompanied by the sweet sound of karatalas and mrdangas. Meanwhile, Krsna and Balaramaa wandered around in the forest, which was very lovely and pleasant, filled as it was with row upon row of young trees. Balarama, the holder of the plow, looked very elegant while strolling about with His own group of young gopi consorts. He wore a single golden earring, and because He had been drinking varum, His eyes rolled around in His head. In His ecstasy Baladeva danced gracefully to the beat of the cowherd boys’ songs. He appeared as if He were the personification of bliss, while singing, laughing, and throwing kunkuma powder on His beloved gopis.

Many beautiful-eyed gopis, like Candravali, Radha, Syama, and Bhadra, along with their innumerable sakhis, started to sing a variety of songs while playing the vina and other musical instruments and throwing colored powder. In the meantime. Krsna played His flute very sweetly. Even though Krsna played very softly and the sound of His flute could not always be heard over the rest of the music, its beauty defeated the gopis’ long-standing pride in the sound of their own voices. Thus being defeated, the gopis surrounded Krsna and threw kunkuma powder at His lotus face. Krsna lowered His face to protect Himself from this loving onslaught but did not stop playing His flute, the sound of which filled the air with rhythmic and melodic flurries.

In this way, Krsna and Balarama, each accompanied by His own group of friends and amorous companions, were each separately enjoying the festival of Holi. At one point, while these two groups were moving about the forest in a playful mood, Krsna's group and Balarama’s group drew near to one another. In one sense. Balarama’s gopi consorts are like the sisters-in-law of Krsna, being something like the wives of Krsna's elder brother. In the same way, Krsna is like the younger brother-in-law of Balarama’s gopi consorts. So, those ladies who were attached to Balarama because of their affectionate feelings for Krsna as their younger brother-in-law moved closer to Sri Krsna while dancing and chanting very nicely. Then they very playfully threw kunkuma powder at Him, and Krsna, now covered in the colored powder thrown by the consorts of Balarama, signaled to His friends by shooting them a special glance. Having received this signal from Sri Krsna, all His cowherd friends gathered together and threw white, red, black, and yellow powder at Balarama’s affectionate consorts, who then ran away to protect themselves from this multi-colored attack. Krsna mocked His brother's consorts with playful smiles and taunting sounds from His flute.
