Krsna’s consorts too began to mock Balarama's companions with their sweet voices, and Krsna’s cowherd friends joined in the fun by clapping and laughing.

This agitated Balarama, who angrily ran after Krsna's associates. The sight of Balarama in this angry mood was more than enough to cause the consorts of Krsna to run away in fear. Krsna's cowherd friends, however, stepped forward to challenge Balarama. But as they drew closer to Krsna's powerful elder brother, Balarama laughed and caught hold of them, one by one, holding each boy under His arm while covering His captive in colored powder.

Overwhelmed by the playful mood, Krsna’s cowherd boyfriends lost all sense of awe and reverence for Balarama. Somehow they all managed to wrest themselves from Baladeva’s grip and, as a group, they fearlessly retaliated with a volley of vermilion powder. Baladeva was defiant, although defeated. When Krsna saw that His brother had been attacked by I lis friends He said, "What you all have done is not proper. It was quite ungentlemanly of you to gang up on My brother, who was alone. I do not approve." In this way Krsna subdued His friends.

Meanwhile, Balarama, anointed with vermilion, looked like a full moon glowing yellow in the red evening sunset. Captivated by this beautiful sight, Balarama’s gopi companions surrounded their beloved and, singing and dancing blissfully, wandered off into the forest with Him.

Once Balarama and His consorts had left, Krsna’s own preynsis. His loving female companions, felt very pleased that they too now had an opportunity to play with Krsna. Standing some distance from their beloved, they quietly made plans to steal Krsna’s flute.

Very discreetly they discussed what they would do, “How can we steal the flute from His hand, which looks like a snake and is scented by aguru? Let us see how our dearmost beloved will be able to cope once He becomes morose in separation from His flute! As long as that flute rests in His hand, it is like a lance, mercilessly piercing our hearts with its sweet and enchanting melodies. We must therefore capture that flute.''

The gopis were now all huddled together in one corner of the forest grove, whispering among themselves, while Krsna stood by Himself some distance away. “We can’t possibly take the flute from Krsna while He's holding it," the gopis said, “but it never leaves His hand. If perhaps He was in some sort of bewildered state, we might be able to take it from Him. But even then the flute will not just fall from His hand. Oh! How can we lake Krsna's flute from Him?”

The gopis decided their best approach would be to steal Krsna's flute while He was bewildered or enchanted or otherwise overwhelmed. So, in this way, hatching a plan to somehow trick Krsna, the gopis approached Radharani and said, “O most fortunate Radha! If You want to capture Krsna’s flute, then for some time please show Him Your stubborn, angry mood. Your impudence will surely drive Krsna away, and thus our association will become more attractive to Him."

Sometime after these secret discussions, Krsna’s close friend Kusumasava, Madhumangala, came to know about the gopis' plan, though ignorant of the details. Kusumasava was talkative by nature, and so he boldly alerted Krsna, “O friend! Listen! Though the gopis are very learned, their singing is no match for Your sweet flute playing. Out of jealousy, they are planning to steal Your flute. To prevent this. You should give Your flute to me and. instead of playing the flute, sing in a loud, full voice. The gopis will not be able to take the flute from me, because of the power I have gained from my brahminical austerities.”

“O friend!" Krsna replied. "We have indeed witnessed your considerable brahminical prowess during the spring festival. There is no doubt that today you will show us how you are uniquely suited to protect My flute.”

Kusumasava said, “O my dear Krsna! I, by whose power You have ascended to this most elevated position, will not simply protect Your flute. Do not underestimate me. I will not even need to protect Your flute, as no one will even be able to see me. I am unrivaled in my ability to run away and hide. If no one can even find me, how could they catch me? I will protect Your flute by keeping it perfectly hidden.’’

“If in this festive mood,” Krsna replied, “the intoxicated gopis somehow manage to take the flute from your hand, what can you do to stop them? If they do take it away, how will you get it back?”

Somewhat offended, Kusumasava replied, “Give me a chance! Why don’t You, just once, trust the power of my penance? Do You not believe in my abilities?” Having said this, Kusumasava grabbed the flute out of Krsna’s hand and lucked it under his arm. “Please, sing a song,” he requested Krsna.

“All right,” Krsna said, “I will do as you like.”

Krsna sang with great virtuosity in a voice so sweet it conquered the sound of the vina. His voice was so beautiful that the Yamuna slopped flowing. Stunned by Krsna’s singing, the river stood still, as if it had become stone, and the trees rained tears. The deer felt so blissful that drops of perspiration appeared behind their ears; in fact, the young does were happily licking these sweet drops from each other’s ears. It was as if streams of sweetness, the liquid form of Krsna's singing, had entered the ear holes of the deer and then oozed out because there was not enough room for it all. Naturally the deer wanted to taste that sweet essence.

Regarding Krsna's transcendent performance, Kusumasava said, “O Lalite! My dear friend is singing in charchari-tala. Never before have I heard someone sing so expertly. I know you are all very proud of your singing, but you must admit - you could never sing well enough to inspire happiness like this."

Sangita-vidya, a gopi who was especially gifted in music, said, "O unintelligent one! Lalita, who is wearing silk cloth, can sing much better than this. So confident am I of this that I would confidently place a bet. Let Krsna's flute be the wager. I have no doubt that you, crooked-minded fellow that you are, will lose the flute.”

“O Sangitavidye!” Kusumasava said. “This sangita vidya, or knowledge about singing, is known only by me. Even through meditation, ordinary people cannot come to know anything about it. If, at the proper time and after careful consideration, I have judged whose singing is the best, then only will it be pleasing to every one, including even the demigods.”

To this Sangita-vidya replied, “O son of a brahmana! This has to do with more than mere recitation of the Vedas, by which Vedic scholars may be satisfied. Who are you to judge something so subtle and refined?”

While this conversation was going on, Krsna continued singing, smiling all the while. Through special glances and movements of His eyebrows Krsna communicated clearly enough with Kusumasava that he could understand Krsna’s intention.

Having thus received Krsna’s message, Kusumasava said, “O proud lady! If you are not willing to accept my judgment, then let us use a different system to assess your singing voice. If any one of you can sing in such a way that the flow of Yamuna is stopped, that the trees and creepers shed tears, and that birds and other animals are overwhelmed with ecstasy - as it so happened when my friend Krsna sang - then we can safely say that your singing is on par with Krsna’s, But I am sure about this: Nowhere can we find so much potency as that potency we feel when Krsna sings. Therefore. I confidently wager the flute. If you agree, then you should offer Sri Radha as a wager for your side.”

“O wicked-minded boy!" Lalita said. “In gambling matches, the stakes on either side must be of equal value. Don't demand that we equate glass with gold!”

To this Kusumasava responded, “O worshipable one! Do you honestly think my dear friend’s flute is like glass and that your friend Radha is like gold? What kind of assertion is that?”

“Why is that so hard to accept?” Lalita answered. “If you want equal stakes, then you should boldly wager your friend Krsna.”

Kusumasava said, “All right then. Since I am the best among all pure-hearted persons, I will wager my friend. Please, begin singing.” With courage and enthusiasm Lalita gave up her bashfulness and started to sing a gentle song in kedara-raga. Her throat soon became red as her eyebrows danced. Lalita sung so nicely the sound of her voice surpassed the voices of the Gandharvas, whose pride was thus vanquished.

But the sound of Lalita’s singing filled Kusumasava with gleeful pride. Loudly he declared, “Defeated! O Lalita! You are defeated!” Laughing and carrying on in this way, Kusumasava was dancing about, both arms raised above his head. He had forgotten that Krsna’s flute was lucked under his arm, and so as soon as he raised his hands in the air the flute fell to the ground. No one noticed, except Sangita-vidya, who grabbed the flute and hid it away. No one saw her do it. Nor did she tell any of her friends.

Once the flute had been properly hidden, she said to talkative Kusumasava, “Hey! Why are you bursting with happiness over something that never happened? You're jumping around like a madman. Before you get so carried away, let your friend with the fickle ankle-bells consider who is the actual winner!”

Kusumasava replied, “O you whom the learned respect! According to my judgment, your defeat is assured. The wager was dependent on singing in car- cari-tala. Lalita sang only one little fragment, and in dvipadika-tala. O smiling face! Please consider: How could she not be defeated?”

This statement brought only mockery from Lalita, “O uncouth one! What is wonderful about carcari, dvipadika, or jambhali? The greatness of these meters is in the use of murchana, svara, and grama.”

To this, Sangita-vidya added, “Just see the great power and sweetness of Lalita s singing. Just see! By the power of her singing the jeweled platform around her has turned into water. And the water that was flowing around this tree has become solid, so that it now forms a platform around the tree. The Yamuna has a divine nature, the trees and creepers of Vrndavana are full of knowledge, and the animals of Vrndavana are fully conscious. It is only natural for them to react to the flute song of your friend. There is nothing special about it. But the singing of our friend is truly special. She has transformed stone and water. That’s why we have won. Therefore, bring your friend here and hand Him over to us.”

Subala-sakha said, “O Sangita-vidya! You have become so foolish, being covered by ignorance. I hardly recognize you as that same, most intelligent Sangita-vidya . Can't you understand how Kusumasava could not possibly give away Krsna in a wager? Surely one can lose something that belongs to him, but no one can give away what was never his. And under no circumstances could one give away his master.”

Krsna said, “Kusumasava! You can no longer sit in the place of greatest respect. You have been defeated by these girls, who are very proud of their victory, having become intoxicated by this festival. You now find yourself in a very hopeless situation, being unable to release yourself. Therefore, you must immediately give Me back My flute. Otherwise it will be lost along with you.”

"But, my friend!” Kusumasava protested. ‘I have won! You should thus exhibit Your prowess and lake hold of their dear friend, Radha, with great pleasure."

Lalita said. "O shameless, servile ass! Dullard! The song upon which you wagered has itself proclaimed my victory.”

Kusumasava replied, “O greedy woman! If you are truly so proud, then take the flute, which I have hidden here on my person, and let me run away from here” Having said this. Kusumasava looked for the flute but could not find it. “O friend!” he said. "Out of fear, the flute has run away from me. I think it has gone to some safe garden where the creepers are unbroken.”

These words brought a smile to everyone’s face.

## Killing Sankhacuda

Meanwhile, Balarama’s beloved gopis were greatly increasing in beauty while playing Holi. At that time, a lowly servant of Kuvera named Sankhacuda arrived there intending to kidnap the jewel-like consorts of Lord Balarama. Sankhacuda was a foolish demon with no sense of what was right or wrong. He came to be in that place as if he had been called personally by death.

In the turban on his head Sankhacuda wore a beautiful jewel. Full of pride, his face was always turned upwards. When the gopis of Lord Balarama saw Sankhacuda coming towards them, they became very frightened, just like doves are frightened in the presence of a tiger. “O Balarama! O Krsna!” they cried out. “Please protect us!” But even before Balarama could hear those painful cries, Krsna abandoned His Holi pastimes and began to chase after the Yaksa. Sankhacuda fled hurriedly away, but Krsna ran after the Yaksa so quickly that His feet did not appear to touch the ground. Balarama too was soon chasing after Sankhacuda. Seeing Krsna and Balarama chasing after him, that sinful fellow abandoned his attempt at kidnapping and ran away out of fear for his life. While running away, Sankhacuda was screaming at his pursuers in great anger. Because he was running so fast, and because Krsna was chasing after him with one-pointed determination, the Yaksa turned pale. The sight of Krsna chasing Sankhacuda looked like the king of lions running after the king of elephants or like a hawk flying after a crow or like Garuda hunting a snake. Moments later, Krsna, who always gives happiness to His surrendered devotees, caught hold of Sankhacuda from behind, taking a fistful of the Yaksa’s hair. With His other hand Krsna pummeled Sankhacuda's face, repeatedly striking the Yaksa with His fist. Though Krsna’s hand is as soft as a lotus flower, still His fist is so hard it could easily smash the head of that wicked Yaksa, whose head was as hard as a tortoise's shell.

In Sankhacuda’s turban there was a very nice gem, expertly carved and looking quite beautiful. Krsna look that gem from the head of the Yaksa and in great ecstasy gave it to His elder brother, powerful Balarama, in front of Balarama’s consorts, who were all witness to this pastime.

## Krsna Searches for His Flute on the Gopis’ Bodies

Krsna, the supreme enjoyer, who is most pleasing to the mind and senses, was relishing the mood of that joyous festival of stealing the flute. And yet, His happiness became still more brilliant by joining with His cowherd friends and three jems like swara mani, chinta mani and ramanimani. In the midst of their great joy-filled festival, Sankhacuda appeared and almost put an end to their fun. But Krsna was able to bring the festival back to life, much like adding more oil to a lamp that was just about to be extinguished or like a great downpour fills a pond about to dry up or like a king might renovate a dilapidated palace.

Taking the opportunity to falsely accuse His cowherd girlfriends of stealing the flute, Krsna first approached one girl and said. "You are the thief!” Then He approached someone else and. catching hold of her. said. "No! You are the thief. The rascal who has stolen my flute is none other than you." In this way Krsna went to each gopi, accused her, and then searched for the flute in her clothes.

In response to Krsna's accusations, the gopis smiled slightly and moved their eyebrows about in very attractive ways while loudly making accusations of their own: “O friend of Kusumasava! This sort of inappropriate conduct is just suitable for You. Indeed, it is what we expect of You! How could the flute slip from Your hand and end up behind one of our veils or in the folds of one of our skirts? If we have actually stolen Your flute, then of course You may punish us severely. But if it turns out that we have not stolen Your flute, then for our trouble we will consider ourselves entirely justified to snatch away the Kaustubha gem and Your necklace.”

Filled with pride, Candravali said, “O arrogant fellow who gives pain to women!

Don’t You remember? The flute that was supposed to be stolen by us innocent maidens was in actuality taken forcefully from Your hand by Kusumasava, who feared that we might take it away from You.”

Kusumasava said, “When you say ‘supposed to be stolen by us' that means you had the intention to steal. This proves you have stolen it. Otherwise, tell us what proof you have that I forcibly took the flute from Krsna’s hand.”

“All are witnesses who are present here,” said Candravali.

“All who are present here gladly oppose me,” replied Kusumasava.

“But Krsna is also a witness. He is your friend." said Candravali.

“That cannot be true." Kusumasava protested. "If my friend saw me forcibly take the flute from His hand, then why did He whose mind is pure and free from inappropriate motives search for the flute on your persons? It is therefore certain that you ladies have stolen the flute. There is no doubt that your explanation is simply a lie.”

After defending himself in this way, Kusumasava, who is effulgent, clever, and fearless, again spoke to Krsna, “O my dear friend! Sangita-vidya, acting alone, has stolen Your flute, which always rests happily in Your lotus hand. These other gopis have not stolen it.”

Hearing this, the most clever Sangita-vidya, though fearful, walked with a graceful gait to where Lalita was standing and, glancing cunningly around to make sure no one was looking, slipped Krsna's flute into Lalita’s hand.

But Kusumasava had been watching, and though he didn't see her give Lalita the flute, by observing Sangita-vidya s movements he was convinced. Once again, he loudly spoke up. "Friend! The thief is definitely this Sangita-vidya. She has definitely stolen Your flute. She has given herself away. Because among ourselves we jokingly uttered the name Sangita-vidya. referring to the presiding goddess of musical knowledge, she heard us speaking and took it that we were talking about her. Therefore she became frightened. I could see it. This is a sure sign that she is the thief.”

While Kusumasava was carrying on, Sangita-vidya made sure that Lalita had the flute safely concealed and then stepped forward to proudly proclaim, “ O Vatu, little boy, what is the matter? I see you are a great hypocrite. I was playing in the Holi festival. How and when could I have stolen the flute? O heartless one! What reason could I have for stealing the flute? Don't you know that if you falsely accuse someone, then your own stock of sinful reactions will swell in number? O talkative boy! Incarnation of injustice! Tell me what is the justification for your false accusations? Go away! It is only because I am feeling merciful that I have not punished you."

Sangita-vidya s soliloquy brought smiles to everyone’s faces. Meanwhile, Vanamali Krsna resumed His search for the flute on the gopis’ bodies, finally coming to Lalita, whom He touched with His lotus hand. But before He could inspect her more closely. Lalita transferred the flute to the daughter of Maharaja Vrsabhanu. Srimati Radharani. in such a way that no one could sec it.

Then Lalita boldly came forward and announced. "O killer of Agha! Though I am innocent. You insist on touching me, due to Your being mad with the pride of love. I will put a stop to this display of impudence, born of pride. I am not lying. I do not have Your flute.” Saying this, Lalita removed her veil and shook it to prove that she was not hiding the flute there. Flashing her brilliant white teeth as she smiled, Lalita continued, “You who are under the spell of pride and lust! Please consider: When You ran after Sankhacuda it’s quite possible that You dropped Your flute without even realizing it. Whatever the case, Your attempts to corner me have proven fruitless.”

“Dear friend,” Kusumasava excitedly said. “If not Lalita, then it must have been Radha who stole Your flute.”

“O cool-headed one,” Krsna said. “I think you are right. My flute must be in Radha’s possession. I must search Her next.” Just then, as Pitambara Krsna, the crest jewel of cleverness, turned His love-intoxicated eyes toward Radha, ready to begin His search, one of Balarama's beloveds appeared on the scene.

She held the Syamantaka jewel, which Krsna had taken from the head of Sankhacuda and given to Balarama. That gem gave one more pleasure than having achieved the perfection of the eight mystic siddhis, and there was nothing that could be compared to its dazzling effulgence. Indeed, the light that jewel gave off could illuminate heaven. Balarama’s consort reported to Radha, with great pleasure, “O Sri Radha, endowed with all good qualities! Please listen to my words. Balarama, the elder brother of Damodara, has sent this gem as a gift for You. Please accept it.”

In great ecstasy Radha immediately extended both of Her hands to receive the gem. In doing so, Radha s veil fell from Her head to the ground, and with it the flute.

Witnessing this, Kusumasava’s face lit up with excitement. He slapped his armpit and twisted his neck while dancing about awkwardly and heartily laughing and clapping. Krsna and the large group of friends who surrounded Him were also loudly laughing, but at the spectacle Kusumasava was creating.

Krsna said, "O Lalita! Unrepentant one! You have spoken truthfully. While running after that demon. My flute, seeing our preoccupation with the jewel of Sankhacuda, fell neglected and fell on the ground. Now. seeing Radha s preoccupation with the same jewel. My flute has again become angry and fallen on the ground.”

Kusumasava said, “Intelligence on the level of Brhaspati cannot be found in anyone of Your age. It is no surprise that Vrsabhanavi Radha, who has stolen the mind of my perfect, faultless, and auspicious friend Krsna, also steals His enchanting flute, which is inauspicious and full of holes. What is surprising to me is that You, Krsna, my friend, at one point concluded that I took the flute! If I had taken the flute, then how did it end up in Radha’s possession? O friend with the hue of a tamala tree, please now reclaim Your flute.”

Having said this, Kusumasava picked up the flute and gave it to Krsna, who accepted the flute with great pleasure and started to play it very softly and sweetly, thereby announcing the end of the pastime as all the beautiful gopis displayed various symptoms of ecstatic bliss.

## Nava Vrindavan pastime

Krsna has said, vrndavanam parityajya padam ekam na gacchati: “I never take even one step out of Vrndavana.’’ In the spiritual world there are three distinct, fully spiritual locales: Dvaraka, Mathura, and Vrndavana. In each place a different form of Krsna enjoys different pastimes, each in a different bhava: aisvarya-bhava in Dvaraka, aisvarya- and madhurya-bhava in Mathura, and madhurya-bhava in Vrndavana. When Krsna exhibits His bhauma-lila, at that time He appears in Bhauma Vrndavana, also known as Gokula, from there he goes to Mathura, and finally He goes to Dvaraka. When He leaves Vrndavana the people there can no longer see Him anymore, at least not outwardly. Though Krsna stays forever in Vrndavana and never leaves, in the eyes of the people in general He is no longer there. For this reason, all the Vraja-vasis feel a sense of separation from Krsna.

The following narration is adapted from Srila Rupa Gosvami’s play Lalita-Madhava, in which it is explained how Radharani and the other preyasl-gopis of Krsna felt separation from Him after He left Vraja. Some time later each one of the 16,108 gopis of Vrndavana were transferred to Dvaraka, where they played the role of Krsna’s queens.

On one special morning, having been invited by Kamsa, Krsna had to leave Vrndavana and go to Mathura. The day before, Akrura had come to Vrndavana to personally take Krsna away. In the early morning, as news of Krsna's immi nent departure spread all over Vraja, there was heard a great and terrible sound, like a roar from the depths of an agitated ocean. Vrnda-devi asked Paurnamasi the reason for this, and Paurnamasi told Her, “Yesterday, when peacock-feather-crowned Krsna came back to Vrndavana village after killing the Kesi demon, Akrura arrived here on the order of Kamsa. Today Akrura will take Krsna and Balarama to the city of Mathura. All the gopis have not slept for the entire night. Because of their ecstatic love for Krsna they have been crying and fearful. They are very angry at Akrura. For them the night passed like a single moment.”

Unhappy to hear this news, Vrnda thought, “If Krsna leaves Vrndavana, what reason will I have to beautify the forest groves of Vraja. What reason will I have to make a bed of forest flowers? And why should I make the fragrant lower-bearing vines blossom, even after the spring season is past?”

Vrnda and Paurnamasi went to the gate of Nanda Maharaja's palace. There they found Mother Yasoda, so agitated that She had forgotten to pray to Lord Narayana for Her son’s safe journey. She had forgotten also to prepare lunch for Her son to take on His journey. All She could do was embrace Krsna, again and again, as She wept. The tears from Her eyes were drenching Krsna’s body.

Meanwhile gopis like Saibya, Syama, and Bhadra were each reacting in their own pathetic way to the thought of Krsna’s imminent departure. Candravali was preparing a flower garland to offer to Krsna at noon, but when She heard the heart-breaking news She was so overwhelmed that the half-strung flower garland slipped from Her hands and She immediately fell down unconscious. Padma carefully placed Candravali’s lifeless body in front of Akrura’s chariot and wept piteously.

Visakha and Radha s other sakhis were hesitant to tell Radha about Krsna’s departure. Should they tell Her or not? How could Radha tolerate separation from Krsna? Even Krsna’s momentary absence from Her vision caused by the blinking of Her eyes was unbearable to Radha, so much so that She was known to glorify the good fortune of fish, who have eyes that never blink.

But when Radha heard the tumultuous sound of the gopis’ crying She look off running like a frightened doe. Agitated with transcendental madness, She babbled incoherently. Lalita and Visakha tried to console Radha, but what could pacify Her? One moment She would be loudly crying, the next moment She would be rolling on the ground in front of Akrura's chariot, and the next moment She would look at Krsna's face, Her eyes filled with tears. She would usually never glance directly at Krsna, even in the presence of Her confidential gopi friends. But now She was directly before Her elders, staring at Krsna with tearfilled eyes. Placing a blade of grass between Her teeth. She begged Balarama not to leave Vrndavana.

Nonetheless, Krsna left Vrndavana, reassuring the gopis with a message of hope that He would come back soon.

Some time later Visakha tried to pacify Radha by reminding Her about Krsna’s promise to return to Vraja. Radha said, “You gopis may be convinced that Krsna is a very compassionate person and that He will come back. But the pain of being separated from Krsna, which is My greatest enemy, is cutting My heart to pieces. This enemy of mine will never allow me to live happily.”

Being very frightened, Radharani began to cry. Between Her tears She said, “Now that Krsna has left, where can ! hear the nectarean sounds of His flute? I do not feel any peace, not even for a moment. There are no words that can free Me from My grief. Alas! Where has the lord of My life gone? I cannot see Krsna anymore! What is the use of My life?"

To pacify Radharani, Lalita said ’’O lotus-eyed Radha! Krsna can never leave us. He's just playing a joke on us. Just search for Him in this forest of Vrndavana. You will definitely find Him.”

Radharani said, “Yes. You are correct. It is not possible for Krsna to leave us. Let us search for Him in this forest.” Radharani started to search for Krsna, but He was nowhere to be found.

After some time they had reached the bank of the Yamuna, where they worshiped the sun god to request that he fulfill Radharani’s desire. But Radha was soon caught up in remembering the different pastimes She had enjoyed with Krsna there on the bank of the Yamuna and She fainted. Lalita held a garland that Krsna had worn under Radha’s nose, and after a long time She regained consciousness.

Not too long after that Radharani saw a Herd of cows in the distance. She thought that perhaps Krsna was tending the cows, and so, desiring to see Krsna, She started to run towards them. But when She came closer to the Herd She did not find Krsna. Actually, She found that the cows were in a very dejected state, making sounds of lamentation due to the pain of separation from Krsna. In an angry mood Radharani said, “King Kamsa is responsible for this. He carried away Krsna, who is the very life of Vraja. Let a black snake bite his chest with great anger.” Having said this, Radha again fainted and fell to the ground. Lalita gently held Radha s body. Gradually Radha opened Her eyes again, and then they all walked back towards the Yamuna.

## The Disappearance of Radharani and Her Appearance in sun planate

When they had reached the bank of the Yamuna, Radharani saw Krsna’s footprints, with all kinds of marks like Dhwaja ( flag) and Ankusa (goad)etc. This sight once again reminded Her of Krsna, and She felt the ever increasing pain of separation as She remembered Krsna more and more. Seeing Radharani in this condition. Lalita told Her, “Let us once again go worship Surya-Bhagavan and beg Him to fulfill Your desire.” So Radharani prayed to the sun god with folded hands, asking that he fulfill Her desire. Visakha felt She could see some indication that the sun god had indeed blessed Sri Radha.

Radharani looked out over the Yamuna and said, “O Yamuna-devi! Every kunja on your banks was once a playhouse of Krsna.” Saying this, Radharaniagain fainted and fell on the ground. And, once again, Lalita and Visakha held one of Krsna's flower garlands to Radha’s nose. After a long time Radha came back to external consciousness. She told Lalita, "O sakhi! I just had the most terrible dream. It was so clear, as clear I am seeing you right now. One very fearsome person, a wicked messenger of Kamsa, came to Vrndavana in a chariot and took Krsna away. Oh! I pray, let even thing in Vrndavana be auspicious. Because I had a bad dream I should take a bath in the Yamuna and then take darsana of Mukunda.”

Visakha said, "O sakhi! Let us go to Khela-tirtha, Kaliya Lake. Krsna used to play there.” The three of them then went to Khela-tirtha.

Once there, Radharani said, "O Visakha! Today you have brought me to Khela-tirtha. Just see! Your friend Krsna is playing in that forest of blue lotus flowers in the waves of the Yamuna River. He is hiding among the blue flowers and waving His hands.”

Hearing this, Visakha said, "Then we should wade out into the Yamuna and try to catch Krsna. This time we will not let Him gel away.” Radha and Visakha both swam out into the deepest part of Kaliya Lake and, without warning, drowned in the deep water there. Lalita watched in great amazement while Visakha followed Radha under the surface of the dark water.

Radha had entered into the lake until the water reached Her knees, then Her thighs, then Her neck...and then it was completely over Her head. At that time Radha could feel Krsna's embrace, a feeling that Radha was all too eager to reciprocate. She was in great ecstasy to find that Her desire to serve Krsna was being fulfilled. In this state, warmed by Krsna's embrace, Radha lost consciousness.

At first Visakha was filled with fear and panic to see Radha disappear into the deep water of the Yamuna. But then in the water she could see Krsna carefully carrying Radha away. Moments later Visakha lost consciousness, giving herself to the Yamuna as she too disappeared beneath the surface of its waters.

In her unconscious state Visakha heard the voice of the sun god say, “O daughter! Radha cannot live without Krsna any longer. Now bring Radha to me.” This is how Visakha came to bring Radha to the sun planet. Wise persons know that the river Yamuna is another form of Visakha. They also know that Visakha is the daughter of the sun god.

Meanwhile, as Lalita saw Radha and Visakha drown in the river, she cried out loudly, first in distress and then in separation from her friends. Lalita said, "Alas! My dear friends Radha and Visakha have entered the waters of the Yamuna and will never come out again!”

Before Krsna had left for Mathura, Rad haram was worshiping the sun god daily. She was very devoted to Surya-deva, and Surya-deva was very pleased with Her service. He knew that Radharani planned to commit suicide by drowning Herself in the Yamuna. He called his daughter Yamuna and told her, “Today, during mid-day, your friends Radharani and Visakha will enter your water in order to leave their bodies due to separation from Krsna. You should rescue them before they leave their bodies and then bring them to my house.” According to these instructions, Yamuna-devi saved Radha and Visakha and brought them to her father. When Radha jumped into the Yamuna, She had the Syamantaka mani on Her head. That jewel fell into the water, so Yamuna-devi collected the jewel and gave it to her father as well. The jewel was so named because Radharani wore it on Her simanta, the part in Her hair.

Yamuna-devi is the daughter of Surya-deva, and Yamaraja is his son. Surya-deva's wife is named Samjna. He ordered his wife to take care of Radha and Visakha as if they were her very own daughters. In fact, Visakha had previously been the younger sister of Yamaraja, the daughter of Samjna. By the will of Surya-deva, his daughter had taken birth as Visakha in Gokula to facilitate Krsna's sweet pastimes with Radharani. Because Radharani was a good friend of her daughter. Mother Samjna was very attached to Sri Radha, whom she cared for very nicely, treating Her as a daughter.

Though Radharani was maintained there with much care and affection, still She was not happy. She was still suffering due to separation from Krsna andfrom Vrndavana. After staying there for some days, when She could not tolerate the pain and suffering of separation one moment longer, She approached Mother Samjna and told Her, “O Mother! Due to My bad fortune I have been removed from Vrndavana and kept in this far away place. In Vrndavana I would have at least satisfied My heart by seeing the places where Krsna performed His pastimes. Please make some arrangement so that I can have darsana of Vrndavana, which is the place of pastimes for Krsna, the crown jewel of the gopis.”

After hearing this from Radharani. Samjna requested Visvakarma to build a replica of Vrndavana next to Krsna's palace in Dvaraka. She said to him, “O architect that decorates the world! Please build another Vrndavana, splendid with bhandira trees. There should be plenty of trees and creepers, with the Yamuna flowing nearby. There should also be Govardhana Hill." According to this request Visvakarma created a second Vrndavana near Dvaraka filled with trees and creepers. Everything there was just like Vrndavana, including the river Yamuna and Govardhana Hill.

Sani’s mother, Chaya, told Radharani, “O lotus-faced girl! You are the essence of all happiness in these three worlds. Here resides Your dearmost beloved, upon whom all the sages of Surya-mandala (sun globe) meditate, considering Him God. Why are You so worried?"

Visakha laughed at this and said. "Radha’s beloved Krsna may be the object of worship for great saints on the sun planet, but that will not satisfy Radha. O Mother! I will tell you something. Please listen. Once, when the gopis were searching for Krsna in the forest of Vrndavana. He manifested Himself in the form of four-armed Narayana. When the gopis saw this exalted and opulent form of Lord Narayana their ecstatic feelings for Krsna dissipated completely. The Vraja-gopis are not interested in any other form the Lord besides His original form as the son of Nanda Maharaja."

## Radharani’s Appearance in Dvaraka as Satyabhama

At that time there was a king in Dvaraka region known as Satrajit. He had been worshiping the sun god for many years with the desire to get wealth and the best child. Being pleased with Satrajit’s worship, the sun god offered him Radharani as his daughter. As the daughter of Satrajit, Radha was known as Satyabhama.

The sun god also gave Satrajit the Syamantaka jewel, which had been given to Surya-deva by his daughter Yamuna-devi. It had fallen from Radharani’s head when She drowned in the Yamuna. Previous to this the jewel had been an ornament on the head of Sankhacuda, who had tried to kidnap Radha and the other Vraja-gopis. Krsna had killed Sankhacuda for this offense. After killing the miscreant, Krsna had taken this jewel from his head and given it to His brother, Balarama. Out of affection Balarama had given the jewel to Radharani as a gift. And Radharani wore the jewel on the part in Her hair, which is called the simanta . That’s why this jewel is known as the Syamantaka jewel.

So, Surya-bhagavan gave this Sayamantaka jewel to King Satrajit along with Radharani, who as the daughter of Satrajit became known as Satyabhama. When the sun god offered these two treasures to the king he told him, ‘'You should offer this girl in marriage to the man whom Narada Muni proposes to you. Meanwhile, keep this jewel in your house very nicely and offer daily worship to it. If you do this properly, the jewel will give you eight bhara of gold every day.”

Some time later Narada Muni visited Satrajit and told him, “A lion will kill your brother Prasena and take away the Syamantaka jewel. That lion will be defeated by Jambavan, the king of the bhallukas (bears). Finally, the jewel will come to the hand of Krsna. As long as all-powerful Krsna does not come to see you, keep this girl Satyabhama safely in your palace.”

## Candravali Appears as Rukmini

After leaving Vrndavana with Akrura, Krsna went to Mathura, where He stayed for thirteen years and six months. During that lime Candravali’s husband, Govardhana Mala, died. Radha and Visakha also left their bodies, having drowned in the Yamuna. And Lalita too left her body by jumping off of Govardhana Hill. Bhismaka, the king of Vidarbha and the father of Candravali, was therefore understandably worried that his daughter too may commit suicide.

Around that time Bhismaka's son. Rukmi. came to know that Candravali was his sister. How did he know? Srutasrava-devi was the mother of Sisupala. She had been living in Vrndavana. When her brother Vasudeva was released from Kamsa’s prison she went to see him at her fathers house. At that time Gargi had told her all the secrets of Gokula, including the fact that Candravali was the daughter of King Bhismaka. Srutasrava relayed this fact to her son Sisupala, who in turn told his friend Rukmi. Thus Rukmi came to know that Candravali was his sister. Soon thereafter King Bhismaka sent his son Rukmi, to bring back Candravali.

Rukmi went to Vrndavana and spoke to Candravali’s mother-in-law, Bharunda. He told Her that Candravali was suffering in separation from her husband following his death and that it would therefore be nice for her to come back to her fathers house and stay there for some time. Bharunda agreed to this proposal, and so Rukma brought Candravali back to Kundan gram, where King Bhlsmaka was staying. After she had arrived there, Bhismaka gave Candravali the new name Rukmini. Thus Candravali came to be known among the people in general as Rukmini. After some time Rukma wanted to give Rukmini in marriage to his friend Sisupala. But when Rukmini came to know of her brother’s plan she became very worried, as she was not at all interested to marry Sisupala. She wanted to marry Krsna, so she sent a letter with an old brahmana to Krsna, requesting Him to come and rescue her. By Rukmini's request Krsna came just in lime and kidnapped her, taking her away from Vidarbha. He defeated all the other kings present there, competing for the hand of Rukmini, and brought herto Dvaraka, where He married Her. Thus Candravali appeared as Rukmini, the first of Krsna's eight principal queens in Dvaraka.

Rukmini served Krsna with sincerity and affection. And yet He was not satisfied, as He was always desiring to enjoy pastimes in Nava-Vrndavana. Thus He was always morose, because without Radharani how could He enjoy pastimes in Nava-vrndavana? He spent day and night remembering all His sweet pastimes with Sri Radha in Vrndavana, like the rasa-lila. in separation from Radharani, Sri Krsna would often faint, being overwhelmed with emotion.

## The Transfer of Satyabhama to Krsna’s palace in Dvaraka

After hearing this news from Narada Muni about his brother Prasena, King Satrajit was lost in lamentation. Somehow Satrajit came to publicly make false accusations against Krsna and thus committed a grave offense at Krsna’s lotus feet. But Satrajit repented for this mistake and so spent his days very painfully. At this time Devarsi Narada returned to Satrajit and told him, “O crooked-minded person! Before Krsna comes back from searching for the Syamantaka jewel you must send your daughter Satyabhama to His palace in Dvaraka. If you want to be saved from your offenses against Krsna, then you must do this immediately.” According to Narada’s instructions, King Satrajit immediately sent his daughter Satyabhama, who is Radharani Herself, to Dvaraka with her grandmother, the mother of Satrajit.

By the order of her son, Satrajit's mother prepared a palanquin to lake Satyabhama to Dvaraka. Satyabhama thought to herself, in the mood of Sri Radha, “I am such a hard-heated girl that I am still alive, even though I am separated from Krsna. Is there any other girl on this earth more hard-hearted than Me? Shame on My futile hopes! Shame on My mind and heart! Shame on My very life breath!”

Satyabhama asked Her grandmother where she was being taken. Her grandmother replied, “By the order of the great sage Narada, I am taking You to Dvaraka to offer You to Rukmini-devi.”

Satyabhama, again in the mood of Radharani, thought to herself, “I have heard that Narada Muni is the spiritual master of Paurnamasi. He is also very affectionate to Me. By the advice of Narada Muni, the sun god has made Satrajit My foster father.’’ Then She remembered something Surya-deva had told her: “Until Krsna has placed Your jewel (mani) on Your wrist (bandha), do not disclose to anyone that Your name is Radha.’

By this time Satyabhama and her grandmother had reached Krsna’s palace in Dvaraka. When Rukmini saw them, she thought. “Who is this old lady coming here with a girl like the goddess of incomparable beauty?” And when Satyabhama saw Rukmini she thought. "This queen is like a great flood of charming sweetness! She is as beautiful as one of the young gopis of Gokula.”

Satrajita’s mother offered Satyabhama to Rukmini and told her. “Rukmini-devi! My son Satrajita has offended Krsna in connection with the Syamantaka jewel. Thus he is offering his daughter Satyabhama to Krsna as a humble gift. I request that you please keep her with you as your sakhi and younger sister and give your love and affection to her."

Rukmini, who is Candravali reborn in Dvaraka, said, “I am blessed to get such a nice girl for my sakhi. Please return home. I will definitely take nice care of Satyabhama.’’ After hearing this promise from Rukmini, the grandmother of Satyabhama left Krsna’s palace.

Rukmini marveled at Satyabhama’s unrivaled beauty. She told her friend Madhavi, “Look! Look! The great ocean that is Satyabhama s beauty will surely attract Sri Krsna. He will fall in love with her and marry her. Even if I am very sober and steady, still I feel enchanted by her beauty. Her eyes look so peaceful. Her breathing, her two cheeks, and the beauty of her body all fill me with wonder. This girl looks as if she has already fallen in love with Krsna and has been separated from Him. And yet, even in separation, she looks so beautiful. How surprising!’’

Having said this to her friend Madhavi, Rukmini then decided to examine the heart of Satyabhama, so she approached Satyabhama and said to her, “O my friend! My heart feels great affection for you. My dear sister! Why do you look so unhappy?”

Satyabhama had faith in Rukmini’s words, because her heart too was filled with affection for her new friend, Rukmini. Satyabhama said, “O Queen! I am honored."

Rukmini thought to herself, “When Krsna returns He will definitely want to marry this girl. But He has promised my father that He will not accept another wife without my permission. Still, she is so beautiful that I think He will fail to keep His promise, and thus He will deviate from His vow.” Rukmini then privately disclosed these thoughts to her friend Madhavi.

Rukmini's thoughts returned to Satyabhama, to whom she said, “O beautiful girl! You look very sad. Can you kindly tell me what is the cause of your unhappiness?”

Satyabhama replied, “O Devi! My father Satrajit sent me here all of a sudden. Therefore I feel very sad.”

Rukmini said. “Do not be worried. As soon as Aryaputra (Sri Krsna) returns, I will offer you to Him.’’

Satyabhama said. "If you really love me. then do not say these words again.”

Rukmini asked. "Please tell me. Where would you like to stay?”

Satyabhama replied. "Please put me in such a place where I will not even hear any man's name. I am following a vow. I must complete it. Please let me keep my purity intact.”

Then, privately to Madhavi. Rukmini said, ’’O sakhi! Whatever I was plannig. this girl is asking for the same thing. My fear was that Krsna, after seeing her beauty, would not be able to keep His promise and would decide to marry her. But, fortunately, this girl is saying she does not want to hear any man's name, what to speak of seeing a man in person. She is following a vow. That is very good. We will arrange for her to stay in Nava-vrndavana. Please call Nava-vrnda.”

Madhavi said. “Yes. This is very good proposal, because there is no possibility hat Krsna will go to Nava-Vrndavana." Then Madhavi left to bring Nava-vrnda.

Before she left for her new quarters Satyabhama looked at Rukmini and thought, in the mood of Sri Radha, “Who is this lady? She looks just like my sister Candravali''

## Radharani Stays in Nava-vrndavana in the Form of Satyabhama

When Nava-vrnda had arrived. Rukmini said to her. “Nava-vrnda! This girl’s name is Satyabhama. She is my sakhi. She is like my sister. Please lake care of her. Take her to Vasami Chatuhsala and maintain her there."

When Satyabhama saw Nava-vrnda her eyes were drawn to Nava-vrnda's yellow sari. It was the same color as Krsna’s yellow dhoti, so when Satyabhama saw that yellow color asta-sattvika transformations immediately appeared in her body. She was trying her best to conceal the ecstatic love that was awakening in her heart, but she was not able to control herself. Bewildered by her roiling emotions, she began to cry.

Rukmini asked Nava-vrnda, "What has happened to Satyabhama? It seems this reaction was caused by her seeing your yellow cloth."

Nava-vrnda asked Satyabhama, "Why did your hair stand on end like the petals of a kadamba flower when you saw my yellow sari? Why did tears come from your eyes when they saw this color?”

Satyabhama did not want to reveal the cause of her ecstatic symptoms. She replied, “O Nava-vrnde! You look just like my sister, so naturally I feel very happy to see you. That’s why these tears of happiness are flowing from my eyes.”

Rukmini took hold of Satyabhama’s hand and said to her, "O my sister, Satyabhama! Please go to with Nava-vrnda to Vasanti Chatuhshala, my favorite courtyard garden filled with flowering vasanti vines. My puspaharini-sakhi named Bakula is staying there. She will take care of you.”

Just as Satyabhama was about to leave, she lost herself in the mood of Srimati Radharani and said to Rukmini, “O Devi! Please, sometime remember Me, your unfortunate sister, Radha.”

When Rukmini heard the name “Radha,” She asked, “O sakhi! What did you sav?”

But Satyabhama had regained control of herself and could understand her mistake. She tried to fix the situation, ‘T said please remember your unfortunate aradhika, your worshiper.” Then Satyabhama left with Nava-vrnda.

## Visakha Disappears from Vrndavana and Appears in Dvaraka

As we have already described,Visakha too swam out into the deep waters of the Yamuna along with Radharani.

Just as she was about to drown Visakha heard the voice of the sun god say, “O my daughter! Radha can not live without Krsna anymore. Now bring Herto me.” Thus Visakha and Sri Radha left the earthly planet and went to the sun planet. They both stayed there for some time, as we have already heard. Radharani’s birth as Satyabhama and Her journey to Dvaraka have already been described as well. Visakha on the other hand stayed on the sun planet for some time and then returned to the earthly planet. No one knew that she had left or where she had gone.

Rukmini had once requested Krsna, through her friend Madhavi, for a large quantity of very special lotus flowers called sura-saugandhika. Krsna had gone to the Khandava forest with Arjuna to fetch these sura-saugandhika flowers. While they were in that forest. Arjuna was searching for deer. All of a sudden the two friends saw two birds being attacked by two eagles. Just as these two birds were about to be killed by their predators, one bird spoke to the other. The first bird, a white swan. said. “Alas! Dear parrot! I will no longer be able to eat mrunal, moon-like lotus roots, in the kanda-vajna of Sri Radha.”

To this the parrot said, “O white swan! I will no longer be able to see the vakra-angaraka fruit, like so many red planets, in Radha's phala-sastra sacrifice.”

When Krsna heard what these two birds had said He decided to save them from their fate.

Then the two friends, now joined by the two birds, continued wandering in the forest. A few minutes later they crossed paths with a peaceful old lady. Krsna asked her who she was, and she replied. "I am a savari (tribal lady). I look after these two birds. They stay by this lake, which is filled with sura-saugandhika flowers. The lake was created for these birds by the performance of sacrifice and austerity. There is also a tree on the bank of this lake. That tree is filled with many fruits that taste like nectar.“

Krsna asked the old lady, ‘ Who performed the sacrifice?”

She replied, “The yajna was performed by one tapasvini observing a vow called radhabhista-sadhana. She was residing in the water of this lake to fulfill the desire of Sri Radha. Now her vow has been fulfilled. She has taken up a new vow - to live in forest.”

The words "Sri Radha” excited Krsna, who very eagerly asked the old lady to show Him where the tapasvini was staying. She told Him about a nearby mountain cave, and following her directions Krsna went to find it. There He found one tapasvini wearing tree bark, her body covered with dust and sand, matted hair on her head. She was chanting on japa-mala made of rubies. When the tapasvini saw Krsna she started to cry and exclaimed, “Alas! You have again appeared before my eyes!”

Krsna was very surprised by the sight of her. He asked, “Who are you?”

With a choked voice she replied, “O my Lord! I am your most unfortunate maidservant Visakha.”

When Krsna saw Visakha in that condition. He shed hot tears of sorrow and cold tears of bliss. His yellow cloth was soaked with His tears. He became stunned, motionless. Then for a moment He leaned on Visakha’s shoulder and stood there like a dry tree in an empty heart. Then He gave her new garments to wear and brought her to Dvaraka.”

## Lalita’s Disappearance from Vrndavana and Her Appearance in Dvaraka

We have described how Radha and Visakha drowned in the Yamuna.

As Lalita had watched Radha and Visakha drown in the Yamuna, she cried loudly in separation from her friends. Lalita said, “Alas! My most dear friends, Radha and Visakha, have gone into the water of the Yamuna and will never come out. I should follow' in their footsteps and also give up my life in Kaliya Lake.”

Lalita was just about to jump into the river Yamuna, but just at that time Vrnda and Mukhara happened to arrive. They were also quite upset, searching for Radha, Visakha, and Lalita. Vrnda-devi saw that Lalita was preparing to jump into the river and she shouted. " Oh! Lalite! What are you doing? Oh! Mukhare! I think Lalita is going to drown herself in the river due to lamentation. Let us go save her.” Both of them ran and caught Lalita just before she jumped into the Yamuna. Vrnda said, "Lalita! Radha and Krsna have left Vrndavana. Visakha also has followed in their path . You are now the leader of all the gopis still here. If you too leave, then what will happen to the rest of us? Please consider this and stay here to keep us alive."

Having been prevented from putting an end to her misery, Lalita blamed herself for her bad fortune, thinking it would be a great obstacle for her. She thought to herself. “My dearmost friends have left their bodies. For what reason should I remain here? I should climb to the top of Govardhana Hill and jump off. That is how I will give up mv body." Thinking like this, she told Mukhara, “ Please release me. I must go and speak to Mother Paurnamasi.” Having said this, she took her leave, and then started walking in the direction of Govardhana Hill.

Naturally, Mukhara was very sad that her grand-daughter Radharani had died. She lamented, “O lotus-eyed Radha! When Pundarikakshva hears about Your death, then what will happen to Him?"

Suddenly a compassionate voice in the sky announced, “Devi! The most glorious Radharani, along with her sakhi, has gone to the sun planet. Be patient! There is a festival of transcendental bliss waiting you in the future.” As soon as Vrnda heard this celestial announcement she wanted to relay this good news to Lalita, so Vrnda ran to find her. But she was not fast enough. By that time Lalita had already jumped from the top of Govardhana Hill.

Luckily, by the order of Surya-bhagavan, Jambavan had appeared at Govardhana and caught Lalita before she fell to the ground. He took her to patalapura in the lower planetary system and kept her there, caring for her as if she was his daughter. No one knew that Jambavan had taken Lalita to his home. The Vraja-vasis had heard a voice from the sky proclaim that Lalita, who was more dear to Radharani than Her very own life and soul, had fallen from Govardhana Hill.

When Mukhara heard this terrible news she said, “O Lalita! You tricked me and were thus able to commit suicide.” Then Mukhara said to Vrnda, "I will now drown myself in the Yamuna to save myself from the unbearable suffering of separation from all my dear ones.” Having said this, Mukhara walked sadly toward the Yamuna.

But Surya-bhagavan spoke to her, in another disembodied voice from the sky, “Do not dare to do such a thing. Do not enter the river with a mind to leave your body. Be patient. You will see Radha again, and your reunion will be a great festival of happiness.” This warning convinced both Mukhara and Vrnda to give up the idea of drowning themselves in the Yamuna.

At this same time Krsna was staying in Dvaraka. King Ugrasena was the ruler. As already mentioned, there was also a king named Satrajit who was worshiping the sun god. Being pleased with the king, the sun god had given him a jewel known as Syamantaka. When Krsna heard that Satrajit was in possession of the Syamantaka jewel He decided that such a valuable jewel should instead belong to King Ugrasena. So Krsna sent a messenger to Satrajit to order the king to give the jewel to Ugrasena. But Satrajit refused and instead offered the jewel to his brother, Prasena, who wore the jewel on his neck.

One day Prasena went to the forest to hunt, riding on his horse. He was wearing the Syamantaka jewel on his neck. While in the forest, Prasena was killed by a lion. The lion took the jewel from Prasena. Some time later the lion was killed by Jambavan, the dear devotee of Lord Ramacandra. Jambavan took the jewel and went through a tunnel to his home on Patalapura in the lower planetary system.

When Prasena did not come back from the forest, Satrajit concluded that his brother must have been killed by Krsna. Satrajit thought, “Krsna asked me for the jewel, but I did not give it to Him. He must have killed my brother and taken the jewel.” With this misconception clouding his mind, Satrajit spoke amongthe people in general, spreading the false rumor that Krsna had killed Prasena and taken the Syamantaka jewel.

When Krsna heard that Satrajit was blaspheming Him in this way, He was determined to clear His good name. He went to the forest and found that Prasena had been killed by a lion. By following the lion's footprints He found that the lion had been killed by a bear. And by following the bear’s footprints He reached Patalapuri through the tunnel Jambavan had taken. There Krsna fought with Jambavan and defeated him after a twenty-seven-day battle.

Finally Jambavan recognized Krsna as non-different from his Lord. Rama-candra, and gave up the battle. Remembering his pastimes with Lord Rama, Jambavan told Krsna, "O my Lord! You must remember how we built a bridge across the ocean. Surely You cannot forget these pastimes. I think You have come here to give happiness to Your old servant on the pretext of recovering this jewel.” Then Jambavan asked Krsna to sit on a jeweled throne in a golden palace while he went to fetch the Syamantaka jewel from his treasury.

While Krsna was waiting for Jambavan to return, an elderly lady approached Him and said, “My dear son! If You take this jewel away from here, then Jambavan’s daughter, Jambavan will die in separation. She is very much attached to this jewel; indeed, when her father first brought the jewel here she was completely overwhelmed by its brilliance. She completely lost her peaceful composure. I have seen her sometime press the jewel to her breasts then smell it and hold it on her tear-filled eyes. She often embraces it with her trembling limbs as if the jewel were her dear friend."

Krsna was overwhelmed by the old lady's wonderful description. Out of curiosity He asked her why Jambavati was so attached to the jewel. The old lady burst into tears and, covering her face with the edge of her sari, she said. "Jambavan has a very beautiful pair of Deities called Radhika and Madhava. Every day she speaks to her Deities, describing to Them the sweet, loving relationship They have for each other. She sheds torrent of tears while speaking to the Deities.”

Krsna was filled with wonder to hear this. He wanted to see the Deities, so He asked the old lady what the Deities looked like. She told Him that they were the most handsome and wonderful Deities she had ever seen. She said, “In fact, the male Deity looks exactly like You. When I saw You, that Deity’s smiling face immediately came into my mind. Since I first saw those Deities Their beautiful forms have never gone away from my memory.”

Then the old lady left Krsna and went inside, where she met Jambavati and told her, “My dear child! Out of the two Deities you have, the most handsome black-complexioned male Deity has accepted the form of a living human being. He is sitting there on the throne. Come with me and see Him for yourself.”

So Jambavati went with the old lady to see Krsna. She hid behind a tree from where she looked at Krsna very eagerly. The sight of Krsna drove away Jambavati's shyness and brought her to tears. She threw herself at Krsna's lotus feet. And Krsna, upon seeing Jambavan, could understand that she was none other than Lalita herself. Of course, Jambavaifs Deities were Radha and Krsna, and the jewel was the Syamantaka jewel, which Krsna had personally taken from the head of Sankhacuda. Having now recognized each other, Krsna and Lalita shared recollections of their pastimes with Srimati Radharani in Vrndavana.

In the meantime, Jambavan came back from his treasury with the jewel and came to know what had happened. In order to explain how Lalita had become his daughter, Jambavan, he revealed to Krsna that he had long been a close friend of Sugriva, who was himself a close friend of the sun god. Therefore the sun god was always very kind to Jambavan. By the order of the sun god Jambavan once went to visit Govardhana Hill, where he saw a girl jump from the mountaintop. That girl was Lalita, whom he had rescued before she fell to the ground. Since that time Lalita had been living as Jambavan’s daughter, Jambavati. Having now explained all of this, Jambavan offered Jambavati and the Syamantaka jewel to Krsna.

Krsna wanted to marry Jambavan, but He could not do so at that time. When Krsna had married Rukmini her father Bhismaka had made Krsna promise that He would not marry another wife without first getting permission from Ruk-mini. So, instead of taking Jambavati to Dvaraka, Krsna kept her in a cave on Raivata Mountain and then continued on to Dvaraka with the Syamantaka jewel. Once there He explained to everyone the history of the jewel and how it was recovered. Then He gave the jewel to one of His servants to return it to King Satrajit.

## Four Sakhis of Candravali and 16,100 Gopis Appear in Dvaraka

The four sakhis of Candravali were taken by different kings to their houses.

Padma was the daughter of King Nagnajit. She was received by the king and given the name Nagnajiti.

Syamala was taken by the king of Maddrass, who gave her the name Lakshmana.

Saivya was accepted as the daughter of King Saivya, who gave her the name Mitravinda.

Bhadra was the daughter of the king of Kekaya.

King Nagnajit, King Madreswar, King Kekaya, and King Saivya came to know from Narada Muni that Radha and Visakha had left their bodies by drowning in the Yamuna and that Lalita had left her body by jumping from Govardhana Hill. After hearing this horrible news they all went to Vrndavana and humbly approached the husbands of Candravali’s four sakhis and brought them back to their respective homes.

There were 16,100 gopis in Vrndavana who worshiped Kamakhya-devi on the bank of the Yamuna to get Krsna as their husband. Some time later all of them were forcefully kidnapped by Narakasura. Krsna eventually killed Narakasura and married all 16,100 gopis after releasing them from their kidnapper.

## Appearance of Nava-vrnda

Paurnamasi-devi, being very much aggrieved, returned to Gokula. She had gone to Kundan gram, saying that she would return to Sandipani Muni's asrama. “Now that Radharani has returned to the earth planet and is staying in Nava-vrndavana as Satyabhama,” Paurnamasi thought, “who will serve Her there?”

There was at that lime a female student of Visvakarma who was very expert in the science of gardening. She was so talented in fact that she could flowers bloom in every season. Her name was Nava-vrnda. Surya’s wife, Samjna revealed to Nava-vrnda everything there was to know about Radharani. She said, “The gopis of Vrndavana are very dear to Krsna, and Sri Radha is the best among those gopis. Now She is staying in Dvaraka. You should go there and serve Her. This will bring all auspiciousness to you.”

Hearing this, Nava-vrnda decided to go serve Radharani in Dvaraka. where she stayed following the orders of Rukmini.

## Discussion Between Nava-vrnda and the Sarat Season Personified Regarding the Arrival in Dvaraka of 16,100 Gopis and the Four Sakhis of Candravali

Nava-vrnda, on the order of Sri Krsna, had gone to meet with Visvakarma and request that he build 16.100 houses on Raivataka Mountain for the 16,100 queens of Krsna, who were all at one time gopis of Vrndavana. Narakasura, the son of Mother Earth, had kidnapped 16,100 gopis from Vrndavana and kept them in a cave. All of these gopis were preyasis (beloved) of Krsna. When Krsna left for Dvaraka these gopis were worshiping Kamakhya-devi to gel her blessing that they might go to Dvaraka and live with Krsna. Narakasura became attracted by the beauty of these girls and so kidnapped them to make them his wives. Narakasura, like these gopis, was also a devotee of Kamakhya-devi. But he hadmisled her by saying that the 16,100 gopa-kumaris were all princesses of kingdoms outside of Vraja and that he wanted to marry them. But when Kamakhya-devi came to know the truth about the gopa-kumaris she became very angry and told Indra, the king of heaven, about the demon’s ruse. As a result, Indra went to Dvaraka and requested Krsna to kill Narakasura. And so Krsna killed Narakasura and freed the 16,100 gopis who had been suffering in separation from Him. Then Krsna called for Visvakarma to build 16,100 palaces on Raivataka Mountain to accommodate these gopis. Thus the sarat season came to know from Nava-vrnda that 16.100 gopis were now in Dvaraka.

## Satyabhama Does Not Accept Rajendra, King of Dvaraka, as Krsna of Vrndavana

Srimati Radharani, in the form of Satyabhama, was staying in Nava-vrndavana near Krsna's palace. After bringing Jambavati to Raivataka Mountain, Krsna had returned to Dvaraka. One day, Bakula-sakhi told Satyabhama. “Our most beautiful king rules over all three worlds. If you desire, then I will tell him even-thing about you, without Rukmini’s knowledge. Even if Rukmini finds out and decides that I am her enemy, still I don’t mind telling Krsna about you."

Satyabhama, in the mood of Sri Radha, said. "Dvarakanatha may be the most beautiful king, who rules over the three worlds. Let Him do so. I have no interest in Him. Please forget what you have suggested. Do not try to ignite the fire of my anger against you. You may tell me millions of things about the Lord of Dvaraka, and still you will not be able to divert my mind from the lotus feel of Vrajendranandana, the prince of Vraja."

Bakula said, “Please speak to Nava-vrnda. She will tell you the truth. She will tell you what is most beneficial for you."

In the meantime, Nava-vrnda had been meeting with Queen Rukmini, After their meeting she returned to Satyabhama, with some ornaments in hand.Satyabhama asked about the ornaments, and Nava-vrnda told her, “Saci-devi, the wife of Indra, has sent some celestial flower garlands, necklaces, silken garments, and other gifts for Rukmini. And Rukmini has sent some of those things for you.”

Satyabhama said, “Gifts such as these are like fuel on the fire of my suffering. What use do I have for such things?”

Nava-vrnda said, “O sakhi! All these things will be very useful for worshiping Surya.”

Satyabhama said in the mood of Sri Radha, “Surya-deva told me, ‘In the middle of the ocean, Visvakarma has created Nava-vrndavana. Go there and enjoy with your husband, the Lord of your life.’ But how is this possible? Sri Hari is now staying in Mathura. I am living here in Dvaraka. I am completely confined, dependent upon others. I feel that meeting with my dear husband will be completely impossible.”

Nava-vrnda laughed and said, “O misguided one! Do you think Vrajendranandana is not here? You think He is now in Mathura?”

Satyabhama became very angry and said, “Stop all this useless talk.”

Nava-vrnda told Satyabhama, “O simple-minded one! That Vrajendra is known here in Dvaraka as Rajendra. He is also called Ramacandra and Upendra. They even call our Rajendra the king of Vraja. I advise you to please give up all doubts and just serve Rajendra, the emperor of Dvaraka.”

Satyabhama said. 'T am not attracted to any other form of Hari. My mind is only attracted to that form with a peacock feather crown on His head, who wears large gunja-mala around His neck, who holds a flute to His lips, a flute that gives great happiness to us ladies.”

Bakula said, “O sakhi! You are much too simple. You are still attached to that cruel Vrajendranandana. How can you still love that hardhearted man?”Satyabhama told Bakula, "O Mugdhe! Please do not talk like this anymore. Syamasundara is completely independent. If He is cruel and shows indifference towards me for thousands of years, then let it be so. I still cannot give up my determination to serve His lotus feet, even for one moment. Krsna is more dear to me than my body, my mind, even my very life.”

Nava-vrnda said to Bakula, “O Bakula! She is too chaste. There is no use in talking to her any longer. Let us keep quiet.”

## Satyabhama’s Dealings with the Deity of Krsna

Looking around at the various features of Nava-vrndavana, Satyabhama said to Bakula, “Sahachari! I see all the creepers who were serving Krsna in Vraja. All these kunjas are very well known to me. I was often roaming in that grove on the bank of the Yamuna just there. Everything I knew in Vraja is here too. But without Gokulapati all these beautiful features are simply causing me pain.”

Nava-vrnda remarked to Bakula. “O Bakule! Just see! She has developed so much pain in separation. Today you should prepare for her a bed of soft lotus petals on the bank of the Kalindi under a kadamba tree."

Satyabhama said, thinking herself to be Radha. ''I am suffering terribly due to separation from my dearest sakhis, like Visakha and Lalita. Surya-deva once pacified me by saying, ‘Go to Nava-vrndavana and stay there happily with your husband.' But although I had placed so much hope in his order, I am hopeless now. My suffering is simply increasing."

Then Nava-vrnda invited Satyabhama to sleep on a bed made of lotus petals. And Satyabhama, lying on the lotus petals, thought, “This is very difficult for me, to live in this Vrndavana. I must find some remedy for my pain.” Then, in the mood of Sri Radha, she spoke to Nava-vrnda, “O Mugdhe! O charming girl! I have become very unhappy, because I cannot perform my daily worship. By the order of Narada Muni, I have long worshiped a Deity whose face is madeall the more beautiful by the flute that He holds next to His mouth. That Deity always looks in a crooked way, wears peacock feathers on His head, and stands with three curves in His body, which is eternally youthful and is the color of a dark monsoon cloud. As I said, it was Narada Muni who told me to worship this particular form.”

Nava-vrnda could understand the mind of Satyabhama. She brought a Deity of Krsna, Visvakarma had made from sapphires to decorate Nava-vrndavana. When Satyabhama saw that Deity of Krsna, with a choked voice she said, “Oh! I have been reunited with the friend of my life inside the kunja-kutira on the bank of the Kalindi. Ah! Now the splendid dark effulgence of this enchanting boy shines before me. Now it is once again worthwhile to live in this body, despite all the pain and suffering I have had to endure.” Satyabhama stepped closer to the altar and spoke to the Deity, ”O my dear friend! You have come to me again. Before You were so slender and affectionate, and I thought You were my own property. But now You are so harsh that I dare not be so proud as to think I belong to You.”

Satyabhama then spoke to Bakula, who was holding her hand, ''O sakhi! This uncommonly cunning boy is no longer telling me jokes. He has become dumb. He has two arms, but He is not embracing me.” She staring at the Deity through crooked eyes and said, “Your heart has become hard because You have been associating with the Kausthuba jewel for so long. I am suffering so much, and You have the power to give me relief. Why do You cheat me instead?”

And to Nava-vrnda, Satyabhama said, “Krsna is wearing a garland of forest flowers, and His skin is as soft as blue lotuses. But why docs He still he kiss this hard, harsh flute. I must take it a wav from Him."

Nava-vrnda thought it would not be good to take the flute away from the Deity, so she decided she must reveal the truth to Satyabhama - that this was not Krsna but Krsna's Deity form. Nava-vrnda said. “O beautiful bewildered girl! Why do you say He is soft as blue lotus flowers. You should know that He is hard as sapphire. If you do not belief me, then touch His broad chest so that you will know how hard it is."

Hearing this from Nava-vrnda, Satyabhama touched the Deity's chest with her soft hand and realized it was not Krsna but His Deity. She realized it was true that she was standing before a Deity made of sapphire, and she was disappointed and upset.

But Bakula look this opportunity to bring the ornaments, clothes, garlands, and so on that had been sent by Rukmini for Satyabhama. Rukmini had received these gifts from Indra's wife. Satyabhama immediately began decorating the Deity, her body trembling and her eyes shedding while she served Krsna in that form.

## Madhavi Meets Satyabhama in Nava-vrndavana

Just at that time Madhavi-devi entered Nava-vrndavana. She had been sent by Rukmini to see how Satyabhama was doing. While entering the forest she saw Krsna’s footprints, with the distinctive marks found on His lotus feet. Anticipating Madhavi's arrival, Nava-vrnda told Radha to leave that place, but Madhavi had already reached that spot. She picked up a blue lotus flower that had fallen from the Deity. Disturbed, she called for Bakula. By this time Satyabhama and Nava-vrnda were leaving that place, but Madhavi saw them and wondered, “Why is Satyabhama here?” So she approached Satyabhama and told her, "O my friend! I have come to pick jasmine flowers."

Satyabhama saw that Madhavi was holding a blue lotus garland Krsna had worn. By smelling the sweet fragrances of the garland. Satyabhama became agitated, her heart and senses bewildered and her body trembling.

Madhavi asked, “O Satyabhama! Please tell me! Why do you tremble just to smell the sweet fragrance of this blue lotus flower garland? The hairs on your body are standing up, and you look very much overwhelmed.”

Instead of answering truthfully, Satyabhama said, “O Madhavi! The sight of that blue lotus garland reminded me of the many snakes I saw today in Kaliya Lake, and so for a moment I was overcome by fear.”Nava-vrnda was happy to hear this answer from Satyabhama. A few moments later Madhavi left to pick jasmine flowers, and the rest of them departed as well.

## Radha and Krsna are Re-united But Mistake Each Other as Illusory

Around that same time Sri Krsna entered Nava-vrndavana in the company of Madhumangala, who noticed a bed of lotus petals on the bank of the Yamuna and pointed it out to Krsna. The sight of it filled Krsna with anguish. He said to Madhumangala, “Just see how these flower petals have become dry due to the heat of separation.”

Then Krsna walked towards Nikunja house, where He saw the Deity of Himself and said, “Just see how nicely Visvakarma has carved this attractive murti of Me in the dress of a forest boy.”

Seeing the murti, Madhumangala said, “Oh! How wonderful! I am now seeing my dear sakha after many days. O Krsna! You are Rajendra, king of Dvaraka. A king is not a suitable friend for a brahmana boy like me.” Madhumangala proceeded towards the Deity and continued, “Just see! Look at this! Some dear girl has worshiped this Deity very lovingly.” Madhumangala was impressed to see sandalwood paste, flower garlands, and silk cloth on the Deity.

Krsna said. "My friend, you are correct. This girl must have been overwhelmed with emotion. You can tell by the careless way she has placed the garland. Just see the cracked sandalwood paste. This shows us that she was shedding tears. And the crooked tilaka proves that her hand was trembling. From all these signs we can understand that this girl must have worshiped the Deity with great love."

At this time they could hear Bakula from a distance saying, “O sakhi! Come this way. Come this way.”

When Krsna heard this, He told Madhumangala, “O My friend! I think the ladies who worship this Deity are coming this way. Let us remove the Deity from here and hide it outside the kunja somewhere. I shall put on the Deity’s dress and stand here on this platform. Thus I will observe the bhava and nistha of the Deity’s worshipers.”

Then Madhumangala took the Deity and hid it somewhere outside the kunja. Krsna took all the Deity’s clothes, ornaments, and other decorations and dressed Himself like the Deity, with a peacock feather in His hair, yellow garments, and a flute in His hand. He stood there in a three-fold form in the same place the Deity had been.

Soon Satyabhama came into the kunja with Nava-vrnda and Bakula. When she saw the Deity - which was actually Krsna Himself - her hair stood on end. She said, “Just see how beautiful this Deity is! It excites my mind, as if I am seeing Madhava personally, face to face.”

And Bakula said to Nava-vrnda, taking hold of her hand, “Nava-vrnde! Just look at the beauty of this Deity! It has become so very beautiful.”

Nava-vrnda smiled and said. “You must have become affected by Satyabhama’s madness. The madness of her love has entered your heart too.”

Krsna, who was standing there very still, posing as the Deity of Himself, was surprised and very happy to see Radha, whom He immediately recognized in Satyabhama. He thought to Himself. "Oh! How very wonderful! Who is this kalpa Latika ( wish fulfilling creeper) girl? She is attracting My heart. I am so enchanted by the sight of her. The effulgence of her lotus face was causing My heart so much pain, and her bluish curly hair was always dancing in My mind’s eye, but today I can see her face to face. She is standing right here in front of Me.”

He kept glancing at Satyabhama as He thought to Himself, “Oh! Is she My dearest Radha, who is more dear to Me than life itself?” Thinking in this way Krsna, disguised as the Deity, started to shed tears. He thought, “Perhaps Visvakarma, understanding My mind, has made an illusory Radha for Nava-vrndava- na in order to give Me pleasure. Otherwise, how could it be possible for Radha to reside in Dvaraka? How could Radha have passed beyond the fortifications of the city?”

Looking at the lotus face of Sri Krsna, Satyabhama said, “Oh! What is this? What kind of madness has my eagerness awakened in me? I am seeing that my Deity is Govinda Himself, standing right here before me!” These thoughts made Satyabhama start to cry. She folded her hands and addressed the Deity thus. “O Pratibimba! O reflection of the Lord! How fares Your bimba, Your original self? Is everything auspicious for lotus-eyed Krsna? Is He well and happy now?”

Krsna said, “O illusory Radha! Krsna is overjoyed to see you, exactly resembling as you do the extraordinary original form of Sri Radha. Not only do I see Radha in you, you are also inquiring about My welfare.”

Satyabhama was struck with wonder to hear the Deity speak. She said, “Nava-vrnda! This Deity is so perfectly crafted that it even speaks sweet words! In the past, whenever I was in Govinda's presence the sweet smell of His body would enter my nose. The aroma of this Deity smells just like that. And just as Govinda’s firm body, the color of a fresh raincloud, would attract my eyes, the cloud-colored hue of this Deity is now attracting my eyes. How is it possible that this Deity has all the features of my Govinda?”

Satyabhama spoke to the Deity, “O Deity of Krsna! This Radha begs You most humbly to please give up this Deity form and accept Your real, conscious, moving form. The eyes of this severely distressed person are greatly suffering due to separation from You. Please give pleasure to these eyes by appearing before them in Your real form."

Krsna thought. "Oh! What a wonderful creation of Visvakarma!” Overwhelmed with ecstasy, saliva started to flow from Krsna’s mouth.

Noticing this, Nava-vrnda said to Satyabhama, ''O sakhi! Please wipe the saliva from the lotus mouth of your most dear Krsna.” Then Satyabhama, gripped by shyness, wiped the lotus mouth of her husband, Krsna.
