Krsna felt the touch of Satyabhama’s hand and could not control Himself. He felt intense happiness.His eyes filled with tears and He began to swoon, leaning on a kadamba tree just behind Him.

Satyabhama was astonished to see how the Deity had taken on the qualities of a conscious person. “What a surprise!" she thought. “The Deity has transformed itself into the real Krsna!" Thinking like this, Satyabhama fainted and her body fell to the ground.

## Rukmini Knew Krsna Met Satyabhama

All of a sudden they could hear the sound of peacocks crying. From this Nava-vrnda could understand that Rukmini-devi.” was on her way to Nava-vrndavana. She told Bakula-devi, ''Rukmini-devi is coming. She may very well come here. Please help me to take Satyabhama away from here.” The two ladies then carried Satyabhama, who was still unconscious, to another part of Nava-vrndavana.

Madhumangala, who had been hiding in the forest nearby while all of these things were happening, came out into the open, and Krsna gave up His disguise. Madhumangala congratulated Krsna on His performance, standing still like a motionless statue. Now that Satyabhama was out of sight, Krsna was missing her very much. Krsna sought out Nava-vrnda and asked her, “Where has that magical statue gone? Can you bring her back again? She filled the universe with wonder. Please, for My sake, go find the statue that Visvakarma created." In response Nava-vrnda told Krsna that Rukmini would shortly arrive.

Sure enough, Rukmini and her sakhis were just coming into the kunja where Krsna was. On the wav there Rukmini had mentioned to Madhavi how much grief she fell having lost her sister Radha. She also told Madhavi that the night before she had heard Krsna calling out the name ''Radha" while He was asleep. Just then Madhavi pointed out to Rukmini that Krsna was just a short distance away in the middle of a forest grove. Rukmini thought that Krsna looked very happy standing there in the forest of Nava-vrndavana. Madhavi thought the same and imagined that He must have met charming Satyabhama.

Rukmini looked more closely at Krsna and noticed that He was wearing the cloth and the ornaments she had given to Satyabhama. Still, she did not want to think that Krsna had met with Satyabhama. She was happy to see Krsna decorated with forest flowers and looking so contented. And yet, in spite of the fact that she was appreciating Krsna’s sweetness, she had to admit that she also felt jealous anger and so could not be completely happy. So, with a smile on her face, she said to Him, “O Deva! It is a source of great joy that today You have met Your new beloved and happily spent time with her. Indeed, I can see that You are trembling with happiness.”

Krsna laughed loudly and said, “O My darling! Do not say ‘new beloved.’ Actually, I have met My old beloved.”

Filled with doubt and apprehension, Rukmini asked, “Who is that ‘old beloved?’”

Krsna replied, “My dearest! Please do not fear or doubt. That old beloved is none other than the creepers of Vrndavana.” Thus Krsna concealed His meeting with Satyabhama.

But Madhavi was not convinced. She had guessed correctly - Satyabhama was that “old beloved.” She sarcastically said to Rukmini, “Your husband has spoken the truth. The creepers must have given this flower garland to Him.”

But Krsna objected, “Madhavi! Do not pollute the heart of pure-hearted Rukmini. Do not make Her unnecessarily suspicious. This garland was made by Madhumangala, the most skilled of artists.”

Rukmini smiled at this explanation and spoke to Madhumangala while pointing at the red garment Krsna was wearing, “O noble Madhumangala! Is this red garment also the product of your artistic skill?”

From this Krsna concluded that His queen must have seen the red cloth before, so He said, “My queen! This cloth was given to Me by the goddess of this forest.”

But Rukmini was unconvinced. She turned to Krsna and said, “O noble husband! Now You should please enjoy freely with the beloved so dear to Your heart. I am going back to my palace.” Having said this, RukminI left that place with her sakhi Madhavi.

It was clear to Krsna that RukminI was angry. He immediately began to consider how He could try to appease her and make her happy once again.

This is how, by the arrangements of Visvakarma, the Supreme Personality of Godhead was bewildered into thinking that Satyabhama was a statue, and Satyabhama was bewildered into thinking the same thing about Krsna. In reality, neither Krsna nor Satyabhama was bewildered. They were simply overwhelmed by their love in separation.

## Krsna Receives Permission from Rukmini to Meet with Satyabhama

With Rukmini gone, Krsna discussed the situation with Madhumangala. How could He make Rukmini happy? In the meantime, Krsnas royal counselor, Uddhava, disclosed Satyabhama’s and Krsna s real identities to one another - Satyabhama was Srimati Radharani; and Rajendra Krsna, the king of Dvaraka, was Vrajendranandana, the darling prince of Vraja. Thus Krsna and Satyabhama, overwhelmed with ecstasy, enjoyed pastimes together.

Not much later Krsna went to meet Rukmini in the inner rooms of her palace. He tried many different things to please Rukmini. Unsuccessful, He said to Rukmini, “O goddess! I want to please you. I can give you anything you like in these three worlds. I am willing to do anything to please you.”

On behalf of the queen, her maidservant Madhavi said, “My Lord! In Your palace are present all the most wonderful things in the three worlds. Still, if You desire to give something to the queen, then listen to me. Once, a swan was flyingin the sky. From the beak of this swan a very wonderful lotus flower fell here. That flower is called surasugandhika. The queen would like as many of these flowers as she needs to string a garland from them.” So, to make Rukmini happy, Krsna went to Khandavaprastha and collected an ample quantity of sura-saugandhika flowers. He then gave those flowers to Madhumangala, who gave them to Madhavi, the sakhi of Rukmini.

On the pretext of some other business, Krsna went to Rukmini's palace. He intended to give some pleasure to Rukmini, and to beg a favor from her. While walking towards the palace Krsna was meditating on Radharani and saying to Himself, "In separation from Radharani, so many tears flow from My eyes. When those tears fall on My chest, which is smeared with sandalwood pulp, the heat of My chest dries up all the tears. Alas! She whose darsana is most rare, even in dreams, will the lime come when that rarest of creatures, My most dear Radharani, sit on My lap?" Soon Krsna could see the door to Rukmini's palace, where she was standing, as if she had been waiting for Him.

When Rukmini saw Sri Krsna, the Lord of her life, approaching from a distance, she told her friend Madhavi, “O sakhi! Just see! My noble husband is coming toward us. Go and quickly bring a nice scented sura saugandhika flower garland to welcome Him."

When Krsna had finally arrived He said to Rukmini, "Gauri! I do not have affection for anyone else except you, not even for a moment. I am fully attached to you, and that attachment is imbued with all awe and reverence.”

In the meantime. Madhavi had returned with the garland of Sura saugandhika flowers and handed it to Rukmini-devi. who then offered the garland to Krsna and said. "Arya Putra! Let this garland remain on Your neck next to the Kausthava mala."

After accepting the garland. Krsna told Rukmini, “O Sundarangi! You reside always on My chest. Nothing except this garland you have offered Me has the right to remain on My chest next to the Kausthava mala.” These words caused Rukmini to pul her head down in shyness. Krsna then very affectionately caughthold of Rukmini's hand and said, “O Kumkum Gauri! I have taken a vow to visit an ascetic lady who is engaged in tapasya and meditation on the Supreme Lord. Please allow Me to keep My promise to her and to go sec her today.”

Rukmini said. “Arya Putra! Kindly do whatever pleases You.”

Hearing this, Krsna thought, “Now I have no reason to fear. I can go to Nava-vrndavana.” And so Lord Sri Krsna returned to Nava-vrndavana.

## Lord Krsna meets Satyabhama in Nava-vrndavana

Lord Sri Krsna accepted His most attractive form of Vana Bihari and entered Nava-vrndavana to meet Satyabhama, whom He considered to be no different from Srimati Radharani.

Nava-vrnda was very happy to see Lord Krsna coming to Nava-vrndavana. She thought, “After a very long time, today I will see the ecstatic mood of reunion between Radha and Madhava.”

Having arrived at the meeting place. Sri Krsna approached Satyabhama, Sri Radha, and said, “O My dear Radha! I have been searching for You for a long time. Finally I have got You, who are just like Laksmi-devi in these three worlds.”

Satyabhama had tears in her eyes. After such a long time she was finally meeting with Krsna. Because her eyes were so filled with tears she was not able to see Krsna clearly. Her body was stunned, practically paralyzed, so she was not able to extend her arms to embrace Krsna. Neither was she able to address the Lord, because her throat was choked up.

Krsna went closer to Satyabhama and said, “My heart is burning in separation from You. Please look upon me with Your lotus eyes. Raise Your face andgive Me a glimpse of Your beauty.” Though the Lord was speaking like this, Satyabhama remained shy.

## Satyabhama and Krsna Enjoy in Nava-vrndavana

Nava-vrnda told Krsna all about Nava Vrndavana, describing it in great detail in order to awaken the mood of Vraja and develop His eagerness to enjoy with Radharani in the form of Satyabhama. The three of them walked around Nava-vrndavana, noticing all the ways it was exactly like Vraja itself. All of Vrndavana’s trees, creepers, and animals and even Govardhana Hill, Nandiswar Hill, and the Yamuna River - everything was there. Krsna and His beloved were thus exchanging loving words and remembering all the wonderful features of Vraja while wandering around Nava-vrndavana.

Krsna said, ''O My dearest Radha! I have never seen such a bounty of malati and madhavi flowers. Please make nice garlands with these flowers and offer them to Me. I will also make a nice crown of flowers for You. I learned the art of making flower crowns when I was staying at the house of my guru. I will make a crown of flowers more beautiful than any crown ever made.”

After walking for some distance Krsna saw His own reflection in a wall made of precious jewels. He was very surprised to see the beauty of His own reflection and thought to Himself, “Who is this residing inside the jewel, attracting My mind and enchanting Me by this abundance of madhurya, sweetness?”

He looked again and realized it was His own reflection. He thought, “Alas! This is My own reflection in this wall made of jewels! Oh! Is My form really so beautiful? What a wonderful form! I have never seen such a beautiful, sweet form. By seeing My own reflection in this jewel-mirror I am developing a desire to experience how Sri Radha feels enjoys by seeing My form.”

At one point during their stroll, the Sura saugandhika flower garland hanging around Krsna’s neck broke off and fell to the ground. Just at that time a swancame and picked up the garland with her beak and flew away. Seeing this, Nava-vrnda said, “O sakhi! Just see! That bird is flying away with Krsna’s garland, and it's flying towards the side of the palace where Rukmini-devi stays.”

Indeed, the bird flew towards Rukmini's quarters, carrying the Surasaugandhika flower garland, and dropped the garland at Rukmini's feel. When she saw the garland, she told Madhavi, “O sakhi! This is the garland I offered to Sri Krsna. That bird must have brought it back from Nava-vrndavana." Respecting the garland as Krsna's prasada, Rukmini pul it on her neck.

But Madhavi soon observed. ''I can smell on this garland not just Krsna’s fragrance but the fragrance of a young girl as well."

To this Rukmini replied, “This garland must have touched the body of Satyabhama. Maybe it was Satyabhama who was wearing this garland. Why should I wear a garland that was on Satyabhama s body? I can understand that Krsna must be in Nava-vrndavana. I will go to find Him, alone.” Having said this, Rukmini dressed herself and departed for Nava-vrndavana.

## Visvakarma’s Gift Boxes for Rukmini and Satyabhama

Some time earlier, Visvakarma had given to Nava-vrnda two boxes full of ornaments. One box was intended for Rukmini, the other for Satyabhama. Nava-vrnda thanked Visvakarma and then brought the two boxes to Rukmini. She told her, "Devi! These two boxes of ornaments have been given by Visvakarma. A note on the first box indicates that it is meant for you. A note on the second box says that it is for Satyabhama.”

Madhavi-devi, a very close friend of Rukmini, was standing there at that time. Because Satyabhama was Visvakarma grand-daughter. Madhavi concluded that he must have sent better quality ornaments for her, so she decided to switch the boxes and adorn Rukmini with the ornaments in second box. “Nava-vrnda!" Madhavi said. '‘Please give me the boxes. I will deliver Satyabhama's box to her and then help Rukmini to adorn herself.”So Nava-vrnda handed both boxes to Madhavi, who made sure that Rukmini received the box intended for Satyabhama.

## Krsna Mistakes Rukmini for Satyabhama

It was for that reason Rukmini left for Nava-vrndavana wearing garments and ornaments meant for Satyabhama. Soon Rukmini could see Krsna not far away. “There is my noble husband." she said. “He is quite near."

But Madhavi corrected her. “That is not your husband,” she said. “That is a reflection of your husband in the sapphires on that wall." It was true. Rukmini was looking at a very wonderful reflection of her husband, who was picking flowers a short distance away. Rukmini decided that she would approach Him alone.

When Krsna saw Rukmini He thought that she was Satyabhama , as she was indeed dressed like Satyabhama . Krsna thought, “How has Radha(Satyabhama), the queen of My life, come here?” Then He said, “O girl! You alone are My beloved. In your heart there should not be any doubt about it. I vow before all My ciders that I speak truth. O My beloved! Why have You come here from so far away?” Krsna glanced at Rukmini, thinking her to be Satyabhama. and the hair on His body stood on end. Rukmini was very happy to find Krsna so affectionate toward her. And she wanted to observe Krsna's real feelings, so she remained silent.

Meanwhile, Nava-vrnda was hiding behind a thick cluster of vines nearby and observing the exchange. She could understood clearly that this girl was not Satyabhama , but Rukmini dressed like Satyabhama . She thought, “If Krsna mistakes Rukmini for Satyabhama and then acts accordingly, there will be a big problem.” So Nava-vrnda wrote a warning to Krsna on a ketaki petal and sent it to Him through one parrot. Very diligently that bird flew Nava-vrnda's warning directly to Krsna.

Krsna understood everything from the letter. Inwardly He thanked Nava-vrnda for saving Him from the awkward position He almost had to face.

Rukmini stepped closer to Krsna, who said to her, "Devi! Why do you now look so indifferent? You do not look so blissful any more. Today your opulence cools Me and gives pleasure to My whole body. My body is eagerly waiting to gel your association. Please accept Me as your most dear one. O My darling! Please do not doubt Me. In the name of My elders I tell you that you only are My most dear one.”

While saying this, Krsna moved closer and closer to Rukmini. Because she was dressed like Satyabhama , Rukmini looking to Krsna so different that He thought he may have made a mistake in deciding she was Rukmini. He thought, “She is not Rukmini. She must be some other girl. Yes! Now I can sec it - she must be Satyabhama, the grand-daughter of Visvakarma." And because Rukmini could read Krsna's mind she could easily tell that He was confused. So she showed Krsna the surasaugandhika garland around her neck.

Krsna thought, “Alas! That swan gave My garland to Rukmini and has thus created a big problem."

“This is very strange!" He said. “I lost this Surasaugndhika garland in the waves of the Yamuna. How has the grand-daughter of Visvakarma come to receive this garland? Let me go back to the palace. I must inform Rukmini so that she will not accuse Me of any offense." Then Krsna left for Rukmini's palace.

“What does all this mean?" Madhavi asked Rukmini.

Rukmini replied, “These are the symptoms of intense, spontaneous, and sincere love."

And Madhavi observed, “This lover is very cunning. It is not possible to understand Krsna’s behavior, His very mysterious behavior. Let us go find Satyabhama.”

## Rukmini Meets Satyabhama to Find Out if She Has Been Associating with Sri Krsna

Thus both girls, Rukmini and Madhavi, went to see Satyabhama. When Rukmini found Satyabhama she was very much pained to see that Satyabhama was looking very blissful and decided that Satyabhama must have been closely associating with Krsna. Rukmini thought, “The inner blissful feeling of her heart is manifesting itself through her blissful face and the attractive movements of her eyes.”

And Satyabhama thought. “Why has Rukmini come here today”

With a smile, Rukmini told Satyabhama. “O sakhi! Please tell the truth. Is it possible that, due to the forceful embraces of Lord Hari, the Kaustva gem has taken up a very intimate place between you and Hari? Without telling me, you have become affectionate toward my husband."

But Satyabhama protested, “O Devi! I am under your shelter. Please do not fail to give protection to one who is under your shelter. Clearly you are joking with me now. Is this proper for an Isvari? I am under the shelter of my friends. They have given me a place in your house. And the Lord of your house is a restless rake. He has been known to destroy the chastity of pure, young girls. I cannot survive anymore in this situation. O Devi! Today I have no other shelter but your mercy, which is like a boat that will take me beyond this danger.”

This made Rukmini think that what she had said was correct. Rukmini asked, "O sakhi! Please tell me - what is your desire?"

Satyabhama said, "Please protect me. I have not yet finished the vow of austerity I made in connection with the Syamantaka jewel.

Rukmini said, “O sakhi! Please believe me. Believe what I say. Krsna will not be able to cheat me again, especially not in this diplomatic way, because my very talented friend Madhavi is always by my side.”

Madhavi told Satyabhama, “O Sundari! Visvakarma has sent a box of clothes and ornaments for you. I will give it to you very soon.” Rukmini added. “O sakhi! Go to Madhavi-mandapa, the pavilion of Madhavi vines, and wait there. I am going to return home with my sakhi Madhavi,” Thus they all departed.

## Rukmini Sees Krsna with Satyabhama in Nava-vrndavana

At one time Rukmini and Madhavi entered Nava Vrndavana. Krsna had taken permission from Rukmini to go to Satyaloka at Lord Brahmas invitation. Rukmini was feeling separation from Krsna, thinking He had gone to the heavenly planets.

Rukmini told Madhavi, ''O sakhi! I have come to Nava-vrndavana being very much disturbed due to separation from Krsna. Because I have no other shelter than Krsna’s indranilamani form, all I can do now is gaze at the blue sapphire Deity of Krsna. I have no other recourse.” Rukmini was missing Krsna so dearly that she could reduce her pain only by looking at the Deity of Krsna made of sapphires. Unfortunately, Madhavi had to tell her friend about a rumor she had heard - Krsna had left His palace in Dvaraka to slay in Nava-vrndavana. It was true that Lord Krsna had been careful to start His journey at an auspicious moment, but He had not left the planet for Satyaloka.

“He must be here somewhere,” Rukmini said. ''O sakhi! It must be true. Krsna must be somewhere nearby. I can smell the enchanting fragrance of His body,”

At this same time, on the bank of the Yamuna in Nava-vrndavana, Krsna and Satyabhama were approaching the edge of a beautiful and secluded forest grove. Krsna invited Satyabhama to step inside the grove and lake rest for some lime. Just as she was accepting Krsna's offer, Satyabhama could see Rukmini and Madhavi some distance away, walking towards them. Immediately Satyabhama put her head down and stepped behind a mango tree to hide. Nava-vrnda, whowas also there with Krsna and His consort, had not yet seen Rukmini. Naturally, she wondered why Satyabhama was hiding behind a mango tree.

Rukmini, meanwhile, could see Krsna from afar. She told Madhavi, “O sakhi! Just see! On the edge of that grove - Krsna is standing there.” Krsna had not yet seen Rukmini approaching. His mind was completely absorbed in Satyabhama, whom He was thinking of as Sri Radha; He was eagerly anticipating His reunion with Radharani in this forest grove on the bank of the Yamuna. “Now I can enjoy with Radha without fear,” He said quietly to Himself, “because Rukmini, the daughter of the king of Vidarbha, is far away in her palace in Dvaraka.”

Rukmini, who was coming closer to her beloved husband, said to Madhavi, “Krsna must have seen me, because he clearly just said, ‘Rukmini, the daughter of the king of Vidarbha.'”

Madhavi replied, “O Devi! You are hiding behind these vines. How can Krsna see you? Though He must have seen you by now. Obviously He is waiting for you there, very eagerly, at the edge of the forest grove. You should secretly go there alone. Surprise Him. Make Him happy. Enjoy with Krsna.”

So Rukmini stepped forward until she was just in front of Krsna, but He did not recognize who she was. He thought she was Satyabhama , whom He had walk off to hide behind a mango tree. With great joy Krsna exclaimed. “What a surprise! You were hiding behind that mango tree. How is it that you are now coming out from behind these vines?”

Nava-vrnda explained. "My Lord! Queen Rukmini is now standing in front of You.”

Rukmini was surprised to see Nava-vrnda there. Krsna said, “Nava-vrnda! Not only is she dressed like Her. but she is also silent and grave, just like Her.” Rukmini was very suspicious to hear Krsna say this.

Sri Krsna looked at Nava-vrnda and said, “ Why does Satyabhama...?” But before He could finish the sentence Nava-vrnda winked her eyes to indicate that this was not Satyabhama.

But it was too late. Rukmini understood, and she felt very sad. She thought, “Now I understand the depth of His love.” Rukmini called her friend Madhavi close to her side.

Krsna thought, “Alas! This is Rukmini. I must try to fix what I have done.” Krsna said to His queen, “O most chaste lady! Why are you not happy? I cannot stand to see you so unhappy. What is the cause of your unhappiness? Are you displeased with me? I am greatly distressed because I do not understand the cause of your unhappiness.”

Krsna was very worried that Rukmini might see Satyabhama hiding behind the mango tree. To help conceal her presence there, Krsna silently requested the mango tree, “O Balamrotaru, young mango tree! Please increase your size so that Satyabhama can better hide behind you and Rukmini cannot see her.”

But Madhavi had already seen Satyabhama behind the tree. She told Rukmini, “Raja Kanye! Please look behind that mango tree and behold your second self.” When Rukmini saw Satyabhama standing there, her head fell in disappointment.

Krsna thought, “The mango tree did not help me. Now I must take help from duplicity.” Thinking like this, He told Rukmini, “Devi! Please raise your head. Let the moon in the sky feel shame to see your most beautiful face. O My dearest darling! By seeing the sadness in your face I too am feeling very sad and worried, because you are more dear to Me than life.”

But Madhavi spoke sarcastically to Krsna, “O Lord! Do not speak to her with such sweet and loving words. She is not Satyabhama.”

To which Krsna replied, “Thank you, Madhavi. Well done! You have removed fear from My heart. This Rukmini is just an illusion created by the magi-cian Nava-vrnda. The real girl is behind this mango tree.” Then Krsna stepped closer to the mango tree and said. “O Rukmini, daughter of the king of Kundina! Out of great kindness you have left the palace and come here of your own accord. Why have you so suddenly, and for no reason, become angry with Me?” But, again, Nava-vrnda corrected Krsna, saying that the girl behind the mango tree was not Rukmini. Rukmini was standing next to Madhavi.

Krsna, bewildered, asked Nava-vrnda , "But I saw a girl behind this mango tree. Is she an illusion?”

“She is not an illusion,” Nava-vrnda replied. “She is Satyabhama, a friend of Queen Rukmini.”

Krsna said, “ How kind is Queen Rukmini! She is so kind that she has given her beautiful form to her friends. I am very much bewildered by this type of kindness.”

At this point Satyabhama decided it was no longer appropriate for her to be present there, so she left with Nava-vrnda.

And Krsna, with folded hands, said to Rukmini. “O Devi! You are more dear to Me than My very life. Kindly be pleased upon Me today.”

Madhavi again replied sarcastically, “O Lord! There is no need to speak to her like this. She is not Satyabhama.”

But Krsna cleverly interpreted the meaning of Satyabhama, considering satya to mean "nature" and bhama to mean "angry." Krsna said to Madhavi, O sakhi! You have spoken the truth. This girl is free from anger: indeed, there is not a trace of anger in her. "

Rukmini said, "O Lord! My heart breaks to see You in this awkward position. Your reluctance makes me sad. Please be happy. I am going back to the inner rooms of the palace. You please enjoy Satyabhama s company without fear.” Having said this, Rukmini left for the palace with Madhavi.

Krsna thought, “Rukmini has gone back to the palace. I should also leave this place.”

## Rukmini Takes Satyabhama to the Inner Sanctuary of Her Palace

On another day, Rukmini-devi went to Nava-vrndavana to meet Satyabhama only to find signs that Satyabhama had been intimately associating with Krsna. She decided it would not be right to keep Satyabhama in Nava-vrndavana any longer. She told Satyabhama. “O my dear sister! It is regrettable that a parrot has scratched your skin like this. It appears that it has scratched your breasts with its beak, mistaking them to be pomegranates. Apparently it has also scratched your lips, thinking them to be bimba fruit. And peacocks appear to have scratched at your jewel necklace, thinking it to be a serpent. The thought of you staying in this forest gives great pain to my heart. You should leave this place and come stay with me in my palace.” This is how Satyabhama came to stay with Rukmini in her palace in Dvaraka.

One day Madhumangala came to sec Krsna, holding a parrot on top of a golden stick. Sri Krsna said, ”My love for Satyabhama has become inflamed and is now burning very brightly. But she is confined inside the palace of Rukmini-devi. Just like a covered flame cannot remove the darkness of a place, because Satyabhama s love is confined to the palace of Rukmini she cannot remove My sadness. In order to see her I have been going to the roof of My palace, and yet I have not succeeded in seeing her."

At that lime Nava-vrnda arrived, and Krsna eagerly said to her, “Nava-vrnde! I am so very eager to see Radharani. whose limbs are splendid, beautiful, and fragrant. In this realm she is known as Satyabhama, the daughter of Satrajit, and her checks are illuminated by the effulgence of Ratna Kundala, the earrings hanging from her ears.”

Nava-vrnda said, ”O my Lord! It is impossible for You to see her. You have cheated Rukmini-devi in so many ways, and so she is now wary of Your tricks. She is very careful not to be cheated again. But she has deep affection for Satyabhama, whom she brought from Nava-vrndavana and is now keeping in the palace, always by her side like her own shadow.”

Madhumangala said, “This is true. But Krsna is desperate. Even if I try to stop Him, He climbs up to the roof to try to see Satyabhama.”

Just at this time Pingala, one of the sakhis of Satyabhama, arrived at Krsna's palace. King Satrajit had sent her to Dvaraka with the Syamantaka jewel. Now Pingala-sakhi was delivering the jewel to Krsna, with the message that it had been sent by Satrajit for Satyabhama. Krsna took the jewel from Pingala and placed it over His heart with great joy. Krsna said, ''Now I have received the association of Pingala, the dear friend of Satyabhama, by which I have found a way to associate with Satyabhama herself.”

Madhumangala asked, “O my friend! By getting the association of Satyabhama's friend, how will You get the association of Satyabhama. How is that possible?”

Krsna replied, “Just listen! I will dress as a sakhi. I will go along with Pingala to meet RukminI, carrying the Syamantaka jewel. I will worship Queen Rukmini. And then with Rukmini's permission I will go to meet with Satyabhama during night and thus enjoy with her.”

Nava-vrnda said, “Yes. This is a wonderful idea. It will be very difficult for RukminI to see through this trick.”

Krsna said. “Nava-vrnde! It is already evening and getting dark. Go to Rukmini's palace. We will join You after I put on My disguise.” Saying this, Krsna left that place along with Madhumangala and Pingala-sakhi.

Meanwhile. Nava-vrnda went to the inner sanctuary of Rukmini's palace and saw the queen sitting there along with her associates. Satyabhama was sitting at Rukmini's right side. RukminI jokingly said to Satyabhama, “O sakhi! I have brought you here into my palace, and I am treating you with friendship and great respect. Why are you always unhappy? Even in the comfort of my palace you are always thinking of Vanamali, just like a doe always remembers the happiness of the wild flower gardens of the forest.”

Satyabhama laughed and said, “O Devi! In the shelter of your palace, which is source of all happiness, why should I long for wildflowers, vanamala?

With a parrot in her hand, Nava-vrnda addressed Rukmini, “O Devi! This is the king of parrots about which you have heard. He has been brought from the country known as Kamarupa.” Rukmini ordered Kanchuki feed the parrot pomegranates, so Kanchuki left that place carrying the parrot with her.

Then Krsna arrived, dressed as a lady, with Pingala and Madhumangala behind Him. Madhumangala said to Rukmini, “O Devi! King Satrajit has sent these two girls to deliver the Syamantaka jewel to Satyabhama.”

Seeing Sri Krsna dressed as a lady, Rukmini thought, “Oh! This girl is so very beautiful!”

She asked, “Who is this beautiful girl with the bright blue complexion? This girl’s splendid hue fills the entranceway and makes it look like it’s made of sapphires.”

Nava-vrnda replied, “O Devi! This lady is a friend of Satyabhama’s. Her name is Rathangi. ”

Satyabhama smiled, having recognized Sri Krsna standing there dressed like a girl. And Madhavi asked Madhumangala, “Why is this girl covering Her face with Her veil? She is acting so shy, like a newly married lady, even though she is safe here inside the ladies' quarters of the palace.”

Pingala said, “O sakhi! This is Her nature. She is always very shy and keeps Herself covered.”

Nava-vrnda told Rukmini, "O Devi! Rathangi has something to say, but She is feeling too shy to speak in front of you. Please send Her with Satyabhama to Swarna-Mandira, the golden temple. She is very much eager to associate with Her dear friend Satyabhama.”

Rukmini said. “Dear Salve! Please go to Swarna-Mandira and welcome your friend Rathangi with a warm embrace."

Satyabhama smiled and said, “I will do whatever you order me." Then Satyabhama left for Swarna-Mandira with Rathangi, Pingala, and Nava-vrnda.

## Krsna Caught by Rukmini

After Satyabhama left with her friends, Rukmini said to Madhavi, “My sister Satyabhama has the jewel her father has sent, a jewel as brilliant as the sun.” Just then Nava-vrnda's parrot flew into the room and chanted a string of verses that revealed to Rukmini the details of Krsna’s plan to sneak into the palace and meet with Satyabhama by disguising Himself as a beautiful girl. Rikmini excitedly said to Madhavi, “O sakhi! Did you hear what this bird has just said?”

Madhavi replied, “Not only have I heard but I also now understand Krsna’s ignoble plan.”

Rukmini said, “O Madhavi! If Satyabhama continues to stay in my palace, it will not be good for me. You should somehow or other arrange to send her to my father’s house in Vidarbha.” Madhavi thought this was a good plan. Rukmini continued. “Even though we were very careful. I was deceived by the words of Nava-vrnda and so sent Satyabhama to Swarna mandira. Nonetheless, whatever was supposed to be, what's happened has happened. Now let us go to Swarna Mandira.” Having said this, both of them left the palace.

Meanwhile. Satyabhama. along with Sri Krsna, dressed as Rathangi, and Pingala and Nava-vrnda had arrived at the swarna mandira. There Krsna and Satyabhama were talking with each other, exchanging a variety of loving words. While this was going on, Rukmini quietly arrived and, concealing herself some distance away, overheard the conversation between Satyabhama and Krsna. Rukmini said to Madhavi, “Did you hear what they are saying?”

Madhavi replied, “Yes. I heard everything.”

Then Krsna saw Rukmini approaching. He told Saiyabhama, “Just sec! Rubin mini-devi has arrived.” Everyone stood up reverentially to welcome the queen and her friend.

Rukmini approached Saiyabhama and told her, “Sakhi Satyabhame! I have come here to see the Syamantaka jewel given to you by your father.” Nava-vrnda took the Syamantaka jewel from Krsna and showed it to Rukmini. Beholding the jewel’s beauty, Rukmini said, “I have heard this jewel was created during the churning of the Milk Ocean.”

Madhumangala said, “O Devi! It could be.”

Rukmini continued, “But there is another surprising event that took place. During the churning of the Milk Ocean, after the pot of nectar had appeared, the demons forcefully took that nectar from the hand of Dhanvantari, the doctor of the demigods. Then Sri Krsna accepted the very attractive form of a woman. Her name was Mohini.” Hearing this, Krsna wondered why all of a sudden Rukmini was talking about Mohini-murti. Perhaps she had somehow found out he was there disguised as a lady. Rukmini continued, “It was befitting for that form to be called Mohini, because in that form Sri Krsna bewildered even Yogesvara Mahadeva. We should not be surprised that He is again bewildering us now.”

Hearing the words of Rukmini, all those present there could understand they were in a very difficult position. Rukmini had obviously come to know that it was Sri Krsna who was disguised as Rathangi. But how did she find out? With a smile, Rukmini said to Saiyabhama, “O sakhi! Is there any way we could have darsana of that Mohini-murti?"

Satyabhama looked at Krsna, raising her eyebrows in anger. Krsna thought, “My plan has been exposed! Nothing will come of it now. What use is there in continuing to hide?” Thinking like this, Krsna said to Rukmini. “O Devi! I accepted this feminine form just to test you whether or not you would be able to recognize Me.” Then Krsna abandoned His disguise.

Rukmini reacted by feigning surprise, as if she had not been aware it was Krsna all along, and then reverentially greeted her husband. She said, “Oh! You are my worshipable husband, Sri Krsna,” and then she bowed her head in respect.

Madhumangala said, “My dear friend, Krsna! Rukmini has recognized you by her own intelligence. There is no need for You to continue to deceive her.”

Now that everything had been exposed, Rukmini told Madhavi, “O sakhi! This calls for a great festival. No one should feel sad during the time of a festival. Instead we should drink in this beautiful form of my Lord in the guise of a lady. This form is very rarely seen.”

Satyabhama was thinking to herself, “What a burden. This is what comes from being dependent upon someone.”

Rukmini told Krsna, “O my Lord! I am less intelligent. Due to my eagerness to see the jewel, I have committed an offense at Your lotus feet. Thus I am very unfortunate.”

Krsna replied, “Devi! Chastise Me as much as you like. Your mercy is My only shelter.”

Then Krsna gave up His disguise and put on His regular yellow garments.

## Satyabhama Goes to Kaliya Lake to Commit Suicide

Soon Nava-vrnda's parrot had revealed Rukmini's plan to send Satyabhama to her father’s house in Kundina. When He had heard. Krsna thought to Himself, “That parrot has caused all these troubles."

And Satyabhama sarcastically, though in great distress, said to the parrot, “O suka! Very good. Very good. What a great favor you have done for me. Youhave revealed all my heart’s secrets to Rukmini. So now I will go to Kaliya Lake, which is a great tirtha that fulfills one's desires, and there enter the lake to offer my body as gift to the most venomous serpents.” Thinking like this, Satyabhama left for Kaliya Lake along with Nava-vrnda and Pingala.

## The Vraja-vasis Arrive in Dvaraka

Rukmini spoke to Sri Krsna very politely, “ O my Lord! I would like to speak to You about something. I am feeling like a great offender because I have become an obstacle on Your path to enjoyment. Please kindly allow me to serve You in the house of Nanda Maharaja in Vraja. There I will make You happy.”

As soon as Rukmini had finished speaking a servant arrived and informed them that the king of Vraja, Nanda Maharaja, had just now arrived in Dvaraka with Mothers Yasoda and Paurnamasi, riding on a bullock cart. Just at that moment they were making their way down the main street of Dvaraka. Of course, this news was music to Sri Krsna’s ears. In great ecstasy He said to Madhumangala, “O My friend! Just see! In accordance with the desire of Rukmini-devi, Nanda Maharaja has come here with all his relatives. Let us go out to meet them.”

So both Krsna and Madhumangala left the palace.

Rukmini thought to herself, “The Vraja-vasis have arrived at the perfect time. This is very good.”

Meanwhile, Mother Yasoda had just stepped down from the bullock cart, and Paurnamasi was leading her towards the palace of Rohini-devi.

Hearing this news, Madhavi said to Rukmini. "What great fortune! Mother Rohini has understood your distress. She knows your suffering.”

Rukmini told Madhavi, “Let us go offer obeisance to all our guests.” Saying like this, they both left for Rohini’s palace.

## The Meeting of Krsna and Yasoda

Mother Paurnamasi was taking Mother Yasoda on a tour of Rohini's palace when Krsna arrived with Madhumangala. Immediately Krsna sal down on Yasoda’s lap, absorbed in the mood of childhood and of Vraja. Mother Yasoda shed tears to seeing Krsna, and milk flowed from her breasts due to motherly love. Thus Yasoda bathed Krsna in her tears and the milk from her breasts.

Beholding this scene, Rukmini told Madhavi, "Just sec my worshipable husband sitting on the lap of Mother Yasoda. Let us stay here for some time and watch this pastime of my Lord.”

Out of affection Mother Yasoda was smelling Krsna's head and shedding tears of joy. She said, “O my dear son! You must have forgotten me. It has been so long since You've seen me.”

Lord Krsna replied, “My dear mother! Why are You talking like this? I fecl embarrassed to hear you say such a thing.”

Mukhara, who was standing nearby, said, “O Yasoda-devi! I have heard from you that Krsna is the Lord of all the universes. But seeing Him now in your arms I feel He is just a cowherd, an inhabitant of Vraja. He is the cowherd people's hero.”

Krsna laughed and said. "Beloved Mukhare! You have revealed My heart. This is exactly what I think. But please bless Me that I will again be fortunate.”

Paurnamasi said. "After many days the seed of my good fortune has sprouted. Today I have seen Madhava on the lap of Yasoda.”

Reminded about Vraja, Sri Krsna asked Mother Yasoda, “O Mother! Are all those suka birds, those parrots, who I had carefully maintained still slaying in that kadamba tree? Are they still giving you pleasure?”

Yasoda said, “O Krsna! The special kapila cow, your most dear companion whom You maintained so affectionately by feeding Him pots full of milk, is still crying in separation from You and running here and there all over Vraja. His pitiful cry pains the hearts of all the Vraja-vasis. Indeed, the hearts of the Vraja-vasis repeatedly break into pieces whenever they see his pitiful situation or hear his sad cry.”

Paurnamasi said, “O Krsna! All the Vraja-vasis are feeling intense sadness to see the peacocks who once offered their soft feathers to You, Missing You, they dance whenever they see black clouds in the sky. Thinking those clouds to be You, they feel so much happiness."

Krsna asked, “O Paurnamasi! How are My friends in Vraja? Are they all right?”

Paurnamasi replied, “O Krsna! All Your friends have come to see You. They are, just this very minute, waiting eagerly in Sudharma meeting hall. You should go there to give them Your darsana as soon as possible.”

“Of course," Krsna replied. "I must go there at once.” And so He left.

## The Meeting of Rukmini and Mother Yasoda

On the way. Krsna thought, “I should bring Lalita and Padma to meet Mother Yasoda. They should go there and offer their obeisance to her.”

When Krsna had left. Rukmini thought, “Now Krsna is not with Mother Yasoda. Now I must go and meet her." Thinking like this, she went to Rohini's palace.

With great happiness Paurnamasi introduced Rukmini. the queen of Dvaraka, to Mother Yasoda, the queen of Vraja. Mother Yasoda stood up and in great happiness said to Rukmini, “O my child! It is my great fortune to see you again.”

And then Yasoda embraced Rukmini, who offered her humble obeisances to Mother Yasoda and with tears in her eyes said, “O Mother! You are so very merciful that you have allowed me to touch your lotus feet. What greater fortune could there be than this?”

Yasoda asked, “My dear daughter! Have you forgotten about our home in Gokula?”

Rukmini replied, “Dear Mother! Who is that most sinful person who could forget Gokula, the auspicious place where you live? You are more affectionate to me than millions of mothers. There is no doubt, I will always remember Gokula.”

Seeing Rukmini cry, Mukhara embraced her and said, “O Radha! I have not seen you for such a long time.”

In great pain Yasoda said, “O Mukhare! Why did you utter these two syllables, ra-dha? By uttering this name, you have inflamed the fire of my sorrow.”

At this point Rukmini, who had been Candravali in Vraja, did not yet know that Satyabhama had been Radharani. As soon as Rukmini heard the name of Radha she started to cry, remembering Sri Radha, and said, “O my sister Radha! I have become blind being deprived of Your darsana. I am most unfortunate. I did not recognize You even once. Where did You go after offering Your body in the waters of the Yamuna River?”

Mother Rohini also began to cry upon hearing Radha’s name. With tears in her eyes, she said, “O most beautiful Radha! Where have You gone after leaving me in this ocean of sorrow. I am suffering in Your separation.”

And when Paurnamasi heard Radha's name, she said. “Alas! I have become hard like a thunderbolt; even though I have long been separated from Sri Radha, I still have not died from the pain of separation.”

With some difficulty Rohini brought her emotions under control and then said to Yasoda, “O my dear Yasoda! Rukmini is feeling extremely sorry. She is greatly lamenting. Please give up your own sorrow and pacify her.''

Yasoda embraced Rukmini, saying, ‘'Dear girl! There is no cure for your lamentation. But do not be unhappy.”

## Rukmini Meets Jambavati

At that time, by the order of Sri Krsna. Jambavati - who had previously been Lalita, and who had been staying in Raivataka Mountain - along with Mitravinda - who had been Padma, the dear sakhi of Candravali - came to Dvaraka from different directions. As they were approaching the main gate of Dvaraka, Mitravinda saw Jambavati coming from her left, though could not immediately recognize her as Lalita. Mitravinda thought to herself, “Oh! What a beautiful girl! Who is this? I have not seen anyone as beautiful as her, and yet I feel I've seen her somewhere before." While thinking like this, Mitravinda had gotten closer to Jambavati and said. "O sakhi! By seeing you I remember my dear friend Lalita and I am overcome with love."

When Jambavati heard this, she said with a choked voice, “O sakhi! Are you Padma?"

Then Mitravinda realized that Jambavati was none other than Lalita and she said, “Oh! This is my dear friend Lalita!” Then she caught hold of the hands of Jambavati, who embraced her friend in response while shedding tears of joy.

Jambavati said. “O Padma! How has your dear friend Candravali become separated from you? Where is she now?”

Mitravinda replied, “O sakhi! I am most unfortunate to have been separated from her.”

At this time Kanchuki arrived. She informed them that they were standing just outside the palace of Mother Rohini and invited them to go inside and meet her.

As they were both entering Rohini’s palace, Rohini said to Paurnamasi, “O Bhagavati! Who are these two girls coming toward us? That one looks exactly like Lalita.”

Paurnamasi looked more closely and then very enthusiastically agreed, “Oh! Just see! Here is Lalita, the dear sakhi of Srimati Radharani.” Then the two ladies and their guests ran to meet Lalita, who was now Jambavan.

Jambavati, meanwhile, was very surprised to see Mother Yasoda and all her friends from Gokula. Shedding tears, she fell at the feet of the elder ladies, who also started to cry and then lifted Jambavati from the floor to embrace her.

Rukmini, in the mood of Candravali, recognized Lalita in Jambavan and said, “O sakhi Lalita! Are you still alive?” Then she embraced her.

With great happiness Jambavan replied, “What is this? It is my dear sakhi Candravali. How is it that you are here?” Then she embraced Rukmini and said, ''Oh! I have found cintamani in the ocean of nectar. What it is to see you and the rest of the Vraja-vasis.”

Rukmini said, “Lalite! You are my sister. Now I am satisfied.”

But Jambavan was soon lamenting in the mood of Lalita, saying, “O my dear friend Radha! Now I have seen everyone but You. Sadly, Your darsana is no longer possible for us.” Speaking like this, Jambavati embraced Mukhara and began to cry.

Then Mitravinda, in the mood of Padma, embraced her dear friend Candravali, now in the form of Rukmini, and said, ''O my dear friend! It is my great fortune that I am able to sec you again today.”

Paurnamasi told Jambavati, ‘"Just sec Candravali, it looks like as if she has embraced Padma so deeply that has melted into her breath."

Being very much surprised Jambavati asked Paurnamasi, “O Bhagavan! I have heard that my dear sakhi Candravali is now known as Rukmini. Is it true?"

Paurnamasi said, “What else could it be?"

Jambavan asked, "Then why was it announced that the sun god sent the girl named Satyabhama here? Her sufferings are known to all.”

Paurnamasi said. “My child Rukmini! I have heard about your sadness and anxiety from Rohini, the mother of Balarama. Do not worry any more."

Mother Yasoda said. “O my child! You stand in place of Radha. Why should you be worried in our presence?"

Rukmini said, “The beautiful sight of my dearmost Rad haram, who is more dear to me than my own life breath, does not for a moment enter the pathway of my eyes. Instead, the image of another girl is my constant companion. The sight of that girl fills my heart with unbearable pain. Alas! She now lives with me in my palace.”

## Krsna Saves Satyabhama

Just at that time Bakula arrived there, greatly worried, and told Rukmini, “O Devi! I tried to stop her over and over again, but Satyabhama has entered Kaliya Lake, where many venomous serpents reside."

Krsna overheard this terrible news and immediately ran off toward Kaliya Lake. Everyone else followed after Him.

Meanwhile, there on the bank of Kaliya Lake, Satyabhama was telling her clear friend Pingala, “Alas! Because I am staying here as a guest of Rukmini's I cannot get the happiness of Sri Krsna’s company. Then why should I fear being separated from Krsna? And what is the use of staying here? That's why I shall now give up this body''

Pingala said, “O Princess! Please do not kill yourself by entering Kaliya Lake in the river Yamuna.”

Satyabhama said, “O sakhi! The water of this lake, which is filled with glistening black venomous snakes, gives pleasure to my eyes.” Glistening black snakes are sometimes compared to Krsna's arms, and so Satyabhama was reminded of Krsna. Consequently, she felt her left eye twitch, a clear indication she was experiencing auspicious symptoms. Still, she chastised herself for feeling ecstasy and said, “The spider of my left eye is shaking. That spider is now weaving a web of hope to trap the fly of my life.”

Pingala said, “That indicates all auspiciousness has come very close. Please wait for a moment. Stay alive just a little longer. Do not give up your life.”

But Satyabhama waded out into the water of Kaliya Lake without caring at all for Pingala's request.

Luckily Krsna and Nava-vrnda arrived there just then and saw Satyabhama enter into the water. Krsna immediately waded out into the water behind Satyabhama. Moments before Krsna entered the Yamuna, Nava-vrnda had tied the Syamantaka jewel to His wrist, thinking that it would remove all inauspiciousness.

Satyabhama was already deep into the water. She was thinking, “What terrible fortune! I have entered Kaliya Lake, but the snakes are not biting me. I am such an unfortunate person!” So Satyabhama tried to swim closer to where she thought the snakes might be.

But Krsna was following close behind. He said, “O brave one! Why are you doing this?" Soon Krsna was able to grab Satyabhama from behind by wrapping His arms around her neck.

She did not understand that Krsna had caught hold of her. Instead she thought maybe two serpents had wrapped themselves around her. Filled with pleasure, she thought. “What good fortune! Now I have been caught by two serpents." Satyabhama was getting pleasure from being touched by Krsna, but she mistakenly thought that pleasure was the result of the fulfillment of her desire to be bitten by venomous serpents. She said, “At a suitable time, even harm can be so much favor, because the touch of these snakes is giving me so much pleasure. Today vidhata has shown me favor, and today I have received my desired fruits; I will be killed by snakebite, and all the suffering of my life will be finished forever."

Satyabhama opened her eyes ever so slightly and saw the dazzling Syamantaka jewel on Sri Krsna’s wrist, but she mistook it to be the jewel on the head of a snake. She thought, “How strange this is! Why is this jeweled serpent not biting me? What is this?"

Then Krsna told Satyabhama, “O Sobhanangi! When Laksmi-devi secs your body she is frightened by its boundless sweetness. There is no greater body than your own. Why are you trying to give it up? The eyes of all the inhabitants of the earth will become useless if deprived of seeing your beautiful form."

When Satyabhama heard these words, she looked behind her and saw Krsna holding her neck with His arms. Satyabhama said, “Alas! Alas! For an insignificant creature like me, the Lord of the three worlds who gives pleasure to everyone has taken the trouble of entering Kaliya Lake."

Krsna swam with Satyabhama to the bank of the Yamuna River. There He put the Syamantaka jewel on Satyabhama's wrist and chastised her, saying, “Merciless girl! Being angry, you have bestowed many benedictions upon these venomous snakes. Why don't you offer benedictions to a serpent like Me who passionately loves you? Let us go now to Madhavi's pavilion." Thus Krsna, Satyabhama, and Pingala left that place.

## Meeting with Satyabhama

Mother Yasoda soon arrived with Paurnamasi. With tears in her eyes she said, ''I am such an unfortunate person. Though Kaliya long ago left this lake, he has come back to torment me due to my misfortune.”

Nava-vrnda thought, ''The plan I devised to free Satyabhama from here dependence on Rukmini has now come to fruition.” Nava-vrnda told Yasoda, Rohini, Mukhara, and the other ladies, “O respectable women! Please be pacified. Drive all sadness from your minds. Lord Sri Krsna has rescued Satyabhama and brought her safely to the bank of the Yamuna.” Everyone was very happy to hear this news. Thus they gave up their sadness and became pacified.

Soon thereafter a gatekeeper came and informed everyone that Lord Sankara, riding on his bull, and his wife Parvati had arrived in Dvaraka. Lord Brahma, the spiritual master of all three worlds, also had arrived. These great persons wanted to meet with Sri Krsna. Nava-vrnda said, “Just see! Lord Sankara is happily approaching Lord Mukunda. And Lord Krsna has come out to please Sankara, the husband of Parvati.” Moments later Krsna could be seen approaching from a distance, and everyone was very happy to behold such an auspicious sight.

Paurnamasi asked Nava-vrnda, “Nava-vrnde! Where is your dearmost friend, Satyabhama?”

Nava-vrnda replied. “She is nearby, at Madhavi’s pavillion.”

Then Paurnamasi said to Mukhara, “While Krsna is absent, let us quickly send Satyabhama to Kundina." Mukhara offered to go fetch Satyabhama.

But somehow Satyabhama could hear someone discussing something nearby. She asked Pingala, “O sakhi! Who are they talking about?”

Pingala replied, “The relatives of Rukmini-devi are speaking ill of you.”

Satyabhama said, “Alas! Now death has fled far away from me. I did not die.” Saying like this, she wept, covering her face with her sari.

Mukhara, meanwhile, was now approaching Satyabhama. But she was taken aback by something very surprising. What did she see? Radha!

Mukhara returned to Paurnamasi, who said, “Mukhare! You said you would bring Satyabhama. What happened?"

Mukhara replied, “O Bhagavan! I want to tell you something. But I hesitate to mention it."

Paurnamasi said, "Tell me whatever you have to say. Don’t hesitate."

Mukhara began to cry and then said with a choked voice. "I have seen Radharani."

Paurnamasi chastised Mukhara for this response, saying, “Pralapani! Keep quiet. How could you have such good fortune?"

Yasoda had overheard but was unsure exactly what had been said, so she asked Paurnamasi to repeat what Mukhara had said. Paurnamasi replied, “O Queen of Gokula! She has said something that can't possibly be true."

But Mukhara, meanwhile, was whispering something in Paurnamasi's ear. Paurnamasi replied, incredulously, "O Mugdhe! Now I know what has happened. You've become perturbed by the Syamantaka jewel. It has made you into a fool."

But Mukhara asked Jambavati to go look and confirm her story. Jambavati looked at Paurnamasi to see what she should do. Paurnamasi said, “If you want, let us all go there. I have no objection." Thus Paurnamasi, Jambavan, and Mukhara all left for Madhavi’s pavilion.

Paurnamasi was slightly ahead of Jambavan and Mukhara. She thought she saw Radha's face, but she could not be sure. Still, she was filled with joy. She said, “Even though I cannot be sure what I'm seeing, this most beautiful girl has awakened such compassion and wonder in my heart."

Next Jambavati walked up very close to where Satyabhama was sitting and, with a choked voice, said, “O slender girl! Please do not cry. "

Hearing this, Satyabhama in the mood of Radharani removed the cloth from her face and said, “Could it be? Is this my dearmost friend, Lalita? And is this the most affectionate Bhagavati? Is this noble Mukhara? " While speaking this way Satyabhama fell to the ground. And Jambavati, who was non-different from Lalita-devi, uttered an inarticulate sound of joy. She embraced Satyabhama, holding her on her lap, and then fainted in ecstasy.

Paurnamasi was now convinced. She said, “Oh! Oh! How could it be? Is this my darling Radha? " Then she began to cry loudly.

Mukhara exclaimed, “O my grand-daughter! It is my good fortune that I have got You back after such a long time. ” Having said this, she completely lost control.

Mother Yasoda, meanwhile, had heard all this commotion and came running, along with Rohini, Her voice choked, Yasoda said, “O my child! Are You alive? ” And she kissed Radha's face.

Finally Rukmini arrived, having heard that Radha had been “spotted. ” Her body was trembling as she said, “Who is this? Is it my sister Radha? ” Then she went and embraced Satyabhama, holding onto her neck.

Paurnamasi said. “What strange illusion is this? In this desert there is a river of crystal clear water that can quench the thirst of most thirsty people. ”

Satyabhama then worshiped the feet of all the elderly, respected ladies there. When she had finished she spoke with great eagerness, “Is everything all right with my sister Candravali? ”

Thus Rukmini, who was Candravali herself, embraced Satyabhama and said, “O my sister! I am that wicked and most unfortunate Candravali. ” And then she started to cry piteously.

Finally Satyabhama, her heart one with the heart of Sri Radha, in great ecstasy and with much awe and reverence fell at the feet of Rukmini and said, “Alas! Alas! I have been killed by Vidhata. Wretched fate is mocking me! ”

Just then Krsna arrived. With great pleasure He said, “Alter such a long time, today I am finally feeling Myself to be a resident of Gokula. Because everyone from Vraja has gathered here together I am feeling so much ecstasy. "

Mother Yasoda embraced Krsna and said, “O my dear son! It is a matter of great pleasure that You and my daughter-in-law Radha have escaped a dire fate in the river. "

Nava-vrnda told Yasoda. Queen of Vraja! You should know all those snakes were an illusion. " Everyone laughed when they heard this.

Jambavati asked Satyabhama, “O sakhi Radha! Where is my sakhi Visakha? "

Nava-vrnda told her, “Just see! Your words have brought Visakha, who is now Mitravinda, from her Nirjhar, river. "

Everybody stood up to embrace Visakha, who fell at the feet of all the elderly ladies and then went to embrace Satyabhama, who she loo now saw was non-dif- ferent from Radharani.

Jambavati, Lalita, then approached Mitravinda and said. “O sakhi Visakha! I have been given a chance to sec you again! " Then both girls embraced each other very strongly.

## Krsna is Married to the Vraja-gopis

Later, in confidence. Rukmini asked Paurnamasi, “O Bhagavati! Please invite my husband Sri Krsna to accept the hand of my sister Satyabhama as His wife. This is my desire. "

When Paurnamasi heard that she said, “O child! You are the most simple and pure of all ladies. If you offer Satyabhama s hand to Sri Krsna in marriage, then all the three worlds will derive pleasure from the marriage ceremony, and you will be eternally renowned as a real chaste lady. ’’

Rukmini replied, “O Bhagavati! This is truly my desire. I enthusiastically request that you arrange the marriage ceremony of Satyabhama and Krsna. Please discuss this with Mother Yasoda and then do the needful. ”

As per Rukmini’s desire, Paurnamasi went to Mother Yasoda and told her what Rukmini had. said. Yasoda then called Krsna and told Him, “My dear son! Rukmini has a request. Shall I tell You what it is? ”

Krsna said, “Mother! Please order Me. I must fulfill her desire. ”

So Mother Yasoda explained Rukmini’s request, to which Krsna immediately agreed. Then Krsna went to Rukmini and said, “O Devi! This is a very serious, very difficult job. You may order Me to do something other than this. ”

With the anger of love Rukmini replied. “You are showing Your fear at the right lime. In this situation, it is good for You to be timid. ” Then Rukmini took Satyabhama's hand and chanted a Sanskrit verse, the meaning of which is “She is my sister. You should show your love and affection to her more than to me. ” While uttering this verse Rukmini put Satyabhama’s hand into Sri Krsna’s.

Krsna said. “Devi! How could I now not accept your mercy? ” Having said this, Krsna happily accepted the hand of Satyabhama.

Rukmini had, by giving Krsna permission to accept the hand of Satyabhama, freed Him free from the prohibition on His accepting any other lady as His wife. This news quickly spread all over. By hearing from Jambavan, Vindhya Mountain, Govardhana Hill, and Raivataka Mountain all reached the door of Dvaraka. Vasudeva, surrounded by all the Yadus, also arrived at the marriage arena, holding the hand of Balarama. Rohini, in the company of the ladies of the Yadu dynasty, came to the arena along with her daughter-in-law Revati. Syamala eagerly arrived holding Bhadra's hand in her left hand and Savva's hand in her right. The most expert Garuda also arrived, carrying the 16, 100 Vraja-kumaris who had been kept in Raivataka Mountain.

When Mother Yasoda saw this assembly she said, “What a wonder! I can sec destiny has become very favorable to us in so many ways. "

At that time Nanda Maharaja also reached there, along with Sridama and Subala. Nanda told Paurnamasi, “O Bhagavati! I have become very fortunate because my long cherished desire has been fulfilled today. ” Then Nanda Maharaja embraced his son Krsna.

Both ladies Satyabhama and Rukmini offered respectful obeisance to Nanda Maharaja, who blessed them to have the good fortune to serve Krsna together. Arundhati, the wife of Vasistha Rsi, also arrived there in the company of many chaste ladies. Lopamudra, the wife of Agasti Muni, reached there as well. And Indra. along with Saci, Kuvera and his wife Ruddhi, Yamaraja and his wife Dhu- morna, Varuna and his daughter Gouri, Surya-deva and his wife Samjya, Marina and his wife Siva. Agnideva and his wife Swaha, and Candra and his wife Rohini all came to Dvaraka.

Thus Dvaraka was overwhelmed with Joy. All the arrangements had been made for a grand festival. Kubja made many varieties of scented cosmetics. Sudama made mam flower garlands with great happiness. The tailor made many beautiful garments. The city of Dvaraka became very splendid and glorious, being filled with so many people who were enjoying perfect happiness.

Jambavati told Mitravinda, “Your life is now successful. You have seen the great festival to mark the reunion of Radha and Krsna. ”

Paurnamasi told Yasoda, "O mother of Krsna! Everything is now ready for the marriage ceremony. Bring Radharani and all the other gopis to the marriage arena. Perform the marriage of Sri Krsna first with Radharani and then with all the other gopis. ” By saying ‘‘all the other gopis” she meant Lalita, Visakha, Padma, Saibya, Syamala, and the 16,100 gopa-kanya gopis. According to Paurnamasi's direction. Mother Yasoda performed Krsna's marriage with Radharani and then with all the other gopis. Once the marriage ceremony was complete, Sri Krsna greeted everyone.

## A Secret Revealed

Finally, with great love, Krsna asked Radharani, “O Radha! mistress of My life air! Now I have married You and fulfilled Your desire. Let Me know what else You would like Me to do. "

Radha replied, “Prananatha! Lord of My life! those sakhis who are endowed with natural love towards You have come here to meet You. I have found all my beautiful, charming, and affectionate gopi friends. I have got my sister Rukmini and all her friends. My mother-in-law Yasoda is also present here. I have finally been united with You, which brings Me the greatest happiness. What more is there for Me to obtain? Still, as per Your order, I would like to pray to You for one benediction. There are many of Your devotees, whose minds are fixed at Your lotus feel, living in Mathura and Vrndavana, where they wailing for You. Lord of Gokula! Kindly appear to those devotees as their friend, in Your youthful form, holding a flute in Your hand. "

Krsna immediately agreed, saying, "O My darling! Yes, I will do that. '’

Radharani asked, “How will You accomplish it? "

Krsna stoped for a moment and then looked towards the south. He. saw Gar- gi and Ekanamsa, who had appeared as daughters of Mother Yasoda and who were living in Vindhya Mountain, coming there very fast. They told Radharani, '‘O sakhi Radhe! Do not doubt that You reside in Gokula, nowhere else. I arranged all these events in various ways just to pass the lime. You should believe that Krsna has come here to Gokula. This truth you will be able to sec directly with in your heart. "

Gargi was watching all of this. Actually, she had already heard all about what had happened from her father Garga Muni. She said to herself, “Now the words of my father, which I heard directly from his mouth, have come true and borne fruit. "

Paurnamasi told Candravali, “You are older than your sister Radha, and yet Radha is superior to you. "

When Radharani heard from Ekanamsa that they are all in Goêula. She was very surprised. Gargi told Radharani, “O sakhi! Be pacified! " Radharani believed the words of Ekanamsa and so looked upon Sri Krsna in a crooked way.

Sri Krsna told Radharani, “O My darling! Whatever you have told Me to do, I have done. What more can I do for You? "

Radharani smilingly said, “We will both decorate this land of Gokula in our original transcendental forms, without the knowledge of outsiders. "

Hearing this. Lord Krsna said, “O My darling! I will do whatever You desire. Now let Us go fulfill the desire of Your sister Candravali. Her desire was to serve Krsna while residing at Nanada Maharaja's house. ” So, surrounded by all His family and friends. Lord Krsna departed for Vraja in another form.

## Prema-samputa: Casket of Love

The following narration has been adapted from a book called "Prema-samputa, written by Srila Visvanatha Cakravarti Thakura. For a more in depth presentation of this pastime, we kindly request that the reader consult the original text.

Samputa means box or casket. Very valuable things are often kept inside boxes. This narration deals with just such an instance. The title Prema-samputa refers to a box in which is kept love.

During the maha-sarat-rasa, Sri Krsna suddenly disappeared from the rasa-mandala. The gopis reproached Him for this. Srimati Radharani, meanwhile, was greatly exalted by the events of the rasa dance. And despite Her also being briefly abandoned by Sri Krsna. Her attachment to Krsna was firmly fixed. Following these well known events, the words of Prema-samputa emanated from Srimati Radharani's lotus mouth.

Lord Krsna once appeared before Sri Radha, disguised as a heavenly damsel. Radharani could not recognize Krsna in this stunning feminine form. Radha said to Lalita, "O sakhi! Lalite! Please tell me. Who is this lady? Her face is so beautiful that it rivals the beauty of the lotus flower. She is wearing such a dazzling variety of ornaments. What’s more. My courtyard is bathed in the effulgence of her blue body. So much so that it looks like my whole house is decorated with indranila gems. "

Hearing these words from Sri Radha, Lalita and Visakha approached this feminine form of Sri Krsna and said to her, “O Krsodari! slim-waisted girl! Who are you? Where have you come from? For what purpose have you come here? Please explain everything to us. "

Even after all these questions had been posed, that mysterious lady did not say a word. Surprised by this silence, Radharani wondered to Herself what the cause might he. After a short time. She asked, “O beautiful one! Who are you? The beauty of your body is pulling on my heart, which is very strongly attracted to your beautiful form. Are you one of the heavenly denizens? The sight of you makes Me feel as if you have subsumed the beauty of everything in this universe and are now reflecting that beauty as you stand here in front of Me. "

But even this soliloquy did not inspire that mysterious lady did to speak. She just stood there silently. So, again, Radharani asked her, "O Bhavini! beautiful girl! Surely you have come here by your own will. You should identify yourself immediately and thus remove our anxiety about you might be. Lajyashile! Do not feel shy in from of us. You have no need to fear. Please consider us your very dear friends. "

But, once again, even after hearing Radha’s entreaties, Krsna in this enigmatic feminine form still did not say a thing. Rather, He turned His face to the ground and stood in silence.

And again Radharani tried to communicate with the mysterious visitor, “O Sundari! beautiful girl! I think you must have some kind of pain in your heart. It look like you are suffering. Otherwise you would not behave like this. Padmanane! Without duplicity kindly reveal to us what kind of mental agony you are enduring. Believe Me fully, and make no mistake: I will try My best to remove your distress. If you reveal your heart to someone who is dear to you, then your suffering will surely be alleviated. Allow Me to venture a guess. Have you been rejected by your husband? Are you feeling sad because your husband has made some error? Have you committed some severe offense at his feet? Are you perhaps afraid of jeopardizing your good relationship with your husband because of some mistake? Or is it maybe that, although you have not made any mistake, notorious people have nonetheless complained against you without reason, trying to ruin the good relationship between you and your husband? Is that why you are feeling so much pain? Or maybe your husband is wicked and unforum ate, and because of this you have come to hate him. Or have you become attracted to another man more attractive than your husband? Or have you been mercilessly chastised by your in-laws as I have been chastised by My mother-in-law? Is that why you are feeling sad? ”

Although Radharani had posed so many questions, and so gently, still that lady did not say a thing. But She would not give up. Radha continued, "O Sundari! Do you have co-wives? Has your husband developed greater attachment for one of your ṣ-wives than for yourself? Is he for that reason chastising you unfairly and using harsh words to harass you? Have you been pained to hear such coarse language? But I don't think any of these things are true in your case. I think that there could not possibly be anyone more fortunate than you in this entire universe. No husband could give up a wife like you, who is clearly endow'cd with all good qualities, just to marry some other lady. I do not think you could have co-wives. Candra Mukhi! moon-faced girl! We have heard from Devi Bhagavati that the Lord has one incarnation called Mohini. In that enchanting feminine form the Lord once bewildered even the great Vaisnava known as Mahadeva. Are you that Mohini? Please tell Me. You have come here all of a sudden in this most beautiful form. To bewilder whom? It is indeed true that you once bewildered Mahadeva. But you yourself were not bewildered. But now, if you look upon the unparalleled form of Sri Krsna, you too will be bewildered by the sight of His beauty. This means that your beauty has no comparison. And our Nagarendar Krsna's beauty also has no comparison. That's why, if you see each other, both of you will be instantly attracted to one another and deeply relish that wonderful rasa of romantic love."

When that heavenly lady - who was actually Sri Krsna in the form of a celestial maiden, and whom we shall henceforth refer to by the name Devangana - heard these sweet words from Radharani’s lotus mouth He relished the unique taste of sambhoga-rasa. As a result of this. His hair stood on end and ecstasy coursed through His body. Krsna wanted to hide His bliss from others, so He tried to cover His whole body with the veil He was wearing.

When Radharani saw Devangana cover herself in this way She thought the mysterious lady must be suffering from some disease. "O sakhi! ” She asked. ‘'Are you ill? Are you feeling pain in your body? Is there pain in your chest. your back, or your head? ”

Thinking in this way, Sri Radha ordered Her friend Visakha-devi, “O sakhi! Visakhe! Out of affection My father has sent Me some very special medicinal oil. The bottle is there in My room. Please bring that oil very quickly and massage it on the body of this Devangana. The highest purpose of My oil will be fulfilled if it is used in her service. Indeed, the nature of love is such that if some object is used in the service of a dear friend or loved one, then that object will become perfect. From the very core of My heart, I love this mysterious girl very much. And My father's affection for Me is present in that oil. I will personally use it to massage the body of this Devangana, and thus all her bodily pain will be removed. By massaging that oil on her head, she will be cured from all sorts of diseases. Please, bring some warm water mixed with scented oil. I will bathe her with that water, and she will immediately feel relief. Her lotus face will shine with bliss. Then, when she has recovered from her disease and she feels belter, she will finally speak with us. ”

Then Radha told Her friends. “O sakhis! Just see! I have spoken to this girl so gently. I have offered to massage her with medicinal oil. I have showed her so much affection, and I have done so without any duplicity. Still. I could not elicit a single word from her mouth. She is not telling us about what ails her. She will not even open her mouth. She is simply standing here, her head hanging down due to sadness. I must try a different approach, another kind of treatment. Just as the transcendental medicine given by Dhanvantari cures all disease and gives comfort to the body, in the same way I will give a kind of treatment to this lady, by which the diseases of her body and mind will be cured. By this treatment her physical strength especially will be restored. Please listen to Me while I tell you about the treatment. Now she is unable to speak a word. She has been rendered mute by some sort of serious disease. But if she gets on her breast the touch of our Kunjesvara Sri Krsna, then she will immediately start to speak, even laugh. She will enjoy inconceivable pleasure just by getting the touch of Sri Krsna. By His transcendental touch, words never before heard will come forth from her mouth. Moreover, she will display some special bodily features, which will bring us much amusement.

When Sri Krsna, disguised as Devangana, heard Radharani reveal Her plan. He wanted to smile but somehow or other controlled Himself and managed to refrain from laughing. Krsna in the form of Devangana, who was standing there dejected. His head hanging down, lifted his face slightly, pulled back His veil enough to uncover His lotus mouth, and started to speak in the voice of a lady. When Radharani and all Her sakhis heard in those soft words the sweet voice of Krsna, their hearts were filled with pleasure. They drank the words of Krsna, like cakora birds drink. Sri Krsna in the form of Devangana spoke, in a feminine voice, '‘O Suvadane, Sri Radhe! I am one of the devis from heaven. For what reason have I come to you? Please hear that reason now. I desire to hear you explain something very serious. No one else can fulfill that desire except you. ”

Radharani continued, “O Sundari! You have identified yourself as a heavenly damsel. This must be true. Even before you said so. we fell you must be a denizen of the heavenly planets, because there are no ladies as beautiful as you on this earthly plane. Your beauty is incomparable. Ai first I was not able to really understand who you are. I have asked you so many unreasonable questions. In fact, I have said all those things to you in all innocence - just to have some fun. Please do not take offense. Because you have shown Me so much affection, I have also become quite affectionate towards you. ”

Sri Krsna, in the form of heavenly lady, finally spoke. “O Sri Radha! You are My friend. Why are You feeling so uncomfortable with Me? I have become Your friend as well. Even if I were from the heavenly planets, still I am fully under Your control. I am fully dependent upon You. Please believe Me. By seeing Your love, and Your form and qualities, I have now developed the desire to be Your own maidservant. But I am overcome by great sadness. I will tell You everything, from the core of My heart. Please listen carefully and then remove all doubts from My mind. Until now You have showered so much nectar upon Me through Your sweet words. But the pain in My heart has still not been removed.

“O sakhi! In Vrndavana there is someone who plays the flute. The sound of that flute travels all the way up to heaven. When that sublime vibration enters the ears of the ladies of heaven they become so bewildered by the sound that they completely forget their husbands. In fact, the ladies of heaven are so affected by the flute that they are pained whenever thoughts of their husbands happen to enter their minds. Of course, the player of that flute is Sri Krsna. And the heavenly ladies become so enchanted by that sound that their minds are filled with thoughts of Jagatpati Sri Krsna. The potency of those thoughts is so great that all their desires for material enjoyment quickly disappear from their minds. In some ways the sound of that flute is like nectar mixed with halahala poison. While hearing, one feels pleasure, like she is drinking sweet nectar. But once the sound stops she feels an unbearable pain, having been separated from the source of her happiness. When the ladies of heaven hear the sound of the flute, they become eager, nay disparate to enjoy with Krsna. They faint due to severe fever. Their bodies become very hot, almost like burning charcoal. If at that lime their husbands embrace them, those men feel their wives bodies are hot to the touch. They conclude that their wives must be suffering from severe fever. The flute of Sri Krsna is imbued with such power that whosoever hears that sound immediately develops the desire to go and meet with Him.

“Another name for heaven, Svargaloka, is Tridasalaya. This indicates that in heaven people go through only three stages of life – balya, kaisora, and yauvana. In heaven there is no old age. so there are no old ladies in heaven. Whenever Krsna plays His flute, all the heavenly damsels are afflicted with the same condition. For this reason there is no question of their criticizing or blaspheming one another. Who will chastise whom? All suffer from the same condition. The sound of Sri Krsna's flute has afflicted all the heavenly ladies equally’, It has destroyed their chastity.

“One day, filled with wonder, I thought, ‘Alas! Where is this sound coming from? Who is making this sound? ' Thinking in this way, I set off in the direction from which the sound seemed to be coming. Finally, after a long journey, I reached this earth planet. I arrived here in Vrndavana, and for a few days I stayed near Vamsivata very happily. For the last few days I have been witnessing Your pastimes with Sri Krsna with My own eyes. I too would like to become one of the consorts of Sri Krsna. ”

Having heard this explanation from Sri Krsna, whom She still believed to be a heavenly damsel, Srimati Radharani spoke these sweet words in a joking mood, “O Dhanye! blessed one! Surely you are the most clever girl in all of heaven. The name Sumana is no longer suitable for the ladies of heaven. Their minds are no longer fixed, due to their newfound anxiety over attaining Sri Krsna, Indeed, they are now always suffering from mental anguish. But you have come here to reduce your own anxiety. That’s why you are the best and most fortunate among all the ladies of heaven. ”

In response Devangana smiled and said, “O Radha! Do not consider other ladies to be like Yourself. Do not think My attachment to parama-purusa in any way compares to Your attachment to Sri Krsna. Will that Krsna be able to see Me here? ”
